# Module & Infrastructure Map

> **Read this before building anything new.** This is the "what already exists and where"
> index for TrainingAI. Its job is to stop the recurring failure where a new feature
> re-implements infrastructure that already exists — the way a GitHub Actions cron was
> nearly added when the app already has a throttled-write-path pattern for exactly that
> (see [Recurring / scheduled / background work](#0-recurring--scheduled--background-work-read-this-first)).
>
> This enforces the **One Formula, One Place** rule from `CLAUDE.md` at the module level:
> before writing a helper, a fetch path, a scheduled job, a validation schema, a date
> calc, a cache key, or a UI primitive, **grep this file first**, then grep the codebase
> for the symbol. If it exists, import it. If it half-exists, extend it in place.
>
> **Keeping it current:** when you add a genuinely new piece of shared infrastructure
> (a new `lib/` domain, a cross-cutting helper, a new provider, a new scheduled-work
> pattern), add a one-line row here in the same PR. This is a map, not an exhaustive
> API listing — link to the file and let the reader open it. If a row here is wrong,
> the file wins; fix the row.

> **Reading a PR number in these docs.** This repository begins at one commit — the 2026-08-16
> snapshot of a private repo that had reached ~#1399. Every PR number cited below roughly **#1250 and
> under refers to that archived repository**, `nekodas-neko/TrainingAI`, not to a pull request here,
> and the numbering restarts from #1 in this one. The archived repo is read-only rather than deleted
> precisely so those references stay resolvable. See [`NOTICE`](../NOTICE) for why the history could
> not come across.

---


## 0. Recurring / scheduled / background work — READ THIS FIRST

**There is no cron layer, no job queue, and no GitHub Actions schedule in this app.**
`.github/workflows/ci.yml` is CI-only (lint/typecheck/test/build on PR + push to `main`).
This is deliberate — it is a single-user PWA on Railway, and it drives "recurring" work
through four existing patterns. **Before adding any scheduler, pick one of these:**

| Pattern | What it's for | Where it lives | Reference example |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Local-day rollover signal** | Anything that must happen once per **local day** — re-offer a prompt, clear a day-stamped marker, re-read a today-keyed value. This is what people reach for a timer or a scheduled reload for, and neither is needed | `components/shell/local-day-provider.tsx` → `LocalDayProvider` (root layout, inside `UserTimezoneProvider`) and `useLocalDay()`; subscribers key an **effect dependency** on it | Mount + `visibilitychange`, no interval: a resume is the first moment the state is looked at. `setState` with an unchanged date is a no-op, so a same-day resume costs nothing and no subscriber re-runs. **The tab shell never unmounts**, so an effect keyed on values that do not change runs once per app *launch* — which is why the morning check-in never re-prompted (BF-86). Subscribers today: `WorkoutDayRollover` (the store's `todayLogged`), the morning check-in prompt and the today-mood read in `session-select-content.tsx`. **`useDayRolloverRefresh(fn)`** (same file) is the refresh-shaped subscriber — the day-rollover counterpart to `useRefreshOnTabShow`, running `fn` on the first resume of a new day and never at mount, which matters because the date is seeded synchronously and a bare dependency would refetch every launch (BF-117). Home, Health and Nutrition take it: `tabEpoch` bumps when the user navigates back to a tab, never when the app resumes onto one they never left. **Do not implement a day rollover as a reload** — BF-80 forbids fixing a resume problem that way, and it trades instant paint for a spinner |
| **Opportunistic throttled prune from a write path** | Server-side periodic maintenance (retention, cleanup) — fire *after* a write, at most once per window | `lib/data/postgres/retention-throttle.ts` → `shouldPrune(lastPruneMs, nowMs, windowMs)` | Prunes (all ≤1×/24h, throttled + fire-and-forget with a logged `.catch`): 180-day `oura_heartrate` (after HR insert), **90-day `rr_intervals`** (migration 124, after `insertRrIntervals` — review H-1/Lever R; safe because `workout_hr_stats` snapshots the workout-HRV scalar), 30-day `error_events`, **30-day `step_live_windows`** (after `upsertStepLiveWindow` — Sub-plan A Lever 3), 7-day `oura_accel_chunks` (after accel-chunk insert), **90-day `oura_ble_battery_poll`** (migration 133, after battery-poll insert) |
| **Oura raw-storage tag whitelist (culling Lever 2)** | Drop pure-telemetry/debug BLE tags at ingest so they never hit `oura_raw_samples` (forward-only, reversible) | `lib/oura-ble/raw-storage.ts` → `RAW_STORAGE_DROP_TAGS`/`shouldDropRawTag(tag)`, applied in `POST /api/oura-ble/samples` | Drops `0x42/0x43/0x45/0x53/0x56/0x5b/0x61/0x79/0x82/0x83`; the rollup-consumed set (incl. `0x7e/0x7f` gait features) is `ROLLUP_CONSUMED_TAGS` in `lib/oura-ble/rollup-consumed-tags.ts` — the raw-storage test imports it and asserts the two sets never intersect (single source, can't drift — review G-1). 0x61 stays dropped except its battery subtypes (`isBatteryDebugEvent`) |
| **Oura `decoded`-JSONB drop / decode-from-hex (culling Lever 1)** | Stop persisting the re-derivable `decoded` column (≈halves `oura_raw_samples` row cost); decode from archival `body_hex` in-memory instead (forward-only, non-destructive) | `insertOuraRawSamples` writes `decoded = NULL`; `aggregateOuraRawSamples`'s `rowsByTags` + `getOuraRawSampleSummary`/`getOuraRawSamplesByTags` coalesce `decoded ?? decodeEventBody(tag, hexToBytes(body_hex))` (all in `lib/data/postgres/adapter.ts`) | Historical rows with a stored `decoded` still work (coalesce); `redecodeOuraRawSamples` no longer re-persists `decoded`. Nulling historical rows (Lever 1b) + `body_hex` retention (Lever 5) are separate data-dropping steps, confirm-first |
| **Walk pattern selection (TN-25)** | Pick today's guided-walk block structure — varied and assigned rather than chosen by the user, from what the week's zones still need | `packages/shared/src/walking/recommend-walk-pattern.ts` → `recommendWalkPattern(quota, opts)` + `WALK_PATTERNS` (steady brisk · long intervals · short intervals · easy steps). Deterministic, pure, mirrors `running/recommend-run-type.ts` | **Zone 2 alone drives it** — a walk is the mode this owner cannot push past Zone 2 in (0 of 44 fast blocks reached the old Zone-3 target), so counting open Zone 3+ minutes would prescribe work the mode cannot deliver; zones 3+ are `recommendRunType`'s job. **It picks a PATTERN, never an HR band** — the same separation `recommendRunType` keeps, and what stops a max-HR anchor change (TN-30) silently moving the walk. **⚠ Never let an LLM choose the pattern**, same rule as `recommendRunType`. The pacer's band is still `walk-active.tsx`'s and is TN-25's open half |
| **Dose ↔ vitals overlay (TN-46)** | Plot vial doses against the nights' resting HR and HRV | `GET /api/health/dose-vitals?days=N` (engine) → `packages/shared/src/health/dose-vitals-series.ts` (`buildDoseVitalsSeries`, `latestDoses`, `doseLabel`; Preview's `DoseVitalsCard` imports the same module, #2888) → `dose-vitals-chart.tsx` / `dose-vitals-card.tsx`, lazy-wrapped and hosted in Readiness's `extraCards` | **The lag is the rule**: a night is `inEffectWindow` only from the day AFTER a dose out to `effectLookbackDays`, because the measured effect peaks 2–4 days later and a same-day comparison finds nothing. The card states 2–4 literally rather than deriving it from the constant. **Annotates, never corrects** — no score reads any of it, and no threshold is tuned against the dosing period. Vial-dosed only by choice; an oral medication would need an explicit "track against vitals" flag rather than a widened filter |
| **Pacer adherence per walk segment (issue 2242 / LA-48)** | What fraction of a guided-walk segment the walker was shown the in-band reading, and which signal paced it | `packages/shared/src/health/pacer-adherence.ts` → `pacerAdherence(counts)`, `summarisePacerTally(tally)`, `addPacerTick`. Counted LIVE: `walk-pacer-bar.tsx` reports its displayed reading once a second through `lib/walk/pacer-sampler.ts`, into `pacerTallies` on `lib/stores/guided-walk-store.ts` (persisted, one tick per wall-clock second). `computeWalkSegmentStats({ pacerTallies })` writes `pacerSignal` / `pacerAdherence` / `pacerTicks` on `WalkSegmentStat` | **Never reconstruct it from `cadenceSeries`**: that is 10 s bin medians and bands differently from the instantaneous `liveSpm` the walker saw. `stopped` ticks are neutral and stay out of the denominator. Absent, never 0, on a segment the pacer did not judge. The segment shape is declared in five places plus `WalkSegmentStatSchema`; Zod strips unknown keys, so a new field must go in the schema too |
| **Did the day's heart-health activity count? (issue 2093 / 2746 / RV-166 / LB-179)** | Any activity counts, through its minutes at moderate effort or above (40% of heart-rate reserve, issue 2746), against the prescribed minutes; mark the day's `prescribed_runs` row done when it does | The rule: `packages/shared/src/running/heart-health.ts` → `heartHealthFloorBpm` (= `moderateIntensityBpm` / `MODERATE_INTENSITY_FRAC` in `health/hr-zones.ts`; no second floor), `heartHealthMinutes`, `heartHealthVerdict`, `heartHealthDayOutcome`, `heartHealthRescore`, `completedAsForActivity`, `activityLogWindow`. Measured server-side by `lib/health/heart-health-service.ts` (`heartHealthDays`, `rescoreHeartHealth`) for the `running-plan` payload's `heartHealth.days` and for `POST /api/admin/backfill-heart-health`. Recorded on the device by `useHeartHealthCompletion` (`lib/activity/heart-health-completion.ts`) through `lib/activity/linkPrescribedRun(userId, runId, activityLogId, date, completedAs)`. **No save path links a prescription** — the measured minutes decide. The card's wording is `packages/shared/src/running/heart-health-copy.ts` (the web `components/cardio/todays-cardio-copy.ts` re-exports it) | **`completedAs` is a required parameter, not an option** — the server writes null on any status change that does not say otherwise and `completedAsRun()` reads null as a run, so a walk that omits it re-plans the next quality session and poisons easy-run pace stats. **There is no manual activity-log writer**: `logCompletedActivity` (`lib/stores/activity-store.ts`) arms a session as already finished so the existing done screen still does the writing — a third copy of the local-store + outbox contract is the rule this avoids |
| **Personal-baseline re-derivation (BF-13)** | Replay the six-metric EMA baseline fold **cold** (`seed = null`) over the nights already in `oura_daily_summary`, to undo a history folded before the zero-seed fix — `computeDailySummaries` resumes from the previous night's persisted checkpoint, so fixing the seed never reaches stored state | `POST /api/admin/rederive-baselines` (admin-gated, 4/min, `dryRun` by default — only `?dryRun=false` commits); folds through `@trainingai/shared/health/daily-summary` → `computeDailySummaries`, no formula restated | Needs **no decoder** — it folds stored nightly values, so it works where `lib/oura-models/constants/` is absent, unlike a full-history Redecode (which also re-derives, but rewrites the nightly values too and has no dry run). **Writes only the temperature baseline + `temp_dev_c`** per the owner's 2026-08-24 decision; the other five are recomputed and reported, written back unchanged. Does **not** re-stamp `oura_daily_derived.illness_*` — that is the rollup's `illness_radar` step |
| **Disk reclaim — `VACUUM FULL` on an allowlisted table (Q-315)** | Return space to the OS after rows are deleted or an index is dropped. MVCC frees dead tuples logically; only a rewrite shrinks the file | `lib/data/postgres/slices/oura.ts` → `vacuumTableFull(table)` + `VACUUM_FULL_TABLES` (the allowlist); `GET\|POST /api/admin/vacuum` (admin-gated, 4/min). `vacuumOuraRawSamples()` and its own route are the original single-table entry point, unchanged | **The table name is interpolated into the statement — VACUUM takes no bind parameter — so the allowlist IS the safety boundary.** Checked with `hasOwnProperty`, not `in` (`'toString' in obj` is true for every object); mutation-checked test. Needed in three places: after Q-541's packing backfill, after migration 193's index drop, and on `error_events` (4 live rows in 49 MB). Takes an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock and needs free disk equal to the table's current size |
| **Oura VACUUM FULL / disk reclaim (culling Lever 1c)** | Physically shrink `oura_raw_samples` after Lever 1b — nulling `decoded` only frees space logically (MVCC dead tuples; the file never shrinks without a rewrite). Admin-triggered only, brief `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` lock | `lib/data/postgres/slices/oura.ts` → `vacuumOuraRawSamples()` (dedicated pool connection, `SET statement_timeout=0`, `VACUUM (FULL) oura_raw_samples`, `release(true)`); `POST /api/oura-ble/samples/vacuum` (admin-gated, 2/min); button in `DbFootprintCard` ① Data section | No data dropped (`body_hex` + all rows preserved); returns before/after/reclaimed bytes. Never auto-run — owner presses it, watching G-2 |
| **Repository "not found" → HTTP status** | Answer "the row you named does not exist / is not yours" with a status instead of a 500 | `packages/shared/src/errors.ts` → `NotFoundError` / `isNotFoundError`; `lib/api/route-errors.ts` → `routeErrorResponse(err)` (maps or re-throws) and `withRouteErrors(fn)` (for routes with no catch) | **Throw the typed error in the repository; map it once at the boundary.** Sixteen bare `throw new Error('… not found')` used to reach Next's default handler as 500 with an empty body, which told the sync path to retry what can never succeed. `isNotFoundError` checks a **marker property, not `instanceof`** — the Next bundle and the rollup worker's esbuild output hold separate copies of the class. There is deliberately **no not-owned status**: 404 for both, or the write surface becomes a membership oracle. The mapper **re-throws** anything else, so a genuine bug still reaches `error_events`. ⚠️ Do **not** apply this to a DELETE of an absent row — idempotent, the end state holds, and seven of them correctly answer 200 |
| **Session duration — bounded, and the bound is shared** | A workout session's wall-clock span is only usable below `MAX_PLAUSIBLE_SESSION_MIN` (240). Above it the session is the app left running, or a `startedAt` that fell back to local midnight — **cull the duration-derived number, do not clamp it** (owner, LA-21) | `packages/shared/src/health/workout-energy.ts` → `MAX_PLAUSIBLE_SESSION_MIN`, `isPlausibleSessionDuration(min)`; applied inside `estWorkoutKcal` / `estWorkoutKcalFromHr` (so `estSessionKcal` is covered on both branches), in `daily-energy`, `weekly-stats` and `app/api/health-trends` | It lived in **three** copies with two different behaviours before it was consolidated, and one of them was declared and never read. Each site keeps its own deliberate choice — `daily-energy` CLAMPS for logged activities and EXCLUDES for sessions; `weekly-stats` EXCLUDES and substitutes the exercise-log span, which is the right fallback for a midnight-`startedAt` session because its logs are real. **The exercise and set logs are never touched: the volume is genuine, only the clock is wrong** |
| **Oura raw-frame reads — ALWAYS two-tier** | Raw BLE frames live in `oura_raw_samples` (the 7-day hot window) *and* `oura_raw_packed` (sealed per-bucket blobs, Q-541). **Never query either table directly for frames** — a hot-only read silently returns a 7-day history and looks exactly like data loss | `lib/data/postgres/slices/oura-raw-frames.ts` → `readRawFrames(db, userId, {tags?, startDs?, endDs?})` (ascending) and `readRecentRawFrames(db, userId, tags, limit)` (newest-first, cold only if hot comes up short); the codec is `lib/oura-ble/frame-pack.ts` → `packFrames`/`unpackFrames`/`hexToBody`/`bodyToHex` | Both return the exact shape of the `select` they replaced (`ds`, `tag`, `bodyHex`, `decoded`). Overlap is deduped on the ingest unique key `(ds, tag, body_hex)`, because the packer writes-verifies-then-deletes and a bucket is legitimately in both tiers meanwhile. **An aggregate cannot use that dedupe** — count via an anti-join on `(epoch, tag, ds_bucket)` instead, as `getOuraRawSampleSummary` does; summing the tiers double-counts. `event_name` and `measured_at` are derived (`eventName(tag)`, `resolveDsToMs`), never read — a packed frame carries neither |
| **Oura frame packer (Q-541 Task 4) — the ONLY delete of an archival frame** | Move a sealed bucket of raw frames out of `oura_raw_samples` into one blob in `oura_raw_packed`. Bounded, idempotent, resumable; **runs automatically from the ingest path** (Task 6) and on the admin button | `lib/data/postgres/slices/oura-raw-pack.ts` → `packOuraRawBuckets(db, userId, maxBuckets)` / `countPackableBuckets` / `verifyStoredBucket` / `frameSequenceSha256`; `GET\|POST /api/oura-ble/samples/pack` (admin-gated, POST 10/min) | Three phases, deliberately NOT one transaction: seal → insert + **re-read from the DB and prove equal** → delete. A bucket is sealed only when its ds is below `max(ring_timestamp_ds) - HOT_WINDOW_DS` **and** `max(recorded_at) < now() - 1 day` — ds says when the ring *recorded* a frame, not when we received it, so a re-drain would otherwise look eligible. `body_sha256` hashes the frame *sequence*, not the blob, so it is an independent check. `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, never `DO UPDATE`. A refused bucket is returned, not thrown — and on an automatic run it is also written to `error_events`, because there is no caller to hand it to. The automatic run is throttled **per user** (`claimAutoPackSlot`, 6 h, `AUTOPACK_MAX_BUCKETS` 8) — one shared timestamp lets a busy user starve another's table — and `OURA_AUTOPACK=off` disables it without a code deploy. Phase 3 deletes **by row id**, never by the bucket's ds range: a frame arriving between the select and the delete would otherwise be removed having never been packed |
| **Device raw-store bound — marking rows rolled up from the SERVER watermark (#2579, interim)** | Let the phone's `oura_raw.db` shrink: mark raw rows `rolled_up` (here meaning **folded on the server**) once their `measured_at` is `ROLLED_UP_MARGIN_MS` (6 h) behind the server rollup's watermark, then — only if the owner's flag is on — call the native `pruneRaw` with a 14-day floor. Device-only; never touches the server archive, the ring key or the history cursor | `lib/oura-ble/raw-rolled-up-from-server.ts` → `runRawRolledUpMaintenance(deps)` / `markCutoffMs` / `pruneCutoffMs` / `markableRingTs` (pure, deps injected); `lib/oura-ble/raw-store-maintenance.ts` → `maintainOuraRawStore({force})` (the device caller, run after `afterDrainSettles` in `lib/oura-ble/sync.ts`, ≤1×/h) and the flag `isRawPruneEnabled` / `setRawPruneEnabled` (`localStorage` `ta_ring_raw_prune`, **OFF unless exactly `'1'`**, switched from the Raw store card in `/admin/oura-ble`); watermark from `GET /api/oura-ble/rollup-watermark` | With the flag off a pass **counts** what a prune would delete and logs it (`[oura-raw-maint]`, last 20 under `ta-oura-ble-raw-maint-diag`) — never calls `pruneRaw`. The bridge cannot see `synced`, so an old row whose backup failed can be marked; the native prune still requires `synced = 1`. Retired at #2302 step 2 (device-primary plan §7). Native paths unverified until the device check |
| **Ring clock epochs — a re-key is DECLARED, never inferred (Q-314)** | Decide whether a ds regression means the ring's clock restarted or that it is replaying history it already sent. Getting this wrong re-times the entire sleep history | `lib/oura-ble/clock.ts` → `classifyClockRegression(batchMaxDs, epochMaxDs, rekeyDeclared)` (the decision), `isCounterRestart` + `EPOCH_RESTART_RATIO` (the net), `isClockEpochReset` (regression only, says nothing about why); `declareOuraRekey`/`getPendingRekeyDeclaration`/`consumeRekeyDeclaration`/`cancelPendingRekeyDeclaration` in `slices/oura.ts`; `GET\|POST\|DELETE /api/oura-ble/rekey` | **A regression alone is NOT evidence of a reset** — a re-drain produces one, and reading it as a reset cost +12.17 h and +14.16 h of history re-timing. A declaration is consumed by the NEXT ingest batch (the new ds is unknowable before then) and only after its anchor is written. `EPOCH_RESTART_RATIO = 0.05` is a *net* for an undeclared re-key, validated only against the two re-drains it must not fire on — there is no observed true reset in the data |
| **Oura wall-clock reads over raw frames — derive, never read a stored stamp** | Answer "which frames in the last N days" and "when did the ring last report" without touching the `measured_at` column, which is dead | `getOuraRawSamplesForTags` (`adapter.ts`) converts the window via `resolveMsToDs` then reads `readRawFrames`; `getLatestOuraBleMeasuredAt` (`slices/oura.ts`) takes `max(ring_timestamp_ds)` across both tiers and resolves it; `hasOuraBleSamples` answers *existence* (what `connected` actually means) | Migration 193 dropped `idx_oura_raw_samples_user_measured` (136 MB) once these were the only readers. `oura_raw_samples.measured_at` and `event_name` are **dead columns** — still present, written at ingest, read by nothing; the redecode's re-stamp of them is a documented no-op, and it was the mechanism of the 2026-08-17 disk_full outage. Do not add a new reader of either column: derive from the anchors and from `tag` |
| **Oura DB-footprint readout (culling G-2)** | Per-table row/byte estimates + an exact `decoded`-vs-`body_hex` split on `oura_raw_samples`, so culling levers are sized from real numbers | `lib/data/postgres/slices/oura.ts` → `getOuraStorageStats(db)` (`pg_stat_user_tables` for cheap per-table stats + one exact scan for the raw-sample column split); `GET /api/oura-ble/db-stats` (admin-gated); `DbFootprintCard` in the ① Data section, lazy-fetched on section expand | Read-only |
| **Health Connect "Import more history" (issue 2169)** | Pull history older than the 30-day cold sync, only when the user asks: 30 days a window, each older than the last, until Health Connect has nothing or the user stops. The cold-sync cap stays a deliberate policy | `lib/health-connect-history-import.ts` → `runHistoryImport(deps)` (the paging, pure, deps injected) and `importMoreHistory({tz, shouldStop, onProgress})` (the device wiring); the row is `components/more/history-import-row.tsx` on Data & Sync; the resume point is `GET\|POST /api/health-connect/history-import` over `health_connect_history_import` (one row per account: the oldest imported local day, `LEAST` so it only moves older) | **Each window is read by `syncWindow` in `lib/health-connect-sync.ts` — the same reads and the same `/api/sync-health` ranked-merge writes (`upsertBodyMetrics`, `saveSleepSession`) as a live sync**, so a later live sync of an overlapping day merges rather than double-counts. A window is imported, THEN its day saved (a crash re-reads one window; the reverse order skips one). An empty window ends the run without moving the cursor. `historyFrom` on a sync payload widens only the heart-rate and interval age bound, to that window and no further. Paced at 1.2 s per request against the route's 60/min. **Reading past the first-grant 30 days needs the user to also grant `READ_HEALTH_DATA_HISTORY` (issue 2712):** `ensureHistoryPermission` in the same file checks it (`getGrantedPermissions`) and asks for it (`requestPermissions({read:['HealthDataHistory']})`, a name the plugin patch adds) on the import press only, before the first window read; a decline ends the run as `permission-declined`. Live sync never asks for it |
| **App-open / resume throttled sync** | "Pull fresh external data when the user opens the app," ≤ once per window | **Nothing — there is no such mechanism any more.** | Its one instance was the Oura Cloud sync on mount + native `resume` (≤1×/6h, `OURA_LAST_SYNC_KEY`), removed with the Cloud integration on 2026-08-13 (Q-224). `lib/oura/sync-throttle.ts` is deleted. If a future feature needs an app-open throttle, write it fresh rather than restoring that one — and note that `lib/oura/ble-freshness.ts` → `isBleDataFresh(...)` survives, as the "does the ring have recent data" question, no longer as a gate on a Cloud call. |
| **Client-scheduled local notifications** | User-facing reminders at a wall-clock time | `lib/notifications.ts`, `lib/meal-reminders.ts`, `lib/workout-reminders.ts`, `lib/supplement-reminders.ts` — all `reconcileX`/`computeXAction` + Capacitor `LocalNotifications` | Meal / workout / supplement / rest-complete reminders, reconciled in `sync-provider.tsx` |
| **Fire-and-forget background work — a rate limit will NOT dedupe it (Q-470)** | Kick off expensive work from a request handler without awaiting it. The hazard is a second request arriving before the first lands: `cachedFetch` revalidates on every screen open, so a route reached from the app is hit repeatedly within seconds | `packages/shared/src/ai-periodization/regenerate-in-background.ts` → `regeneratePrescriptionInBackground(userId, sessionId, {today, allow, run, onError})` — one marker per `(user, session, local day)`, taken before the work and released when it settles | A rate limit is a counter over a window and cannot tell "already running" from "ran a minute ago" — Q-535 says the same of its 4/min. **Release on rejection too**, or a failed run wedges that key until restart. **Check the guard before the limit**, so a deduped call spends no budget. Process-local on purpose: a DB marker (Q-535's partial unique index) survives replicas but needs a staleness reaper, because a process that dies mid-run holds the slot forever |
| **Which dose a supplement log freezes (BF-3, OR-104)** | Writing a supplement log on either path, or changing what a log records | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/supplement-dose-freeze.ts` → `freezableDoseText(resolvedAmount, definitionDoseText)`; called by `adapter.ts` `logSupplement` and `sqlite-backend.ts` `upsertSupplementLog` | The definition's free-text `dose` is frozen ONLY when no structured amount was resolved — the two are the same question asked twice, and `Retatrutide` froze `0.5 mg` beside `'10mg'`, 20× apart. Shared because there are two write paths and an offline log must not disagree with an online one; the local suite greps SOURCE (no SQLite in node), so this shared function is the only place the rule is actually executed in a test. Blank and whitespace-only free text count as absent |
| **Route tests — every API route has one, and the ways they go green while testing nothing** | Writing or reviewing a test for an `app/api` route | [`docs/route-test-fixtures.md`](route-test-fixtures.md) · ratchet `scripts/check-route-test-coverage.js` (baseline **0** — a new route arrives uncovered and fails CI) | All 222 routes import their handler in a test as of 2026-09-09 (PS-39). The doc is the fixture checklist the sweep produced: fifteen shapes that passed review and were caught only by mutation, the commonest being **a case meant to fail on guard X that a different guard rejects first**. The count is a floor on attention, not a measure of it — an import is not a test of behaviour, and no sufficiency check is proposed because the threshold would be indefensible |
| **Ownership-scope tests — and how to measure what they hold** | Adding or changing a user-scoped query in `lib/data/postgres/`, or a test that claims to cover one | Tests: `lib/data/postgres/__tests__/repository-ownership-scoping.test.ts` (hand-built, Q-155) and `repository-ownership-scoping-sweep.test.ts` (B seeded in every table via `every-user-table-fixture.ts`; one READERS/WRITERS row per method) · measure: `scripts/ownership-mutation-sweep/` (local DB only, not CI) · write-up [`reviews/2026-10-07-ownership-mutation-coverage.md`](reviews/2026-10-07-ownership-mutation-coverage.md) | The sweep neutralises one `user_id` predicate at a time and lists those whose removal leaves the tests green: 308 of 353 before #2425, 31 after (19 redundant by construction, 12 open). A new query gets a row in the sweep test, checked by re-running the sweep on its slice, never by reading the test |
| **Long admin operations — return a job id, do not hold the request (Q-535)** | An admin action whose work outlasts the gateway timeout. The only instance is the full redecode; the 502 it produced was read as a failure for work that had completed, which invited a retry of the heaviest pair of calls in the app | `POST /api/oura-ble/samples/redecode?async=1` → `{jobId, status, startedAt, alreadyRunning}`, `GET …?jobId=…` polls · store in `slices/oura.ts` → `startRedecodeJob` / `getRedecodeJob` / `getLatestRedecodeJob` / `finishRedecodeJob` / `reapStaleRedecodeJobs` · table `oura_redecode_jobs` (migration 196) | `status` is DERIVED from the timestamps, never stored, so nothing can disagree with them. One in-flight job per user on a partial unique index — a rate limit does not prevent overlap. **A staleness reaper is not optional**: a process that dies mid-run otherwise holds that slot forever and refuses every future run. State is in a table, not memory, so a restart cannot silently lose a job. `?async=1` is opt-in until Q-318 lands the client poller. **A request follows a running job only if that job writes everything it asked for** (`lib/oura-ble/redecode-job-kind.ts` → `redecodeJobKind` / `canFollowRunningRedecode`): a plain redecode during a stress-bucket backfill (or the reverse) is refused with 409, never queued, and the poll reports each job's `kind` (issue 2383). The step backfill (`allowStepsDecrease`) that was the original second kind was retired in #2862 |
| **The Oura rollup — ONE implementation, behind an I/O port (Q-545 / D2 Task 2)** | Turn decoded ring frames into finished health rows (sleep sessions, body metrics, HR series, wear time, daily summaries/derived). **Never write a second rollup** — a device copy is the duplicate-implementation bug One Formula, One Place exists to prevent | `lib/oura-ble/rollup/run.ts` → `runOuraRollup(io, timezone, opts)` (the whole computation, runtime-agnostic) · `lib/oura-ble/rollup/io.ts` → `RollupIO` (23 methods: every store the rollup touches, bound to one user by the implementation so the rollup never handles a `userId`) · `lib/data/postgres/rollup-io.ts` → `createPostgresRollupIO(deps)` (the server implementation) · `PostgresRepository.aggregateOuraRawSamples` is now a 10-line wrapper | Adding a `RollupIO` method adds a store the rollup depends on — narrow an existing one first. **Its neural dependencies are still server-only:** `sleepnet-assemble` and `daytime-stress` reach `onnxruntime-node` through `inference/session.ts`, so a device implementation needs the model session injected the same way the I/O is (plan Task 4 / `session-web.ts`). `sourceRank` now lives in `@trainingai/shared/health/source-rank` (driver-free) for the same reason; the SQL rank ladder in `health-source.ts` is generated from it rather than hand-written |
| **Vendored model CONSTANTS on the client — inject, never bundle (Q-221)** | Give a client-side port a vendored Oura constant table. A static JSON import lands in `_next/static`, which `middleware.ts`'s matcher excludes, so it is fetchable **with no session** — the owner's rule is that nothing Oura-derived is reachable unauthenticated | `lib/oura-models/steps-motion-decoder.ts` → `setStepsDecoderConstants` / `hasStepsDecoderConstants` (the injection point) · `GET /api/oura-ble/decoder-constants` (auth + rate limit + `private, no-store`, reads through the same accessor so server and client cannot drift) · `lib/activity/steps-decoder-constants-client.ts` → `ensureStepsDecoderConstants()` (fetch + cache + seed) | The server injects from disk, the client from the route; the getters stay **synchronous** because the table is pushed in before use. This is the pattern for every other vendored constant a device port needs — **all four** constants a device port needs now take this shape — `steps-motion-decoder`, `daytime-stress`, `stress-resilience`, `cumulative-stress` — and `lib/oura-models/constants-inject.ts` → `ensureServerOuraConstants()` is the one module left that reads the directory (boot, the rollup worker's realm, the rollup composition roots). Every port **throws** when unset rather than defaulting, so a forgotten site is a hard failure; `lib/oura-models/__tests__/constants-inject.test.ts` pins the coverage list. **Never call a constants getter at module scope** — it runs on import, and `next build` imports every route to collect page data, so the directory would have to exist at build time (Q-313: that is exactly the ENOENT that failed a deploy). Read on first use; the memoised `let c; const C_ = () => (c ??= getFoo())` shape is the one the fixed modules use. `scripts/check-constants-module-scope.js` holds it, parsing with TypeScript rather than counting braces because a brace counter flags that memoised shape as a violation. It runs from `lib/oura-models/__tests__/constants-module-scope.test.ts` rather than the Custom Rules job, which is checkout-only and has no `node_modules` to load the compiler from. **Boot injection alone is NOT sufficient and never was** (LA-20, 2026-08-23): it sets module state and `OURA_CONSTANTS_DIR` in the process that ran boot, and a request need not be served by that process — `/api/body-battery` 500'd in production for two hours on exactly that, with a probe route reading the accessor as `false` while boot had logged success. So `getRepository()` injects too, via the non-throwing `tryEnsureServerOuraConstants()` (the repository is on every DB route's path and must not inherit this failure), and `constantsDir()` falls back to the delivered `<cwd>/.oura-constants` when the env var did not come along. Do not add a second mechanism, and do not go back to a static import |
| **ONNX inference — the runtime is INJECTED, never imported (Q-545 / D2 Task 4)** | Run a vendored Oura neural model. A port that imports `onnxruntime-node` directly can only ever run server-side, which is what kept the rollup off the device | `lib/oura-models/inference/runtime.ts` → `ModelRuntime` / `ModelSession` (structural, imports neither package) · `runtime-node.ts` → `nodeModelRuntime` (the server one) · `session-web.ts` → `getWebSession` (WASM, **still has no importers**) · consumers: `sleepnet.ts`, `step-counter.ts`, `dhrv.ts` | The tensor factory rides on the `ModelSession`, not on the runtime — a feed tensor must come from the same runtime as the session consuming it. `session()` returns `null` on any failure and callers fall back to their heuristic path; it must never throw. Composition roots pass the runtime in: the rollup wrapper (`previewStepsBackfill` and the step-counter-export route were retired in #2862) |
| **Rollup step failures reaching `error_events`** | `aggregateOuraRawSamples` wraps every write in a `step()` that CATCHES — so the rollup never throws and a caller's `.catch` can never see a failed sleep/summary/illness/resilience write. **Any new rollup caller must report `stepErrors` explicitly** | `lib/oura-ble/report-step-errors.ts` → `reportRollupStepErrors(stepErrors, {userId, url})`, called from all three rollup callers: the ingest route's `startRollup` `.then`, and both the async and synchronous redecode paths | One event per pass, not per step — a lost connection takes whatever is in flight, and N rows per pass would evict genuine faults from a table that prunes at 30 days. The synchronous redecode path is the **most** blind of the three, not the least: it 502s past the gateway, so the caller never receives the JSON carrying `stepErrors` at all |
| **Off-loop CPU work — a `worker_threads` worker** | Server-side work heavy enough to stall the request loop. **The only instance is the BLE rollup** (Q-213 Stage 2) — do not add a second without reading why this one exists | `lib/oura-ble/rollup-worker.ts` → `runRollupOffLoop(...)` / `runRedecodeOffLoop(...)` (main-thread client: lazy singleton worker, restarts on exit, **falls back to in-process** if the bundle is missing) · the ingest route's coalescing is `lib/oura-ble/rollup-debounce.ts` → `createRollupDebouncer` (trailing-edge debounce + max-wait, injected clock/timer) · `lib/oura-ble/rollup-worker-entry.ts` (worker side) · `scripts/build-rollup-worker.mjs` → `.rollup-worker/rollup-worker.cjs`, built by `pnpm build` and `pnpm dev` | It needs its own esbuild bundle because the repository reaches `onnxruntime-node`, which webpack cannot bundle (`serverExternalPackages`), so there is no Next output to point a `Worker` at. The worker runs the same `lib/data/postgres/client.ts` in its own realm, so it sets `PG_POOL_MAX=2` — `poolMax()` can only ever *lower* the 10 |
| **Testing local-store SQL behaviourally (BF-185)** | Assert what a `lib/local-store/` statement actually DOES, instead of grepping its source for a substring | `node:sqlite`'s `DatabaseSync` (Node 22, in-memory), typed by `types/node-sqlite.d.ts` — a shim only because @types/node is pinned at 20; delete it when that moves. Reference: `lib/local-store/__tests__/bf185-local-retick-preserves-taken-at.test.ts` | **The sibling suites' "no local SQLite under node" reasoning has an escape hatch** — `getLocalStore` does return null, but the SQL is a plain string and `node:sqlite` will run it. The test **extracts the shipped statement** from `sqlite-backend.ts` and substitutes the real ternary's branches, so a change to that SQL changes what the test runs; a copied-in fixture would not. Build the table from the migration's own DDL (`lib/sqlite/migrations.ts`) — the partial unique index is what makes a re-tick an UPDATE, so omitting it tests nothing |
| **Cold-start schema apply** | Run DB work once per deploy/boot | `ensureSchema` (Postgres migrations) / `MIGRATIONS` (`lib/sqlite/migrations.ts`) applied on first DB open | Migrations auto-apply on cold start — no migration runner job needed |

`retention-throttle.ts`'s own header comment says it plainly: *"never a scheduled job,
since this app has no cron layer."* If you think you need a real scheduler, that's a
design decision to raise with the user first — don't add one silently.

There is no server push either, since Q-285 deleted the web-push stack (§9) — so nothing
server-side can reach a device at all. Anything time-based has to be a local notification
scheduled on the device.

---

Shared profile summaries use `packages/shared/src/profile/measured-overview.ts` and `performance-overview.ts`; plate loading uses `packages/shared/src/workout/plate-loading.ts`; plate rounding (`weightStepFor`, `mroundStep`, `mroundStepUp`, `barIsCapped`) and a set's starting weight (`prescribedBar`, `initialSetWeight`) use `packages/shared/src/workout/load-rounding.ts` (#2995; `components/workout/utils.ts` re-exports them). Spoken-set parsing (`parseVoice`, `clampVoiceLogResult`, `VOICE_LOG_EXAMPLE`) lives once in `packages/shared/src/workout/voice-log.ts`; the current app's voice button and Preview's (`mobile/src/features/workouts/voice/voice.ts`) both read through it (`components/workout/utils.ts` re-exports it). Food `source` rules (`scanOriginToSource`, `sourceAfterEdit`, `lookupHitSource`) and the per-ingredient split (`ingredientsToEntries`) live in `packages/shared/src/nutrition/food-source.ts`, re-exported by `log-food.ts`. Both clients import these helpers.

## 1. Dates & timezones — `packages/shared/src/date-utils.ts`

**The app is AEST (`Australia/Brisbane`, `DEFAULT_TZ`). Never `new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10)`.**
See the full timezone rules in `CLAUDE.md`.

| Need | Use |
|---|---|
| Today as `YYYY-MM-DD` in user tz | `todayInTz(tz?)` |
| Day-of-week (0–6) in tz | `todayDayOfWeek(tz?)` |
| Start of week | `startOfWeekInTz(tz?)` |
| Local midnight `Date` (overflow-safe) | `aestMidnight(y, m, d, tz?)`, `todayMidnightUtc(tz?)` |
| Start of a "last N days" window: local midnight N calendar days back | `midnightDaysAgoInTz(tz, daysAgo, at?)` — never `now − N×86400000` (#3117) |
| Shift a date string by N days | `shiftDateStr(dateStr, days)` |
| Workout kcal from heart rate | `estWorkoutKcalFromHr` (`packages/shared/src/health/workout-energy.ts`) — Keytel et al. 2005, kJ/min → kcal, floored at 0, `null` outside `[HR_MIN_PLAUSIBLE, HR_MAX_PLAUSIBLE]` or on an incomplete profile (Q-421). `computeActiveEnergy` prefers it per session and falls back to the MET tier, which was always Oura's `has_enough_motion === false` branch. `avg_bpm` batch-read via `getAvgBpmBySession`, which leaves out a session whose in-window HR comes only from a source #2437 gates (`DENSITY_GATED_HR_SOURCES`) and fails the density rule (`isDenseHrSpan` in `intraday-hr-contributors.ts`: ≥2 readings, no gap over `HR_DENSE_MAX_GAP_MS`; #3078), so that session takes the MET path. The ring's BLE, the strap and Oura Cloud are never gated here (Oura is the owner's call, #3114). The verdict is `workoutHrSpanVerdict` over `workoutHrSpanSql` (`lib/data/postgres/slices/oura.ts`), the one query and function for it: the live gate reads it, and `freezeWorkoutHrDensity` stores it as `workout_hr_stats.hr_dense` inside the HR prune's transaction, just before a session's series is deleted, so a stored verdict decides once the prune has reached the session (#3150). A session with neither a series nor a stored verdict keeps its snapshot. |
| Per-session workout kcal | `computeActiveEnergy` (`packages/shared/src/health/daily-energy.ts`) returns `workoutKcalBySession: { id, kcal }[]` — the exact addends of the day's `workoutKcal`, surfaced on `/api/nutrition/energy-balance` under `activeBreakdown` (Q-391). Join on `workoutSessionId` from `/api/day-log`. **Do not compute a second per-session estimate elsewhere** — the day screen's Energy total reads this same route precisely so the two cannot disagree. Unrounded; round at render. |
| Client "today" in the user's zone | `useUserTimezone()` (`components/shell/user-timezone-provider.tsx`) → `todayInTz(tz)`. A **bare** `todayInTz()` falls back to Brisbane and a bare `localDateString()` reads the *device's* zone — three answers, of which only the first follows the user's setting (Q-477). `scripts/check-client-today-timezone.js` ratchets the remaining bare calls, shrink-only; `--print` lists them. |
| Is a date-**shaped** string a real day? | `isCalendarDate(dateStr)` — the shape regexes accept `2026-13-45`, `2026-02-31`, `0000-00-00`, which then fail at the driver as `[pg 22008]`: a client input error filed as a server fault (Q-496). Both separators. Used by `MutationSchema.date` and `resolveIngestDate`. |
| Health-Connect ingest body | `packages/shared/src/validation/health-connect-ingest.ts` → `IngestBodySchema`. Moved out of the route (Q-495) so it is unit-testable. Accepts a JSON number **or** a numeric string, never `[]`/`true`/`""` — `z.coerce.number()` turned those into 0 and 1 and stored them as readings. Weight uses the shared `WEIGHT_KG_MIN`/`WEIGHT_KG_MAX`. |
| Bound an ingest date to a plausible range | `resolveIngestDate(date, todayInUserTz)` (`packages/shared/src/validation/ingest-clock.ts`) — clamps future to today and past to the 7-day boundary, so a year-9999 row cannot own `ORDER BY date DESC LIMIT 1` forever (Q-494). |
| Format a `Date`/ms to tz day/string | `toAestDay`, `toAestDateStr`, `fmtAest(ms, tz?)`, `formatTime12h(hhmm)` |
| **Label a date STRING for display** | `formatDateDisplay(raw, style)` — five styles, one option bag each, listed with their exact output in the function's own header and pinned in `__tests__/date-utils.test.ts`: `short` `15 Sept` · `long` `Tuesday 15 September` · `weekday` `Tue` · `weekday-date` `Tue, 15 Sept` · `weekday-date-long` `Tuesday 15 Sept`. `formatDayShort` is a named alias for `short`. **`long` is the form a DAY-SCOPED HEADER takes** (RV-208 ④, 2026-09-30) — Health → Day, Nutrition and the week-day sheet had three different ones; `short` is for a compact ROW label and is not the same job. That leaves `weekday-date` and `weekday-date-long` unreferenced outside tests; they are kept as vocabulary. **Do not write these strings from memory or trust a comment about them** (LB-125): `en-AU` is day-first, its `month: 'short'` is four characters for June/July/Sept so a column of labels is ragged-width, and it emits a comma after a SHORT weekday but not a long one. This helper's own comment claimed `Jan 5` / `Monday, 5 January` for months and a review quoted it as evidence of what a screen rendered. **Deliberately device-local and takes no `tz`** — its input is an already-resolved calendar day, so an explicit `timeZone` would reintroduce Q-130 on a device ahead of that zone rather than prevent it. |
| **A clock time from MINUTES since midnight** (LB-183) | `formatMinutesOfDay(minutes)`, the same "6:40 am" form as `formatTimeOfDay`, for values that are already wall-clock minutes (sleep onset, chart axes). Never turn such a value back into a Date to reuse `formatTimeOfDay`. `formatTime12h("HH:MM")` delegates to it. |
| A clock time that gets **persisted** (`activity_logs.start_time`) | `msToHHMMInTz(at, tz?)` — never `d.getHours()`, which stores the phone's clock rather than the user's wall time. Replaced four private copies of the same helper. |
| In an API route, from the session | `session.user.timezone ?? DEFAULT_TZ`, then `formatInTimeZone(...)` from `date-fns-tz` |
| Local supplement rows in the server's shape | `localSupplementsToStatus(defs, logs, userId)` — `lib/supplements/local-status.ts`. The one local→`SupplementWithStatus` mapping, shared by `useSupplements` and the sync-provider's reminder reconcile. A second copy is BF-112: the inline one dropped the dose fields, so a prompt worked on the web and never fired on the APK. |
| The CALENDAR MONTH a client screen is in | `calendarMonthInTz(tz)` / `previousCalendarMonth(m)` — `lib/calendar-month.ts`, client-only. Returns `{year, month, mm}` for the `calendar-data:` cache key and the local overlay readers. Four surfaces built this from `new Date().getMonth()`, which keys the month to the phone (RV-176); the back-step uses `Date.UTC` overflow rather than hand-adjusting the year. |

Range/window math beyond "today" also lives here — do not hand-add to calendar
components (`CLAUDE.md` "Date Arithmetic" section).

---

## 2. Client cache (SQLite-backed) — `lib/sqlite/cache.ts`, `lib/cache-groups.ts`, `packages/shared/src/cache-ttl.ts`

Client GETs of `/api/*` go through this, never bare `fetch`. See the strict
cache-invalidation rules in `CLAUDE.md`.

| Concern | Use |
|---|---|
| Fetch + cache (background revalidate) | `cachedFetch<T>(key, url, ttl)`; today-scoped: `cachedFetchToday` |
| **A chunk that failed to download** | `lib/chunk-load-error.ts` → `isChunkLoadError(error)`, used by `app/error.tsx` (LB-184). The root boundary retries **once, after 400 ms, only while online**; the offline path already recovered on the `online` event and the online one dead-ended. **The guard is module-level and must stay so** — `reset()` remounts the boundary, so a state or ref guard reloads forever. The match is narrow on purpose: a false positive silently reloads a genuinely broken screen. Three producers emit these strings (webpack/Turbopack, `next/dynamic`'s async loader, the native ESM loader), which is why they are matched in one place. |
| **Home's notification banners** | `components/home/home-banner-stack.tsx` → `HomeBannerStack` (RV-119), with `home-banner-strip.tsx` for the collapsed row and `home-banner-presence.tsx` + `home-banner-keys.ts` for the registry. **Severity split, and it is the owner's:** the illness advisory and early deload stay full-width; an activity to review, the goals check-in, the day review and the weekly recap collapse behind one strip. **The four stay MOUNTED while collapsed, hidden rather than unrendered** — two of them decide their own visibility internally and `return null`, so the strip cannot count them any other way, and keeping them mounted is also what preserves their own dismiss controls. A banner reports with `useReportBannerPresence(key, present)` above its early return; the two the stack controls are reported by the stack, and forgetting that half undercounts the strip **silently**. |
| **A component that reads a cached key** | **`useCachedValue(key, url, ttl)`** (`lib/hooks/use-cached-value.ts`) — seeds, fetches, and **refetches when the key is invalidated**. Use this instead of a hand-rolled `useEffect(() => { cachedFetch(…) }, [])`: that shape never re-runs, so a component in the persistent tab shell holds its first payload until the app is killed (Q-402, the owner's report). Built on `subscribeToInvalidation` in `lib/sqlite/cache.ts`, which `invalidateCache` fires after the delete lands |
| **A cached read the hook above cannot own** | **`useInvalidationRefetch(keys, fn)`** (`lib/hooks/use-invalidation-refetch.ts`) — subscribes to the same signal and re-runs your loader. For reads `useCachedValue` cannot replace outright because they also seed from the local SQLite store, wrap the fetch in `fetchWithRetry`, or set several pieces of state. It replaced three hand-rolled `ta:oura-ble-synced` listeners, and subscribing to the invalidation is **wider than the event**: `sleep-sessions` is cleared by `invalidateBiometrics` too, so only the BLE writer used to self-heal. Coalesces, because `invalidateCache` fires once per key (Q-359) |
| **A cached card must refetch when the app resumes or its tab is re-shown** | **`useRevalidateOnReturn()`** (`lib/hooks/use-revalidate-on-return.ts`, #2906) — a number that goes up on app resume (only while this tab is the visible one) and each time the shell re-shows the tab; pass it as `useCachedValue`'s `reloadToken`, or put it in a fetch effect's deps. 0 at mount, so the first paint costs no extra request. A revalidation behind the cached value, never a purge. Invalidation covers writes this device knows about; this covers the ring or another device writing while the app sat in the background. Used by the Health heart-rate card (and its stress overlay) and Home's heart-rate widget |
| **Energy in vs out across a day** | `packages/shared/src/health/energy-timeline.ts` (maths) + `energy-timeline-chart.tsx` (SVG). Distributes the day's ALREADY-COMPUTED expenditure across the day weighted by HR above resting — it never recomputes it, so the curve is a partition of `computeActiveEnergy`'s total and cannot disagree with the figure printed above it (Q-401's lesson). Weight is MEAN elevation per equal-width bucket, not summed: the chest strap logs ~7× the ring's samples and only during workouts, so summing would weight by which device was worn (Q-414) |
| Synchronous seed for instant paint | `readCacheSync<T>(key)`, `readTodayCacheSync<T>(key)` |
| Direct read/write/update | `getCached`, `setCached`, `updateCache` |
| Invalidate after a write | **A named group in `lib/cache-groups.ts`** — never a hand-rolled key list |
| A control that toggles a disclosure or a mode | `aria-expanded={state}` + `aria-controls` on an id the revealed region carries, OR `aria-pressed={state}` for a mode — the two are NOT interchangeable, and naming the wrong one is worse than naming neither. `scripts/check-toggle-aria.js` ratchets it (Custom Rules, baseline **empty**): it matches a `set…(v => !v)` inside an `onClick` whose state gates a render, and reports the *question* rather than picking an attribute (Q-491) |
| The moving part of a progress bar | `components/ui/progress-fill.tsx` → `ProgressFill` (RV-72) — `transform: scaleX()`, never `width`, because animating width forces layout and **reflows the bar's siblings** (a target tick in the same track, the labels beside a macro row). It renders the FILL only; the track keeps its own `role="progressbar"`, ARIA values, background and height. **Its precondition is the caller's: the fill is SQUARE and `scaleX` scales a radius with it, so a rounded track must clip** (`overflow-hidden`) or the fill shows square ends and reads oval at a low percentage — a track with no radius needs neither. `scripts/check-progress-fill-track.js` enforces it in Custom Rules over all 11 call sites, judging the element that DIRECTLY wraps the fill: `weekly-muscle-sets-card`'s outer track is `overflow-visible` on purpose so its `h-3` markers can escape an `h-2` track, and it clips one level in. `origin="right"` is a PROP rather than a `className` because both spellings are `transform-origin` utilities of equal specificity, so Tailwind's emit order would decide the winner (LB-162) |
| Naming a control for a screen reader | Name it for **the thing it acts on**, never for the screen it sits on — and make it unique across the whole app, not just its own screen. **The tab shell keeps every tab's tree mounted** (deliberately; it is what makes a tab switch paint instantly), so a name unique per screen is not unique per document: a screen reader cannot use the viewport to disambiguate, and a Playwright `getByRole(… name)` resolves to both and clicks whichever is first — which may be the off-screen one. Two energy-balance ⓘ toggles shared one name and a `.first()` clicked the invisible one for 60 s while `aria-expanded` stayed `false` (LB-189). `scripts/check-duplicate-aria-labels.js` holds it in Custom Rules, with an EXEMPT list whose bar is *"could a screen-reader user tell which one they are on"* — one dismiss or nav control per surface is fine, two controls explaining different figures are not. **Its blind spot is printed, not hidden:** interpolated labels cannot be compared, so the run reports how many it skipped |
| Unsent changes at sign-out (issue 2532) | **`lib/sign-out-pending.ts`** — `countUnsentChanges(userId)` (outbox rows by domain via `countQueuedMutationsByDomain`, logged sets of a running workout, `ta_prefs_unsynced`, the five upload queues; never throws), `describeUnsentChanges()` (the plain-words "what would be lost" list), `syncBeforeSignOut(userId)` (runs `pushMutations`, 10 s timeout, returns `offline` without a request; outcome `synced`/`partial`/`offline`/`timeout` + the recount) and `prepareSignOut(userId)` = sync + recount + `lost`. `prepareAccountDeletion` in `lib/account/delete-account.ts` is the same function. Counts and syncs only — `signOutAndClearDevice()` is untouched and still wipes everything. More → Data "Sync now" is `pullDelta` and never pushes. The dialog is **`useSignOutFlow(userId)` in `components/more/sign-out-flow.tsx`** (More > Profile Sign Out: Syncing…, then straight out if nothing is unsent, else "N changes haven't synced" with Sync now / Sign out anyway / Cancel); `DeleteAccountSheet` runs `prepareAccountDeletion` on its first confirm tap and lists the same lines in its own sheet (second tap, "Delete anyway"). `lib/__tests__/sign-out-single-flow-2532.test.ts` fails any other importer of `signOutAndClearDevice` |
| Sign out | **`signOutAndClearDevice()` in `lib/sign-out.ts`** — the only sign-out path, reached from screens only through `useSignOutFlow` (sync first, warn on unsent, issue 2532). Wipes the local store and the cache before the server action; a `<form action={signOut}>` cannot run either clear. CI-enforced by `check-sign-out-clears-device.js` (Q-172). Browser storage: **`clearAccountStorage()` in `lib/sign-out-storage.ts`** clears every `localStorage`/`sessionStorage` key except `DEVICE_STORAGE` (pairings, ring toggles, theme), resetting the persisted account stores in memory first; every key in the code must be classified there, CI-enforced by `check-sign-out-clears-storage.js` (#2453) |
| Bypass the browser HTTP cache | Already done, twice over. Client side: `cachedFetch` sends `cache: 'no-store'` and so does the service worker's `/api/` branch — a new fetch helper needs the same bypass (2026-08-09). Server side: every `app/api` response is `Cache-Control: private, no-store`, enforced by `check-api-no-store.js` (Q-166, 2026-08-10) — **do not add `max-age`/`stale-while-revalidate` to a route**; that layer is under this one and `invalidateCache()` cannot reach it |
| TTL for a key | A named constant in `packages/shared/src/cache-ttl.ts` (`TTL_SHORT/MEDIUM/LONG` + per-key) |
| "Is this cached payload still today's?" | `isBodyMetadataFresh(data, tz)` / `isWorkoutDataToday(data, tz)`. **Always pass a timezone** — `useUserTimezone()` in a component, `user?.timezone` where the user is in scope. Both compare a SERVER-stamped date to a client-computed one, so omitting `tz` silently asks "does the server's date equal Brisbane's", which is false for |Δ| hours a day for a user Δ hours from Brisbane. CI-enforced by `check-tz-aware-cache-guards.js` (Q-478, 2026-08-18). The generic `{date, data}` envelope helpers (`cachedFetchToday`, `readTodayCacheSync`, `unwrapToday`) deliberately do **not** take a `tz`: their date is client-written and client-read, so they are self-consistent |
| Warm list on app open | `CACHE_TASKS` in `components/sync-provider.tsx` |
| Offline seed floor (localStorage survives a fully-offline device) | `floorSeedTtl()`/`OFFLINE_SEED_TTL_FLOOR` (7d) in `packages/shared/src/cache-ttl.ts`, applied in `setCached`'s localStorage write — does not affect the SQLite `api_cache` layer's real TTL |

**Cache groups** (call after the matching write): `invalidateWorkoutSummaries`,
`invalidateReadinessInputs`, `invalidateProgramStructure`, `invalidateBiometrics`,
`invalidateGoalRecommendations`, `invalidateOuraSync`, `invalidateInjuryWrites`,
`invalidateActivityWrites`, `invalidateBodyMetricWrite`, `invalidateFriends`,
`invalidateSupplements`, `invalidateHealthTrends`, `invalidateNutritionWrite`,
`invalidateExerciseLogged`, `invalidateMealTypes`, `invalidateUserProfile`,
`invalidateOuraToken`, `invalidateAiPeriodization`, `invalidateExerciseLibrary`,
`invalidateActivityTypes`. `invalidateWorkoutSummaries`/`invalidateProgramStructure` both
call the shared `clearLegacyHomeSeeds()` (sessionStorage `ta_recommendation_v1`/`ta_meta_v1`)
so neither group can miss it independently.

**BLE drain settling → client cache bust:** `syncOuraRing()` (`lib/oura-ble/sync.ts`) polls the
plugin's `draining` status flag after kicking a drain/start, then calls `invalidateOuraSync()`
and dispatches a `window` event `ta:oura-ble-synced` once settled — home
(`session-select-content.tsx`) listens for this to bump its `refreshTick` and refetch the
gated readiness/body-battery/training-load/oura-hr-day effects. No-ops (never fires the event)
off-device or without the plugin.

---

## 3. Offline sync / outbox / local store

Offline-first: the on-device SQLite **local store is the source of truth**
(`CLAUDE.md` "Offline-First" section). Every offline domain has a web API route *and*
a mirrored `pushMutations` branch.

| Concern | Where |
|---|---|
| Where every table lives (server-only, device mirror, device-first, native) | **[`docs/data-residency.md`](data-residency.md)**, generated from `scripts/table-residency.json`: one entry per Postgres, JS SQLite and native table, with its writer, reader, tombstone, export and account-deletion status. A new `pgTable` or local `CREATE TABLE` with no entry fails `scripts/check-table-residency.js` (#2489) |
| Local store handle + interface | `lib/local-store/index.ts` → `getLocalStore(userId)`, `LocalStore`, `clearLocalStoreData()` |
| SQLite backend / service | `lib/local-store/sqlite-backend.ts`, `lib/sqlite/sqlite-service.ts` |
| Local SQLite migrations | `lib/sqlite/migrations.ts` (`MIGRATIONS`) — register new tables/cols in `RECONCILE_TABLES`/`RECONCILE_COLUMNS` same commit |
| Sync engine (pull/push) | `lib/local-store/sync-engine.ts` → `pullDelta`, `pushMutations`, `SyncedDomains`. A pull's `synced` is summed from the same object `applyDelta` is handed, so every written table counts (#2543) |
| Invalidate after a pull | `invalidatePulledDomains(delta.domains)` in `lib/cache-groups.ts` — maps each `SyncedDomains` flag to its group (a total record, so a new flag fails to compile until it is mapped). Acts on the flags, never on `synced`. **Every `pullDelta` caller hands its result here** (`if (res) await invalidatePulledDomains(res.domains)`), beside any screen-specific refresh: the pull advances the cursor, so its caller is the only one that ever sees those flags. `restoreFromCloud` ORs every page's flags into its return for the same reason. Pinned by a source scan in `lib/local-store/__tests__/pull-invalidation.test.ts`, which fails on a new caller that drops them. Never re-list the flags at a call site (#2543, #2550) |
| **Never `Promise.all` a wide DB fan-out** | `lib/data/postgres/in-sequence.ts` → `inSequence(queries)` (Q-308). `getSyncDelta`'s 24 reads used to be a `Promise.all`, which demands 21–24 connections from a pool of 10 for a SINGLE sync — measured: 14 of one sync's own queries queued behind an exhausted pool. The fan-out is 22.6 ms of query work total, so the parallelism spent 21 connections to save ~8 ms. Serial is 21× fewer connections and a shorter p95. **This re-frames Q-107/Q-213**: the pool is not the binding constraint, the fan-out shape is — a bigger pool measured slightly worse. |
| **Invalidate on both sides of a push** | `lib/local-store/push-then-revalidate.ts` → `pushThenRevalidate(userId, invalidate)` (LB-4). A local write must invalidate **twice**: immediately, so this device repaints (and because offline that is the only one that ever fires), and again once the server has the write. Invalidating only before is the bug — subscribers refetch a server that lacks the write and **re-cache the stale payload** for the key's full TTL. Only revalidates on a push that moved something. Use it at every local write site instead of a bare `pushMutations(...).catch(() => {})` |
| When was this meal last eaten? (BF-39 follow-up, Q-395c) | `listSavedMeals` (`lib/data/postgres/slices/nutrition.ts`) returns `SavedMeal.lastUsedAt` and orders most-recently-EATEN first, never-eaten last in their existing `createdAt` order. **Derived on read from `max(food_logs.logged_at)`, never stored** — a "last used" column needs a write on every log and an un-write on every delete, and is wrong forever the first time either is missed (docs/data-layer-rules.md, Stored Counters). One grouped query, not a correlated subquery repeated in SELECT and ORDER BY, so the rule that decides the order exists once; the sort is in JS and **depends on `Array.prototype.sort` being stable** (ES2019+), which is what makes the query's `ORDER BY created_at DESC` the secondary sort without repeating it. Scoped `eq(userId)` as well as by meal id: the FK does not stop a row owned by someone else naming this meal, and matching on the id alone would both mis-sort and leak when they ate. `idx_food_logs_saved_meal_recent` (migration 238) is the index it reads. |
| A logged meal keeps its identity (BF-39) | `food_logs.saved_meal_id` + `food_logs.meal_group_id` (migration 238; `claude_ro` views regenerated in 239; local SQLite **v31**). **Two ids, not one, and the distinction is the design:** `saved_meal_id` is WHAT was eaten, `meal_group_id` is WHICH TIME — two servings of one meal on a day share the first and must differ in the second, or the diary merges them and the second serving disappears. Stamped by `logMealItems` (`packages/shared/src/nutrition/log-meal.ts`), **one group id per call**, on both the local and the web-fallback path, and carried through the outbox payload → `pushMutations` → `getSyncDelta` → `pullDelta` → `applyDelta` → the local `getFoodLogsWithItems` read. `foodLogRefsValid` ownership-checks `savedMealId` on **both** write paths — it is a client-supplied row id like the other two. The FK is `ON DELETE SET NULL` because `deleteSavedMeal` is a HARD delete: the default would make a saved meal permanently undeletable once eaten, and the log is a record of eating it either way. **Shape chosen deliberately** — one row per ingredient plus a grouping key, NOT one row per meal, which would change what a `food_logs` row is for the diary, the energy balance, the adaptive-TDEE window, the sync delta and the local store. Rendering is Lane B and not built; nothing back-fills pre-2026-08-30 logs. |
| A SCANNED meal keeps its identity too (BF-97) | `food_logs.meal_group_name` (migration 252; `claude_ro` views regenerated in 253; local SQLite **v33**). BF-39's grouping names a group from its `saved_meal_id`, and `groupDiaryEntries` **refuses to head a group it cannot name** — so a scan, which has no saved meal, could not group even in principle. This column is the group's own name, denormalised onto every row for the same reason the id is: a group **is** the rows sharing an id, there is no group table, and the local store must draw the header offline with no join. Normalised in exactly one place — `packages/shared/src/nutrition/meal-group-name.ts` → `normalizeMealGroupName`, `MEAL_GROUP_NAME_MAX_CHARS` (120) — because **three** surfaces write it (the web route, the `pushMutations` branch, `logFoodEntries`), and it **truncates rather than rejecting**: a 4xx in the push branch is a poison pill the outbox quarantines, which would cost the whole food log over a display string. `logFoodEntries` mints a group **only past one entry** and **only alongside a name** — a group of one is collapsed back to a plain row anyway, and a nameless group is exactly what the diary rule refuses. **Rendering is Lane B and not built**: `groupDiaryEntries` still requires a `savedMealId`, so nothing draws differently yet. |
| Re-running a scoring function with a constant bracketed (TN-56) | `POST /api/admin/replay` + `lib/tuning/replay/registry.ts`. Register a function (load once, evaluate purely) whose constants take an options argument defaulting to the shipped values. Writes nothing. Every response carries a defaults run whose `matchesStored` count is the self-check. Admin auth is shared with db-query in `lib/admin/claude-token-auth.ts`. |
| A recent medication dose beside a flagged day (TN-46) | `packages/shared/src/health/dose-context.ts` (`recentDoses`, `withDoseContext`, 5-day lag window) + `repo.listDoseEvents` (the log's own `amount`/`unit`, vial-dosed only) + `GET /api/health/dose-vitals` for an overlay. Annotates the advisory; never adjusts a score. |
| A dose start, stop or change inside the maintenance window (#2184) | `packages/shared/src/health/dose-change-caveat.ts` (`doseChangesInWindow`, `doseChangeCaveat`) + `repo.listDoseHistory` (EVERY supplement's live logs, null amounts kept, and definitions stopped in range) → `computeEnergyBalance` sets `maintenance.doseCaveat` on the calibrated path only, over the days the accepted estimate used → the coach's `getEnergyBalance` (the Calorie Nudge card that also showed it was retired, issue 2622). **A caveat, never a correction**: the owner refused a pause/re-baseline, so no number reads it, and a test pins the payload identical with and without dose logs. Supplement-agnostic, keyed on the supplement id. Amount + unit decide a change; `dose_text` only when neither dose has an amount (the first Retatrutide row's text is the 10 mg vial). |
| A pull must not drop a column `applyDelta` writes (LA-137) | `lib/local-store/__tests__/la137-pull-drops-no-column.test.ts`. It gives the caller one fully-populated server row per delta table (generated from `information_schema`, with FK parents), runs the real `pullDelta` against the real `/api/sync/pull` handler into `MIGRATIONS` on `node:sqlite`, and fails on any column an executed INSERT binds to NULL, false, 0, NaN or `'undefined'`: the plain drop and the coerced one (`Boolean(undefined)`). It also asserts all 30 local tables were written. **Adding a synced table or column needs nothing here**, because the fixture reads the schema. The specific RV-172 guard stays as the cheap source-level twin. |
| A queued delete must not be undone by a server read (BF-47) | `packages/shared/src/sync/pending-deletes.ts` → `pendingDeletedIds(mutations, domain)`, `withoutPendingDeletes(rows, ids, idOf)`. **Pure**, so the rule is testable without a device; the store read that feeds it is `getQueuedMutationsForDomain(userId, domain)` (`lib/local-store/`), which deliberately has **no `next_retry_at` clause and no status filter** — unlike `getPendingMutations`, which hides a mutation waiting out a backoff, and a read path that forgets a delete during that window puts the row back on screen. One consumer: `app/nutrition/use-food-logs-loader.ts`, the **only** screen-level `applyDelta(` call site outside the sync engine and therefore the only read that can re-insert a deleted row locally. **The filter runs before `applyDelta` AND before the `applyLogs(server)` fallback** — after the hydrate it would still write the row back to the device; `app/nutrition/__tests__/food-logs-loader-pending-deletes.test.ts` pins the order at source, because the hook cannot be rendered in a node test environment. This is the screen-level twin of the `sync_status = 'synced'` gate `applyDelta` already applies to pulls. **Do not "fix" the underlying report by inverting the authority** — the server-copy fallback is itself a fix, for logged food that "vanished on reload" when a local read threw. |
| Editing or deleting a LOGGED exercise, or deleting a session (RV-175) | `lib/workout/exercise-log-edits.ts` → `editExerciseLog` / `deleteExerciseLog` (+ `ExerciseLogEditSchema`), and `lib/workout/delete-session-reconcile.ts` → `deleteWorkoutSessionAndReconcile` (+ `WorkoutSessionDeleteSchema`). The ONE implementation for both `PATCH`/`DELETE /api/workout-entry`, `DELETE /api/workout-sessions` and the outbox domains `exercise_log_edit` / `exercise_log_delete` / `workout_session_delete`. PR reconcile and recap invalidation live inside them, so neither path can skip them. The device half is LA-165/LA-166. |
| A stale phone cannot overwrite a newer workout log (issue 2721) | **`packages/shared/src/sync/edited-at.ts`** → `withEditedAt` (client stamp), `editStampFromPayload` (caps a stamp at server now + `INGEST_FUTURE_TOLERANCE_MS`; a bad stamp reads as unstamped, via `PushedEditedAtSchema`), `isSupersededEdit` (the rule), `SUPERSEDED_WARNING` / `UNSTAMPED_WARNING`. Column `exercise_logs.client_edited_at` (migration `202610170305`): when the row's content was last decided — the phone's clock for a push carrying `editedAt`, server time for a web route or the lbs-to-kg fix, NULL after an unstamped (old-client) push. `logExerciseAndSets` and `editExerciseLog` skip a push stamped older than the row, count it `processed` with a `superseded` warning, and touch the log and its sets so the pull brings the newer copy down (`skipSupersededExerciseLogPush` decides a `workout_log` before its session is written). On the device a superseded `exercise_log_edit` confirm calls `dropUnpulledSetsOfSupersededLog` (a `workout_log` replay adds no phantom sets and its local sets stay unsynced until the pull, so it does not), and `applyDelta` clears a synced set tombstone only when the server set is live and newer than it. A phone ahead of the server by less than the tolerance is not clamped. A server rename (`renameExercise`, `adminUpdateExercise`) and the 1RM re-derive stamp `client_edited_at` too (issue 2939), and a rename moves `programs.updated_at` on every program whose `session_exercises` it rewrote, so the delta re-sends that subtree (`renameSessionExercises`, issue 2952). **A delete is never superseded.** Stamped by `queueMutation` (current app), `buildWorkoutLogPayload` (stranded replays, local `updated_at`), and in the native app by `HistoryOutbox.enqueue` and `mutationFor` (the workout's `completedAt`). `updated_at` cannot carry this: it is server write time and the pull cursor |
| A hard delete must reach the device without a tombstone (RV-174) | `getSyncDelta` sends `programRoster` / `progressionStyleRoster`: every id the user has, on every page, unwindowed. `pullDelta` hands them to `LocalStore.pruneProgramStructure`, which deletes mirror rows not listed (children too) and nulls a deleted style on its exercises. An ABSENT roster prunes nothing; an empty one prunes everything. Use this, not a `deleted_at` column, for a small read-only mirror whose server rows are hard-deleted. |
| A best re-dated into the past must reach the device (issue 2763) | `personal_records` has no `updated_at`, so `getSyncDelta` sends **every** best on every page with `personalRecordsComplete: true`. `pullDelta` keeps only the ones that differ from the local copy (so an unchanged set reports no change) and hands the whole set to `LocalStore.prunePersonalRecords`, which deletes synced local bests the server no longer has. A best with `sync_status` other than `'synced'` is never overwritten or dropped. A delta without the flag is a window and prunes nothing. The native app reads `/api/personal-records` and is unaffected. |
| `food_items` outbox domain | Mints the item id client-side in `packages/shared/src/nutrition/log-food.ts` (`logFoodEntries`) instead of awaiting the create POST — queued before the `food_logs` mutation for the same entry so push order matches the FK dependency; server side is `createFoodItem`'s `id`-aware `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` branch (`lib/data/postgres/slices/nutrition.ts`) |
| Restoring intraday HR to a new device (Q-180 · BF-55) | `lib/data/postgres/slices/oura.ts` → `getOuraTimeseriesDelta(userId, { heartrate, bucket, budget })`. **Has no production caller and that is a decision**: `ouraHeartrate` appears nowhere in `SyncDelta`, and the device-local raw store is a 14-day window, so a re-install loses intraday history the server still holds. **⚠ Its keyset index is GONE (migration 249) and the restore driver must recreate it** — `oura_heartrate_user_updated (user_id, updated_at, id)` measured 21 MB at `idx_scan` 0, a quarter of the database's index budget, so BF-55 dropped it and kept the method. Without it these queries fall back to a scan: fine at test size, not fine over 87 k rows. The `CREATE INDEX` is in the function's doc comment and a test holds it there. |
| Steps → calories, and the base credit (BF-88) | `packages/shared/src/health/daily-energy.ts` → `computeActiveEnergy` (steps count from the FIRST one) and **`stepEnergyKcal(profile, steps)`**, the one place steps become calories. `STEP_BASE_CREDIT` (`health/energy-baseline.ts`, the dependency-free leaf) is 3,000 steps whose energy is subtracted from the **formula** resting base in `lib/health/energy-balance-service.ts` — so the amount removed is by construction the amount added back, and a day at 3,000 steps burns exactly what it did before. **Three traps, each of which survived a first test:** the credit is computed per profile and never a constant; it applies on the formula path ONLY (the calibrated base is `maintenance − avgActiveKcal`, where the subtraction already happens); and `formulaBaseline` is both the resting base **and** the uncalibrated maintenance estimate, so taking the credit off it moves the user's calorie target too. It was `STEP_BASELINE`, a threshold — renamed rather than edited because a test pinning 3,000 cannot notice a change of meaning. |
| Blood panel results (BF-1) | `blood_panels` + `blood_analytes` (migrations **250**, `claude_ro` views in **251**), `packages/shared/src/health/analyte-keys.ts` → **`rangeVerdict`**, `parseResult`, `parseReference`, `analyteKey`, and `POST/GET/DELETE /api/blood-panel`. **A leaf module importing nothing**, for the reason `energy-baseline` is one — a card asking *is this out of range* must not drag a node builtin into the bundle. **The schema comes from a real 63-row report** (the owner’s clinical baseline, held privately since 2026-09-24 — RV-199) and four of its shapes are load-bearing: `<0.2` is a result that is not a number (`value_num` + `value_operator`), ranges are two-sided / one-sided both ways / absent (both bounds nullable), the date is a MONTH (`date_precision`), and flags are commentary — so **out-of-range is DERIVED from the bounds, never read off the flag**, with `unknown` where a bounded result cannot decide. Re-saving a panel REPLACES its analytes, because this is the confirm target of a correct-then-save flow. ⚠ No column may identify a person and the route body is `.strict()`; the crop-before-upload step is separate and still owed. |
| A chosen rest day (BF-84) | `rest_days` table (migration **247**; `claude_ro` views regenerated in 248) + the `rest_days` outbox domain. **The write is `chooseRestDay(userId, { tz, resting })` in `lib/home/rest-day.ts` — one path for every surface**: outbox row when the local store is there, `POST /api/log-rest-day` when it is not, both ending at `repo.setRestDay`. Read by `getNextSession`, which prefers the stored row over inferring rest from a gap in `workout_sessions` — **after** its already-trained branch (logging a workout is the stronger statement) and **before** the readiness/AI branch (which would otherwise offer a deload on a day you said you were resting). Withdrawal tombstones and re-choosing resurrects the same row, because a mistap is now durable where the old `localStorage` marker expired at midnight. **No local SQLite table, deliberately** — nothing reads historical rest days on the client, and `withRestDayOverride` only ever cares about today; the marker survives as the optimistic echo between the tap and the next fetch, not as the record. Cache group: `invalidateRestDayChoice()`. |
| **Sleep rows → nights** (one row per night, naps dropped) | `lib/sleep/merge-sessions.ts` → `mergeByDate(rows)` (the server's: `/api/sleep-sessions`) and `localSleepRowsAsNights(local)` (#2414: the cold-open seed from the local store, used by `health-content.tsx`, `health/sleep/sleep-content.tsx` and Home). **Never run untimed rows through `mergeByDate`**: clustering needs `sleepStart`/`sleepEnd`, and without them the additive merge sums a night and its naps into a fabricated 11.5 h night (BF-115). `localSleepRowsAsNights` merges a date only when every row on it is timed and passes the rest through raw. Local rows carry the window since SQLite v50; a row pulled before that is NULL until the server re-sends it (a full resync or More → Data → Restore from cloud fills it — `applyDelta` lets an equal-`updated_at` re-send fill a missing window). Since SQLite v53 they also carry Oura's period type (`oura_sleep_type`, #3060), so the seed keeps a `sleep` fragment out of its `long_sleep` night as the route does; the period upsert bumps the night's `updated_at` when a type changes, and a pre-v53 row fills the same way as the window. |
| **A night the user enters by hand, and why a device night always beats it** (#2338) | Parse, bounds and derived fields: `packages/shared/src/health/manual-sleep.ts` → `parseManualNight(input, tz, now)` / `manualNightFromWindow` (wake date = the wake time's local date in the user's timezone) with the bounds in `packages/shared/src/validation/plausibility.ts` → `manualSleepImplausibleReason` (1-16 h, woken by now, within 30 days). **The client write is `saveManualNight` in `lib/sleep/save-manual-night.ts`** (local row + `manual_sleep` outbox mutation, or `POST /api/sleep-sessions/manual` without a store; invalidates `invalidateManualSleepWrite`). Server write: `repo.saveManualSleepNight` (one manual night per user + wake date, `sleep_sessions.manual_entry`). **The ranked merge is `preferDeviceNights(rows)` in `packages/shared/src/health/sleep-night.ts`**, applied inside `listSleepSessions` and the local `getSleepSessions`, so no consumer ever sees a manual night a device also recorded.
