It is NOT `SOURCE_RANK`, which ranks `manual` highest for per-field corrections. **Removing one (issue 2606) is `removeManualNight` in the same file** (local tombstone + `manual_sleep` `{ id, deleted: true }`, or `DELETE /api/sleep-sessions/manual`), ending at `repo.deleteManualSleepNight`: a soft delete (`sleep_sessions.deleted_at`) of the caller's `manual_entry` rows only. **Every statement on `sleep_sessions` must skip `deleted_at` or be allowed with a reason in `lib/__tests__/sleep-session-tombstone-readers-2606.test.ts`**; consumers get it free through `listSleepSessions` / `getSleepSessions`. Entering a removed night again revives its row (id kept). |
| The week in review, as numbers (BF-5) | `packages/shared/src/health/weekly-digest-metrics.ts` → `WeeklyDigestMetrics` (the shape `/api/weekly-digest` returns) and **`buildWeeklyDigestText`**, the one place those numbers become the recap's prose. **It replaced `buildWeeklyDigestContext` in RV-201, which fed a model; the model is gone and the text is what the user reads.** The route assembles the metrics and formats FROM them, so there is one set of numbers and one wording. | **Never parse the digest's sentences to get a number back** — the prompt only *asks* the model to quote what it was given, and that is the `JSON.parse`-of-model-output class. Every metric carries a 7-day `byDay` series because the route already computed one and averaged it away. **HRV picks overnight-vs-`body_metrics` for the WHOLE window, never per day** — a per-day fallback draws two instruments on one line. `volumeChangePct` is `null`, not `0`, with no prior week. Adding a line to the prose means editing `buildWeeklyDigestText`, and `lib/__tests__/weekly-digest-metrics-route.test.ts` freezes the whole digest byte-for-byte. **`friendCount` is deliberately not rendered** — it counts connected friends, not friends who trained, and the prompt's line said the latter. The route is a **GET and only a GET** (RV-201): `cachedFetch` caches nothing else, and that is what lets `/health/week` paint with no network. |
| Recovery Index — hours from the overnight HR minimum to waking (Q-500, Q-509) | `packages/shared/src/health/recovery-index.ts` → `computeRecoveryIndex` for one sleep window, `nightRecoveryIndexHours` for a night made of several. The second exists because `run.ts` computes the index PER WINDOW and a fragmented night must still be one night: the minimum is taken across every window, the wake time from the last. **`MEDIAN_WINDOW` and `RECOVERY_INDEX_OPTIMAL_HOURS` are both under a standing do-not-move order** — the BLE-era refit lands at 3.31 h against a shipped anchor of 5 because the input got ~2× noisier at the re-key, and a constant moved to absorb that bakes it in (Q-509). Fragmentation is decided by `groupSleepPeriods`, NOT by rows-per-date: a second `sleep_sessions` row on a date is usually a nap. Zero of the owner's 61 BLE-era nights are fragmented, so `nightRecoveryIndexHours`' multi-window branch has never run in production. |
| Vial dose maths — mg ↔ syringe units (OR-102a) | `packages/shared/src/health/vial-dose.ts` → `concentrationMgPerMl`, `unitsForMg`, `mgForUnits`, `frozenReconstitution`. **Concentration is DERIVED (`strengthMg / waterMl`) and stored nowhere** — a stored concentration is a third number that can disagree with the two it came from. Both directions live here because the log screen converts one way and history the other, and two implementations of one ratio is this repo's most repeated bug class. Zero or negative water returns `null` rather than `Infinity`, which would survive every later multiplication and render as a plausible dose. **`frozenReconstitution(log)` returns null unless all three `vial_*` columns are present** — a log with no stamp cannot be expressed in units, and falling back to the CURRENT vial is the retroactive rewrite the freeze exists to prevent. Storage: `supplement_vials` + `supplement_logs.taken_at`/`vial_*` (migrations 267/268, local SQLite v38). |
| What a vial can still do — doses left, barrel guard (OR-102b) | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/vial-plan.ts` (both apps import it; Preview had a copy until #2888) → `planDose`, `concentrationWorking`, `doseWorking`, `isMilligramDosed`. The ratio itself is NOT here — it imports `vial-dose` above. This adds only what the ratio cannot answer: whole doses left (counted from **milligrams**, since the substance is what runs out and the water is whoever mixed it's choice) and whether one draw exceeds the barrel. `concentrationWorking`/`doseWorking` render the division rather than the result, because a concentration cannot be checked from its answer. `isMilligramDosed` is the honest proxy for "is this injected" — there is no such field, and the vial that would settle it is a per-supplement fetch a list must not make once per row. |
| Reta response over time: weight rate, and resting HR / HRV by days since each dose (OR-102b, issue 2152) | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/weight-response.ts` — ONE module for both, and the one copy both apps import (Preview had its own until #2888; one `DEFAULT_BAND_PCT_PER_WEEK`) (owner, 2026-09-30: no second reta module). `weightResponse` = loss rate + 95% interval via `computeWeightRateFit`, verdict withheld unless the whole interval commits. **`recoveryResponse({ doses, nights, tz })`** = per substance and dose level: each dose is a cycle aligned on its `log_date` (day 0), with the median and 25th–75th percentile of the DEVIATION from baseline for every day-since-dose offset, plus the cycle count. Typed `insufficient` under `RECOVERY_MIN_CYCLES` (2) usable cycles per metric (a cycle needs `RECOVERY_MIN_NIGHTS_PER_CYCLE` readings); a missing night is a gap; an offset reached by one cycle has a null centre, not 0. Reuses `stats.ts` `iqrBand`/`median`/`quantile` for the baseline (nights before the first dose, else all nights and marked `all_nights`) and `dose-change-caveat.ts` `doseDiffers` for what a dose change is (cycles are never pooled across one; `mixedDoseLevels` flags it). Alignment is day-level for every dose; timed and untimed counts are returned because a dose with no `taken_at` cannot place a dose night. Takes the nights `nightVitals` returns (the ring's summary before the Oura API switch, then the per-device values readiness v8 scores; see the #3077 row), each judged against the baseline of its own device (`restingHrSource` / `hrvSource`; a night with no device named is one group), so an Oura API night is never offset against the ring's level and reads as left out while that device has too few nights. Served by `/api/supplements/[id]/heart-response` and shown by `heart-response-card.tsx`. Returns numbers and a state, no causal wording. |

Every reading carries its date, because a one-off scale figure beside today's step count reads as equally current otherwise. **`circularMeanMinutes` is the one to reuse for any average clock time**: a plain mean of 23:50 and 00:10 is 12:00, the middle of the next day; it averages the unit vectors and returns null when directions cancel. Reads local-first via `store.getBodyMetrics` — `/api/body-metadata` keeps its usual 7-day response and adds a bounded year of readings with `overview=1` for the native profile. |
| Compliant rest-gap, from the schedule (BF-122a) | `packages/shared/src/schedule-utils.ts` → **`maxCompliantRestGap(program)`** / `maxCompliantRestGapFor(schedule)`. The one answer to *"was that gap still following the plan"*, in REST DAYS. **Not `7 ÷ sessionsPerWeek`** — a count cannot see where the hole is, so Mon+Tue (5 rest days) and Mon+Thu (3) come out different on the same count. Wraps around the week end, which a pairwise scan of a sorted list misses. `getScheduledSessionsPerWeek` beside it stays the input for Home's "This Week X/Y" chip. Adopted by `computeStreak`'s two call sites, which hardcoded `1`. |
| **The training streak — ONE formula, in calendar DAYS** (RV-216) | `packages/shared/src/workout/day-streak.ts` → `computeDayStreak(trainedDates, todayStr, gap)` returning `{current, best}`, and `streakRestGapFor(schedule)` for the allowance. Two functions were called `computeStreak` and they counted **different quantities**: Home's did `count += 1 + consecutiveRest` (calendar days, rest included) and `lib/achievements.ts`'s did `streak++` per dated entry (sessions). On the owner's real history that is **111 against 83 at the same gap**, and the "best streak 49" he saw was the session count at BF-122a's rotation gap of 1. Neither was wrong arithmetically; both were labelled "streak". **Calendar days is what the app already promises** — the card says "days", the four achievements say "7-day / 30-day", the banner says two rest days are fine. `streakRestGapFor` is `Math.max(2, maxCompliantRestGapFor(schedule))`: the floor is the banner's promise, and the max keeps **BF-122a**, whose Mon+Tue user has a legitimate five-day hole that a flat 2 would break every week. `lib/achievements.ts`'s function is renamed **`computeEntryStreak`** and is still right for food, sleep and calorie-goal streaks, which count entries at gap 0. Home still holds its own identical copy — the swap is `LA-156`, Lane B. |

| The collection ladder (BF-122a) | `packages/shared/src/collection/ladder.ts` → `replayCollection`, `LADDERS`, `STEPS_MAX_REST_GAP`/`SLEEP_MAX_REST_GAP`. A pure fold over day series — **no table on purpose**: merges are automatic and the inputs immutable, so a back-dated workout replays correctly with nothing to migrate. ⚠ That holds only while the thresholds are CONSTANTS: state is replayed on every read, so changing `N` retroactively un-merges history. Version any exposed threshold with an effective-from date. Decay takes the smallest item first and breaks a bigger one into its components rather than deleting it. **Fed by `GET /api/collection` (LB-60)**, which returns the three finished `CollectionState`s rather than the `ReplayInput`s — the fold is shared and pure, so sending its inputs to the client lets two places disagree about which days paused. It reads ALL history (`repo.listTrainedDayKeys`, no window: a windowed replay is a wrong answer, which is why `/api/streak-data` could not be reused), takes `maxRestGap` from `maxCompliantRestGap(activeProgram)` for the workout ladder and the constants here for the other two, and feeds `pausedDays` from `listRestDays`. **A steps or sleep faucet day is a RECORDED day, not one above a threshold** — measured 2026-09-07, only 35 of 130 step-days reach 8,000, so a bar would decay the calm half this file says not to tighten. Deload days are the known gap in `pausedDays` (LA-76). `COLLECTION_RULES_VERSION` rides on the response so a cached collection and a live one cannot straddle a threshold change. |
| The collection v2 bank (PS-49, #2085) | `packages/shared/src/collection/ladder.ts` → `replayBankCollection`, `V2_LADDERS` (Tank, Ranger, Rogue, Health cat; six tiers), and each bank's provisional constants (`STEPS_*`, `HEALTH_*`, `CARDIO_*`). Computed BESIDE v1 in `GET /api/collection`'s `v2` block; the surface switch is #2187. Each day the drain comes off what was carried in and the day's own gain lands whole, so one session, 5,000 steps or a fully logged day is a cat on the day. **`CARDIO_ACTIVITY_TYPES` is the list of activity types that count as a cardio session** (seeded sustained-movement types; yoga, stretching, `other` and admin-added types are out until listed), read per day by `repo.listCardioSessionCounts`. The owner accepted on 2026-10-05 that v2 re-scores all history, so retuning a provisional constant needs no per-span versioning. |
| The collection's SURFACE (BF-122b) | `packages/shared/src/collection/summary.ts` — the display decisions as pure functions (`nextMerge`, `nearestMerge`, `mergeLine`/`mergeCountLine`, `FAUCET_TITLE`/`FAUCET_NOUN`, `restGapSentence`, the roster and decay lines), shared by the web and the native Preview and tested without a browser because this project's vitest is `environment: 'node'`. The rules text is `collectionRules()` in `rules.ts` beside it (the web page's paragraphs), read from the engine's constants; Preview's four-diagram "How this works" prints `collectionRuleCaptions()` from the same file, so its wording is never retyped in `mobile/`. **"Nearest" is measured in FAUCET DAYS, never in how full a rung looks**: 3 of the 4 scouts a Tank costs is 75% full and 15 workouts away, while 3 of the 5 slimes a scout costs is 2 — ranking on fraction offers the Tank and then has to say "1 more cat scout", a unit nobody earns in a day. A consequence, pinned by a test: on these ladders a higher rung can only ever **tie** the bottom one, so the tie-break (same effort, higher prize) is the whole mechanism by which a top tier is ever offered. Rendered by `components/home/collection-card.tsx` (a tenth `CardWidgetKey`, off by default) and `app/collection/` on the web, and by `mobile/src/features/collection/` in Preview. `packages/shared/src/collection/sprites.ts` is the tier → glyph map and class → art map and the ONLY file drawn art would change (BF-126, owner-gated). `components/home/collection-summary.ts`, `components/home/collection-pen-cats.ts` and `components/home/collection-sprites.ts` are one-line re-exports of the shared `summary.ts`, `pen.ts` and `sprites.ts`. |

Route `app/api/fitness-tests`; UI in `components/fitness-tests/` (guided-test flow) + `/baselines` page; equations in `packages/shared/src/health/fitness-tests.ts` (Ross 6MWT / Cooper), protocols in `packages/shared/src/fitness-tests/protocols.ts`. Cache group `invalidateFitnessTests()`. **A completed test also writes an `activity_log`** — `buildTestActivity` (`packages/shared/src/fitness-tests/test-activity.ts`) decides the type/title/duration/distance from the protocol's `activityType` field, and `test-result.tsx` writes it; the budget has no fitness-test source of its own (BF-160). |
| What the Heart Rate tile shows (TN-13) | `readiness-payload.ts` → **`restingHrLastNight`** + `restingHrLastNightDate` (last night's single reading, bounded to 7 days, picked by MAX DATE — `bodyMetrics` has no ordering the module may rely on, which is why it defines its own `asc()` helper), beside the pre-existing `restingHr` (7-day mean, still the score's input) and `restingHrBaseline` (28-day, low-wear-excluded). `latestRestingHrRow` is exported for its test. The cue is **`packages/shared/src/health/resting-hr-cue.ts` → `restingHrCue`**, moved out of `oura-score-chip-row.tsx` because a component file cannot be imported by a test in this repo's node-only vitest projects. **It renders a DELTA, never a bare bpm**: expressing the reading as a deviation from the owner's own baseline roughly doubles its correlation with felt state (+0.291 vs +0.176), and which metric you pick matters far less than raw-versus-relative. Measured over 71 production nights the nightly value changes on 61 of 70 night-pairs against the rounded mean's 29. Tile fallback order is `restingHrLastNight ?? restingHr ?? hrCurrent` — `hrCurrent` last, because it is a live BLE sample rather than a resting rate. |
| What dose was actually taken (BF-3) | `supplement_logs.amount` / `unit` / `dose_text` (migration **244**, local SQLite **v32**) and `supplements.default_amount` / `unit`. **The dose is stamped on the LOG**, because `supplements.dose` is free text on the DEFINITION and editing it rewrote history — titrate 2 mg → 4 mg and every past log retroactively read 4 mg, which for a titrating drug destroys the one thing that cannot be reconstructed. **`dose_text` is the load-bearing one**: every existing supplement has only free text, so the snapshot freezes history with no data entry and no UI change. Stamped by `logSupplement(id, user, date, dose?)` — **omit the dose and the definition's CURRENT value is used**, right for the web route and wrong for a mutation queued before a titration, which is why `enrichPayload` in `lib/local-store/sync-engine.ts` fills it from the local row at push time and `upsertSupplementLog` stamps from the local definition. `listSupplements` returns `loggedDose` **beside** `dose`/`defaultAmount`: what you took, and what you would take now, are different questions. Chain: local table → local write → outbox → `pushMutations` → `getSyncDelta` → `pullDelta` → `applyDelta`. `POST /api/supplements/[id]/log` takes an **optional** body — the shipped client sends none, and an empty body is `readJsonLimited`'s new `empty` reason rather than `invalid_json`, which is what stops it 400ing every real request. |
| The line under a supplement's name | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/supplement-subtitle.ts` → `supplementSubtitle(s)`: what today's log recorded, else the definition's amount and unit, else its free-text dose, else `null`. Both apps call it (the current app's `supplements-section.tsx`, Preview's `SupplementsSection.tsx`); Preview had an inline copy that printed a blank second line for a supplement with no dose, which made its rows taller than the web's. |
| The day's exposure to a substance (BF-69/BF-112, hoisted by LB-57) | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/supplement-day-totals.ts` → `summariseSupplementDay(logs)`, returning `{ loggedToday, loggedDose, loggedAmount }` per supplement id. **One implementation, called by both lanes**: `listSupplements` (`lib/data/postgres/adapter.ts`) for the server and `lib/hooks/use-supplements.ts` for the device, which never reaches the server's copy because the nutrition tab's local-first branch returns early once the local store has definitions. Three behaviours are load-bearing and each is pinned by its own case: `loggedToday` (and `loggedDose`) count the MANUAL contribution only — a meal's dose turning the tick on leaves a control that refuses to turn off; `loggedAmount` counts every live contribution and its `amount` stays **null**, never 0, when none of them carried a number; the unit is the FIRST one any contribution supplies. An absent `source` reads as manual — the server column is `NOT NULL DEFAULT 'manual'` so it only arises locally, and the tolerant form is what lets one function serve both. `applyManualToggle` stays in `components/nutrition/supplement-day-totals.ts`: it is the optimistic version for the page's own tick, with no server analogue, and without it the row keeps rendering the previous log's number until the next pull. |
| A meal label anyone can scan (BF-57) | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/label-payload.ts` → `encodeSharedMeal`, `decodeSharedMeal`, `decodeMealLabelScan`, `QR_BYTE_CAPACITY_M`, `qrVersionForBytes`, `MEAL_SHARE_MAX_BYTES`. **The whole meal travels in the QR** — positional JSON `[1, name, servings, [[name,g,kcal,p,c,f], …], rolled?]` — so a label scans offline, for a user with no account, as a COPY. **Meal ids are deliberately NOT globally resolvable**: that would turn a photo of a label into read access to someone's meal on an app heading for a Play Store health-data declaration, and it couples two users' rows. **Nothing is ever dropped to fit** — the tail rolls into one remainder entry carrying its combined macros, so a trimmed copy's totals match the original to the gram; truncating names buys one QR version and cannot rescue a long recipe. Budget **251 bytes = version 11 at EC M**, 61 modules, 0.49 mm/module across ~30 mm. **Only the `share` label style reaches that** — see the row below; the five print styles cannot, and carry the private id token instead. The spec capacity table is dependency-free by design and checked against the real `qrcode` encoder in `components/nutrition/__tests__/qr-capacity-table.test.ts`. `decodeMealLabelScan` is the single entry point: **both formats, one decoder, indefinitely**, because printed labels carry the old 22-char id token. |
| Printable saved-meal label (Q-389) | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/label-payload.ts` → `encodeMealLabelToken`/`decodeMealLabelToken` (the QR payload: base64url of the 16 raw UUID bytes, **22 chars — the only form that fits a v2 25×25 code at EC M**; recognition on scan is by SHAPE, never a prefix, because the payload cannot spare the bytes) and `mealLabelFigures` (**`totals / servings`** — `SavedMeal.totals` is the whole recipe while scanning logs one serving, so both are derived here or they drift). **Split in two (LB-33): `packages/shared/src/nutrition/meal-label-geometry.ts` is every decision that needs no canvas — `SPECS`, `MEAL_LABEL_STYLES`, `mealLabelCodeMetrics`, `mealLabelShareBudget`, `mealLabelCarriesRecipe`, the centred-stack budget — and `meal-label-render.ts` is the painters, which need a `CanvasRenderingContext2D`. Import geometry from the first: both vitest projects run `environment: 'node'`, so it is the only half a unit test can reach, and every existing test imports from there.** Canvas, **not** SVG→PNG: fonts in an SVG loaded as an `<img>` do not resolve; seven styles; fonts embedded via `next/font` in `app/layout.tsx`. Sheet `components/nutrition/meal-label-sheet.tsx`; scan branch in `components/nutrition/capture-actions.tsx` → `food-logger-sheet.tsx`. **Which payload a style draws is `mealLabelCarriesRecipe(style)` (BF-57)** — `share` carries the recipe, the five print styles carry this token, and `mealLabelShareBudget(style)` derives the byte budget from `codeUnits` at the `MIN_MM_PER_MODULE` (0.49) floor, so **shrinking a code silently shrinks what its label can carry**. |

It used to run unconditionally, before the count was known, because the plan's NAME came out of it; the cost was the smaller half, since the `catch` around it cannot tell the call was unnecessary and a plan needing nothing from the model returned **502 whenever the model was down** (reproduced on `pnpm dev` with no API key: pre-fix 502, post-fix 200). Up to three meal names are listed and the rest becomes "and N more", capped at 120 chars against the plan `name` column's 200. **The AI path's own `restDayAdjustment` prose is NOT reconciled with `restDayCarbLine`** — that is a separate question; this one exists so a derived plan answers the same field instead of silently returning `''`. |
| What a rest day means (LA-131) | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/rest-day-macros.ts` → `macrosForDayType(daily, dayType)`, `REST_DAY_CARB_REDUCTION` (0.15). Imported by the meal-plan **generate** and **restructure** routes, which are the two paths that write variants for the same plan. Both declared the constant AND the three lines deriving from it separately — `carbShift = round(carbs × reduction)`, `carbs − carbShift`, `calories − carbShift × 4`. They agreed, which is why this was cheap to fix and expensive to leave: tuning one copy would have made a restructure silently re-target every rest-day meal against a different definition of a rest day, with both numbers still looking plausible. Protein and fat are held and only carbohydrate moves (D3); the shift is **rounded to whole grams before** the calories come off it, so the kcal figure stays an integer. `carbShiftG` is returned as well as the targets because the generate route's prompt line (`restDayCarbLine`, the row above) describes the shift — it used to be handed the UNROUNDED product, and now gets the same number the targets used. |

Anything else → `sanitiseNutrition`, the scan hallucination repair. **Never call `sanitiseNutrition` on a food-item write directly**: it reads untouched macro fields as 0 g and saved a 50 kcal hand-entered food at 0 kcal. |

That was the bug, and `scripts/check-sanitiser-no-image-field.js` keeps `RawNutrition` from re-declaring the field. **`origin` exists because neither existing signal can identify the route**: `confidence` is `'high'` from `offProductToNutrition` for both OFF routes and set by the photo scan too, and `notes` is model-authored prose on the photo path. `'search'` maps to `'ai'` — a wrong label the `food_items.source` column cannot express without a migration. |
| Resolving a scanned barcode (LB-158) | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/barcode-lookup.ts` → `lookupBarcode(code, userId)`, the one path both scanners take (`capture-actions.tsx`, `ingredient-picker.tsx`). Asks `store.getFoodItemByBarcode(code)` FIRST and only then `/api/nutrition/barcode`, so a product already in the library resolves with no network. Returns `found` \| `notFound` \| `unavailable` \| `error` — `unavailable` and `error` are separate because the route draws that line: a food database that is down is not a product that does not exist. A `found` from the library carries `localItem`, the stored row, so the caller reuses the id its logs already point at. **`food_items.barcode` had existed server-side the whole time and held nothing** — 341 rows, zero codes, 42 of them `source: 'barcode'` — because no client ever set it; the write half is `NewFoodItem.barcode` / `NewFoodEntry.barcode` / `NutritionScanResult.barcode`, and the device mirror is local SQLite **v41**. The local upsert **COALESCEs** this column rather than taking `excluded`: a code is known only at the scan and every other write offers null, so `excluded` wins would erase it on the next log-from-Recent. |

(BF-38) | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/food-item-identity.ts` → `foodItemIdentityKey(item)`, `findDuplicateFoodItem(candidate, existing)`, `identityCalories`. **Exact, not fuzzy**: normalised name + brand (case and whitespace only) AND serving size, calories, protein, carbs, fat. Grams round to 0.1 and calories to an integer, matching what `sanitiseNutrition` and the integer column already store. Called from **both** write paths — `POST /api/nutrition/food-items` via `createFoodItem(..., { reuseExisting: true })` in `lib/data/postgres/slices/nutrition.ts`, and the device before it mints an id in `packages/shared/src/nutrition/create-food-item.ts` (candidate set from `store.findFoodItemsByCalories`, mirroring the server's prefilter). **The offline PUSH branch deliberately does not de-duplicate**: its id is already referenced by a queued `food_logs` mutation, and `food_logs.food_item_id` is ON DELETE RESTRICT. **Not the same rule as `meal-duplicate.ts` above and must not be merged with it** — a duplicate *meal* is offered to the user, who can say no, so a fit-distance is affordable there; a duplicate *food* is resolved silently at create time, and `food_logs` multiplies against the item's serving size, so a looser match would change what a new log means. |

(BF-11d) | `components/nutrition/meal-duplicate.ts` → `findDuplicateMeal(candidate, existing, excludeId?)`, `normaliseMealName`, `DUPLICATE_MAX_FIT_DISTANCE` (0.15). **Two independent tests and BOTH must pass**: `fitDistance` on the macros — reused from `meal-macro-fit.ts` rather than a new threshold — and **equality of the normalised name, not a fuzzy match**. Macros alone match every protein shake against every other one; the name alone matches a slice against the loaf. Exactness follows BF-38's *prefer under-merging*: a duplicate is deletable, an offer to overwrite the wrong meal is not. Both save paths call it — the builder's Save (prompt: Update / Save as new, with Save-as-new the safe default) and the multi-dish import (duplicates start unticked rather than raising N dialogs). **Update keeps the existing id** — `meal_plan_meals.saved_meal_id` and the printed QR label both reference it. |

The layer is `-z-10`: `SheetContent` is `fixed z-50` and so a stacking context, and without a negative z-index an `absolute` child paints OVER the sheet's content and hides every row. **Opt-in, never default** — `SheetContent` is the app-wide primitive, so a default here is the "no global element-selector styling" hazard. The hook mirrors `DynamicBackground`'s own activation test (global switch, this section's switch, a static palette for the route) and returns null otherwise, including for the store's shipped `enabled: false`. |
| Which wallpaper a route gets | `lib/background/pathname-routing.ts` → `pathnameToSection` (the 5-key toggle bucket) and `pathnameToPaletteKey` (the finer per-screen scene). **One copy, shared by the wallpaper and by any `surface="page"` sheet** — two would disagree the first time a route moved, and the failure is a sheet in one colour over a page in another. Extracted from `dynamic-background.tsx` by BF-75, where they were private and untestable. The bucket is deliberately coarser than the palette: `stats` gates off the *home* switch and `workout-select` off the *workout* switch. Returns null for the four health detail pages (own DetailHero art) and for the sky-scene routes, which a sheet cannot reproduce. |
| Reading a recipe into a meal (BF-52) | `components/nutrition/recipe-import-run.ts` → `runRecipeImport(payload, fallbackName, userId)`, returning `imported` / `candidates` / `empty` / `error`. **The one import for all three inputs** — `/api/nutrition/scan` branches on `image`+`mimeType` / `url` / `text`, so the payload passes through whole. Called by `ingredient-picker.tsx` and by the builder's `meal-source-row.tsx`; each owns its own spinner, message and field resets. **`recipeYield` is NEVER defaulted to 1** (the banana-bread four-fold error), a multi-candidate page asks before minting anything, and quantities are floored at the schema's 0.01 — the three behaviours its old in-component form defended with prose alone. `offlineHint()` lives here too. |
| Where a meal comes from | `components/nutrition/meal-source-row.tsx` → `MealSourceRow`, above the collapsed ingredient picker in the builder. `Recipe photo` reuses `RecipeImageButton variant="tile"` (its Capacitor `CameraSource.Prompt` branch and its **named** file input are the part worth reusing). **The barcode is deliberately absent** — these three produce a whole ingredient list, the barcode and the AI estimate produce one ingredient, so it stays beside the search. The URL branch inside `ingredient-search.tsx` also stays: without it a pasted link falls to the estimate, which reads a URL as a food called "https". |
| Writing a saved meal | `components/nutrition/save-meal.ts` → `saveMealToLibrary(input)`. Offline-first: local upsert + outbox mutation + `invalidateSavedMeals` + `pushThenRevalidate`, with a **web fallback that is a pure pass-through**. Returns the refreshed local list, or `null` when the caller must refetch. Carries Q-216's inner catch — a local write that throws falls through to the server, never into an error toast — and `local-store-write-fallback.test.ts` scans this file for that shape. Carries `mealTypeIds` (BF-11f) on **both** write paths from one destructure — `[]` clears the tags, `undefined` leaves them alone, and the builder passes `undefined` only when overwriting a duplicate it never showed the user. **Not the same function as `savePlanMealToLibrary`**, which takes raw ingredients and always creates; this one takes food ids and can overwrite. |
| Saving a SCANNED shared meal (BF-57) | `components/nutrition/save-shared-meal.ts` → `saveSharedMealToLibrary(shared, userId, tz)`. Turns a `decodeMealLabelScan` `shared-meal` payload into the scanner's OWN meal — a **copy**, never a reference, which is what lets it work offline and for an account that has never met the author's. Mints each ingredient through `createFoodItem` **normalised to per 100 g** (same basis as `ingredientToEntry`, so the scanner's library dedupes them instead of minting a row per scan) with the weight carried in the quantity multiplier, then hands the items to `saveMealToLibrary`. **Saves, never logs** — a scan must not put a meal in today's diary. `sharedIngredientToEntry` is the exported arithmetic; it guards a zero `weightG` (the encoder rounds, so a sub-gram garnish arrives as 0) and floors the multiplier at the schema's 0.01. |
| Lowering the meal count with meals pinned (BF-11h) | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/meal-count-reduction.ts` → `reductionNeeded(pins, mealCount)`, `applyReduction(...)`; prompt in `meal-count-reduction-prompt.tsx`. **Fires at `K > M - 1`, matching `my-meals-picker.tsx`'s own `maxKeepable = mealCount - 1`** ("one slot stays open for the plan"), which is one STRICTER than the server's `kept.slice(mealCount)`. Both thresholds are correct for what they guard; the prompt uses the client's. Exists because `setMealCount` never re-truncated the pins, so lowering the count after pinning dropped them silently — the server reported `droppedPins` and nothing read it. A dropped typed meal is unticked, never deleted. |
| Swapping one plan slot for a saved meal (BF-11h) | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/library-swap.ts` → `libraryMealForSlot(slot, library, mealTypes, usedIds)`, `usedSavedMealIds(meals)`. Runs `selectLibraryMeals` — **the generator's own matcher**, not a second one — on cached client data, so a reroll's library option costs no route and no model call. Excludes the meal already in the slot and any used elsewhere in the day. |
| Where a plan meal came from | `components/nutrition/meal-source-badge.tsx` (`MealSourceBadge`) — reads `DraftMeal.source`, never `savedMealId`: **`kept` and `library` both carry a savedMealId**, so the old check called a meal the planner CHOSE one the user had PINNED. `matchReason` is non-null on an AI slot only when the library was actually searched, which is what lets it say "none fitted" without implying a search that never ran. |
| Which question a scan image is asking (BF-40) | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/scan-prompt.ts` → `scanImageKind(raw)`, `scanImagePrompt(kind, note)`. `/api/nutrition/scan` takes an image for two acts — photograph your dinner, screenshot a recipe — and the **per-request line is the only thing that distinguishes them**; the system prompt already understands recipe pages. **Absent means `'plate'`**, and the plate strings are reproduced verbatim so a pre-BF-40 caller's request is byte-identical rather than merely equivalent. Extracted and tested because the failure is silent both ways: dinner read as a recipe, or a recipe read as one plated portion. |
| A meal's name and photo without its ingredients (BF-39) | `lib/hooks/use-saved-meal-summaries.ts` → `useSavedMealSummaries(userId)`, a `Map` of saved-meal id → `{ name, imageDataUri }`, for the diary's grouped rows. **Local-first**, because `saved_meals` is a local-first domain: an API-only read would show a meal renamed offline under its old name until the next sync. **Its key is `saved-meals`, shared with the library sheet and the plan picker** — a second key for the same endpoint is how this app has produced stale and blank first paints before, and the shared key is what makes `invalidateSavedMeals()` reach this read. |
| Which meal types a saved meal suits (BF-11e, issue 2153) | **The declared set is `saved_meal_meal_types`** (join table, both FKs `ON DELETE CASCADE`, composite PK), exposed as `SavedMeal.mealTypeIds` on `/api/nutrition/saved-meals` and mirrored in on-device SQLite; `undefined` on a write leaves it alone, `[]` clears it, and soft-deleted types are filtered on read. **History seeds it once and is never the set:** `lib/data/postgres/migrations/202610080512_seed_saved_meal_meal_types.sql` ticks a type at >= 3 logging events AND >= 60% of the meal's events (thresholds in its `params` CTE, nowhere else), only for meals with `saved_meals.meal_types_seeded = false` (server-only; every existing row flips to true in that migration and the column then defaults to true), so a user's choice, including a deliberately empty set, is never overwritten. The seed test runs the migration file itself. |
| Searching the food database (BF-48) | `lib/hooks/use-food-database-search.ts` → `useFoodDatabaseSearch(query, active)`, returning `{ results, searching, unavailable }`, and `components/nutrition/food-database-results.tsx` → `FoodDatabaseResults` for the section it draws. **Two call sites: the meal builder's ingredient picker and Log Food → Single foods.** The second is why it was extracted — the tab searched only foods already logged, so a food never eaten was unreachable from the screen for adding one (owner, device pass N7). **The 700 ms debounce is load-bearing, not a spare knob**: Open Food Facts rate-limits to roughly ten searches a minute, so the 250 ms clock the own-foods search uses gets 503ed. Nothing here is cached — results are per-keystroke and a third-party index that goes down is the last thing to serve from memory as if it were the user's own data. The macro/calorie mismatch warning lives in `FoodDatabaseResults`, so a row cannot start looking verified on one screen and not the other (Q-406). |
| Preferences that follow the account (Q-392) | `lib/user/preferences-sync.ts` → `hydrateUserPreferences(bag)` seeds every device key from the server; `savePreference(name, value)` / `savePreferences(patch)` write the device copy **and** PATCH `/api/user/preferences`. Driven by `PREFERENCE_STORAGE` (`packages/shared/src/user/preferences.ts`), which owns each key's **encoding** — `ta_ss_widgets` is JSON, `ta_weight_lookback` a bare number, the reminder toggles `String(boolean)` compared against the literal `'false'`; a value seeded in the wrong shape reads as the default. Warmed in `sync-provider.tsx`'s `CACHE_TASKS` beside `hydrateGoalSeeds`, the same shape of problem. **The rule is one-directional — the server wins**, `localStorage` is a seed written *from* it and never the reverse. **⚠ But hydration NEVER deletes a key the bag lacks, and do not "restore" that.** `savePreference` PATCHes in the background, so between a tap and its acknowledgement the bag legitimately lacks a key the user just chose — clearing it wiped a meal-label style mid-flight (caught by `meal-label.spec.ts`) and reverts every offline change on the next launch. The server cannot distinguish "cleared" from "never set" (`mergePreferences` deletes the key), so this is the client's call to make. The cost — a key cleared on another device lingering here — applies to exactly one thing, the mutually-exclusive brand preset / custom hue pair, which `EXCLUSIVE_GROUPS` resolves. `null` clears a key; use `savePreferences` for a mutually-exclusive pair (brand preset vs custom hue) so two PATCHes cannot land out of order. **Not an outbox domain on purpose** — losing one costs a toggle, not data, and every caller is a tap that must feel instant. **⚠ A mirror effect uses `usePersistedPreference(name, value)`, NEVER `savePreference` — `useEffect(() => localStorage.setItem(K, v), [v])` was a free write and the same line calling `savePreference` is a PATCH on every mount.** One such site put a PATCH into Health's launch burst and stranded it, and a GET behind it, past sixty seconds, failing nine e2e specs on `waitUntil: 'networkidle'` (2026-08-30). The hook compares the **value**, not a first-run ref: StrictMode invokes an effect twice on mount, so a run-count guard is spent by the second invocation and PATCHes anyway — measured, that version failed identically to no guard. `writePreferenceLocally` is the device-only write it uses on mount. |
| Handing the meal builder a recipe picture | `components/nutrition/recipe-image-button.tsx` → `RecipeImageButton`, shown only on an EMPTY ingredient search (a typed query means the estimate row, a pasted link the import row). **No `capture` attribute and `CameraSource.Prompt`** — `capture="environment"` forces the camera and a screenshot lives in the gallery, which would make the originating use case unreachable. Posts through `ingredient-picker.tsx`'s `importRecipeFrom(...)`, shared with the URL path so the multi-candidate branch, the serial minting, the 0.01 floor and the `recipeYield` refusal exist once. |
| Which meal slots a saved meal suits | `components/nutrition/meal-type-tags.tsx` (`MealTypeTags`) — the builder's tag chips, over the shared `ChipGroup`. **No tags means EVERY slot, not none** (`packages/shared/src/nutrition/library-match.ts` → `eligibleForSlot`), so the hint under the chips has to say so — nothing ticked otherwise reads as "excluded from everything", and the inverse reading would shrink a whole library to zero. |
| Meal-builder ingredient quantities | `components/nutrition/use-ingredient-quantities.ts` → `useIngredientQuantities()`. Owns the builder's ingredient list and its per-row serving/gram unit; the arithmetic stays in `saved-meal-qty.ts` (`qtyFromInput`, `steppedQty`), which is the half worth testing. Extracted to keep `saved-meals-sheet.tsx` under the 800-line ceiling. |
| Saved-meal thumbnail (Q-396) | Column `saved_meals.image_data_uri` (Postgres **204**, local SQLite **v28**), a base64 data URI. Cap and validation live once in **`packages/shared/src/nutrition/meal-image.ts`** → `SAVED_MEAL_IMAGE_MAX_BYTES` (**16 KB decoded**), `rejectMealImage`, `mealImageRejectionMessage`; enforced by the shared `SavedMealSchema` (both routes) **and again in `pushMutations`**, because the offline replay goes through neither. **`users.avatar`'s 5 MB is NOT the precedent** — an avatar never enters the sync delta and this does. `undefined` leaves a stored image alone, `null` removes it, on the server and in the local upsert alike (`COALESCE`), so a name edit cannot delete the photo. The `claude_ro` view withholds the blob and exposes `image_bytes`, which is the tripwire for the cap slipping. |
| Downscaling an image before upload | `lib/media/downscale-image.ts` → `downscaleToDataUrl(blob, {maxDim, quality, mimeType})`, with `downscaleToJpegDataUrl` delegating to it. **The one implementation** — the avatar crop, the feedback screenshot, the nutrition scan and the meal-photo tile all use it. Fits within BOTH dimensions and never upscales. `mimeType: 'image/webp'` is REQUESTED, not guaranteed: `toDataURL` answers an unsupported type with a **PNG** and no error, several times the bytes, so the helper checks the prefix of what came back and re-encodes as JPEG. `fitWithin` is split out because both vitest projects run in `node`, where `Image`/`canvas` do not exist. **`downscaleToThumbDataUrl(blob, maxEncodedChars?)` is the STORED-thumbnail spec** — 128 px WebP at q0.8, the one shape every `image_data_uri` is written in (OR-108 extracted it from `meal-photo-tile.tsx` when the food-capture paths needed a third copy; three arguments, all silent when wrong). Its optional budget is in **characters of base64, what a request body counts**, and walks 0.8 → 0.6 → 0.45 to fit — needed because `POST /api/nutrition/food-items` caps its body below the image it permits (LB-101). **`dataUrlToBlob(dataUrl)` is here too, and `await fetch(dataUrl)` is banned** — a `fetch()` of a `data:` URL is a **`connect-src`** request, and this app's CSP does not open `connect-src` to `data:`, so it rejects with a bare `TypeError`. That ran on `MealPhotoTile`'s native branch only, inside a `catch {}` for picker cancellations, and cost three owner reports of a meal photo that silently would not save (BF-46 ①b). Prefer `CameraResultType.Base64` and skip the data URL entirely; `lib/media/__tests__/no-data-url-fetch.test.ts` fails on the next one |
| Picking a meal photo (Q-327) | `components/nutrition/meal-photo-tile.tsx` → `MealPhotoTile`, beside the meal-name field in Edit Meal. 128 px WebP (~6 KB) against `SAVED_MEAL_IMAGE_MAX_BYTES`; the size shown on the tile is `mealImageBytes` from `meal-image.ts`, the **same** arithmetic `rejectMealImage` rejects on, because nothing else fails loudly when the cap slips. `imageDataUri` is always sent explicitly — `undefined` means "leave a stored photo alone" and `null` removes one |
| One serving of a recipe | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/scan-totals.ts` → `perServing(ingredients, servings)`. Divides the **weights** and leaves the per-100g densities alone — the densities describe the food, not the portion, so scaling them too divides twice. Two callers and they must agree: `/api/nutrition/scan` divides when a recipe page states its `recipeYield`, and `my-meals-picker.tsx` divides when it does not and the user answers how many it serves (Q-409). Importing a whole tray as one meal is a 4x calorie error that reads as plausible, which is why this is shared rather than written twice |
| Is TODAY a deload? | Two different questions, and conflating them was BF-8. `PhaseStatus.isDeloadActive` = "the current PHASE is a deload week"; `prescription.deload` = "today's session runs light", which is what a readiness-driven auto-applied deload sets. **Surfaces must ask the second.** `sessionContextLabel(phaseStatus, sessionIsDeload)` (`components/workout/utils.ts`) resolves the active header's line; the pre-workout toggle adopts the prescription via `useDeloadChoice(seedFromUrl, periodizationState)`, which stops adopting once the user chooses. A `consumed` prescription is ignored — it describes a session that already ran |
| Revalidating after a local write | `lib/local-store/push-then-revalidate.ts` → `pushThenRevalidate(userId, invalidator)`. **Every fire-and-forget `pushMutations` beside an invalidation goes through it.** Invalidating only *before* the push makes subscribers refetch and **re-cache** the pre-write payload, which then stands for the key's full TTL (LB-4, LB-6); invalidating only *after* repaints nothing offline, because `pushMutations` never resolves usefully with no network. So the caller invalidates immediately AND hands the same invalidator here. `scripts/check-invalidate-after-push.js` holds the class shut — an **awaited** push and a bare one with no invalidation nearby are not hits |
| When a food log was **eaten** (Q-413) | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/eaten-at.ts` → `resolveEatenAt({date, window, at, tz})`, plus `hourInWindow`/`windowMidpointHour`. `logged_at` used to be `defaultNow()` — when the row was CREATED, not when the food was eaten — so a back-filled day stamped the hour you reached for your phone. Inside the meal window on the log's own date → keep the real instant; otherwise → the window's midpoint on that date, in the USER's timezone (`fromZonedTime`, never `setHours`). A wrapping window (22→02) spans `end + 24 - start` and projects back onto the log's own date; `timeEndHour = 24` is end-of-day, not a wrap. **One call site per layer:** the server resolves in `createFoodLog` (covering the web route AND the `pushMutations` branch), the device in `local-eaten-at.ts` → `resolveLocalEatenAt`. A client-supplied `loggedAt` is a candidate, not an answer. |
| Reaching a native plugin at all (the `{ plugin }` wrapper) | Every getter that resolves a Capacitor plugin returns **`{ plugin }`, never the plugin** — `lib/oura-ble/plugin.ts` (which documents why in full), `lib/scale-ble/plugin.ts`, `lib/polar-ble/plugin.ts`, `lib/media/save-to-gallery.ts`, `components/workout/voice-log-button.tsx`. `registerPlugin()` hands back a Proxy whose `get` trap answers **every** key with a callable, `then` included, so resolving an async function's promise with it makes the promise-resolution algorithm invoke `plugin.then(...)` across the bridge: the promise then **never settles** and the bridge error escapes as an unhandled rejection. `scripts/check-plugin-proxy-thenable.js` (Custom Rules) enforces the shape — the prose alone did not, and voice logging was dead on the APK until production `error_events` reported it (LA-37, 2026-08-30). Pure scanner in `scripts/lib/plugin-proxy-scan.js`; invariant pinned executably in `lib/__tests__/capacitor-plugin-thenable.test.ts`. **The hazard is the proxy, not the plugin:** `@capacitor-community/bluetooth-le` exports `BleClient = new BleClientClass()`, a plain instance, so `lib/colmi-ble/ble.ts` and `lib/live-hr/chest-strap-source.ts` return it bare and are correct — the check exempts it by name with that reason. |
| Getting the label off the device (Q-400) | `lib/media/save-to-gallery.ts` → `saveImageToGallery(blob, filename)`, over the `MediaSave` Capacitor plugin (`android/…/media/MediaSavePlugin.kt`). **Writing a file is not the hard part — being visible is:** a file in app storage, or even in `Pictures/`, does not appear in the Photos app until it is registered with MediaStore, so this is a bridge rather than a `@capacitor/filesystem` call. Never throws; returns the failure reason as a value, because every caller ends in a toast. The `<a download>` branch is the `pnpm dev` fallback **only**, and the native branches never fall through to it — inside the Samsung WebView it is a silent no-op, so a fall-through would toast success and do nothing, which is worse than the dead button this replaced. Requires API 29+; below that it reports unavailable rather than pretending. |
| Declared print size of a generated PNG (Q-400) | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/png-density.ts` → `withPngDensity(bytes, dpi)` / `readPngDensity(bytes)`. `canvas.toBlob` writes **no `pHYs` chunk** — the canvas API cannot set one — and a PNG with no declared physical size prints at the viewer's default, 96 dpi almost everywhere: a 1,179 px 50 mm label arrives at **312 mm**. Splices a 21-byte chunk after `IHDR`; idempotent (a PNG that already declares a density is returned untouched) and a no-op on non-PNG input. Invisible to every other check the repo runs — the defect only appears on paper. |
| Pagination cursor contract | `packages/shared/src/sync/cursor.ts` → `resolveSyncCursor`, `DomainPage` |
| Outbox mutation schema | `packages/shared/src/sync/mutation-schema.ts` → `MutationSchema`, `PushMutation` |
| **Sync parity vectors** (#2490) | JSON in `packages/shared/src/sync/vectors/` (one file each for incidents, outbox domains and pull flags), validated by `packages/shared/src/sync/vectors/schema.ts` → `SyncVectorFileSchema`. Each vector is device tables before, steps (`local` write, `pull` pages, `push` answers), tables / outbox / wire after, in SQLite names, so a second client could run the same file. **The one runner** is `lib/local-store/__tests__/parity/runner.ts` (`runVector`), driven by `lib/local-store/__tests__/parity-vectors.test.ts`: real `MIGRATIONS` on `node:sqlite` (`lib/local-store/__tests__/parity/node-sqlite-service.ts`), real `SQLiteLocalStore`, real `pullDelta` / `pushMutations`; only the connection, the network and `getLocalStore`'s choice of store are stood in for. The test fails if an outbox domain in `SYNCED_MUTATION_DOMAINS` or a pull flag has no vector. **A new outbox domain or pull flag needs a vector here.** A vector that exposes a defect is committed with `knownFailing` naming its issue and runs as `it.fails`. Keep new sync tests as vectors rather than another hand-built `CREATE TABLE` harness. |
| **The comparative self-report** | `day_checkins.vs_normal` (was `vs_yesterday`, renamed by LB-190: migration **202609301320**, local SQLite **v46**; first added by migration 280 / v40), with **`vs_question`**: WHICH question the row answered, `VS_QUESTION.YESTERDAY` (1) or `.NORMAL` (2) in `packages/shared/src/types/day-checkin.ts`. `CURRENT_VS_QUESTION` is what the sheet asks today and is **`.NORMAL` since OR-206** (2026-09-30); it moves ONLY together with the on-screen copy, and a guard in `components/checkin/__tests__/tn58-vs-normal-control.test.ts` asserts the pair, because a prompt and a marker that disagree mislabel every row written between them and nothing downstream can tell. The two questions are different measurements and must never be pooled, and a CHECK keeps an answer and its question together. **`vs_normal_touched`** (LB-198, migration **202610050636**, local **v47**) is the SECOND boundary: LB-191 seeds the neutral, so it records per row whether the owner TAPPED the picker. **NULL = unknown** (every row from before it; never read as false), set only from the picker's `onChange` and never inferred from Save, and refused without an answer. `resolveVsAnswer` (`validation/day-checkin.ts`) stamps both write paths and reads the pre-rename `vsYesterday` key, still in queued outbox mutations, as question 1 — `'better' \| 'same' \| 'worse'`, **nullable with NO default**. TN-58's replacement for the absolute 1-5 beside it, which produced two distinct values in 81 days. **NULL means not answered**, and since **LB-191** (2026-09-30) the control **seeds the neutral** *"About the same"* — the owner's explicit call. So the only thing now separating "not answered" from a reflexive "same" is **DISMISSING the sheet**, which writes nothing; anything that later writes a row on close erases that distinction silently. ⚠ This row previously said there was no neutral and therefore no need for a `*_touched` twin — **the seed is exactly the midpoint-slider case that argument excluded**, and `LB-198` carries the proposal for the twin. Text rather than `-1/0/+1` because every other scale on this table stores 1 = best … 5 = worst, and a column where +1 meant better would put two polarities in one row; a correlation gets its number from one shared mapping at read time. Validated in `DayCheckinExtrasSchema` and **counted by `dayCheckinHasAnswers`** — the comparative control REPLACES the scale rather than joining it, so a check-in whose only answer is this one must not read as empty. |
| Day-logging completeness (Q-387) | `day_checkins.food_logging_completed_at` (migration 201, local SQLite v27) + `POST /api/food-logging-complete`. The ONLY thing that makes a day usable by the maintenance calibration — `estimateMaintenance` filters on it, and **absent means excluded, never assumed-complete**. Before it, `intakeKcal > 0` counted a day abandoned after lunch at its partial total and dragged the estimate 86 kcal lower per partial day with `confidence: 'medium'`. `saveDayCheckin` leaves the column alone when the caller omits it (and the local upsert COALESCEs) — a bare overwrite would clear it every time the evening check-in was saved. |
| Push-failure classification (Q-475) | `packages/shared/src/sync/retryable-error.ts` → `isRetryableWriteError`. Answers "was this the *server* failing to write, or the mutation being wrong?" — the two are indistinguishable at the wire because `pushMutations` catches per mutation and always returns HTTP 200. Walks the `cause` chain (Drizzle wraps the driver error); pg SQLSTATE classes 08/53 + named codes, plus `syscall === 'connect'` for socket/TCP unreachability. Used in `pushMutations`' catch to stamp `retryable` on a `PushResult` error; the client (`sync-engine.ts`) then leaves the row queued and backs the whole queue off instead of counting it towards `MAX_MUTATION_ATTEMPTS`. |
| At-least-once replay ledger (Q-481) | `applied_mutations` table (migration 199) + `incrementWaterLogOnce` in `lib/data/postgres/adapter.ts`. The outbox delivers at-least-once; **one** push branch of nineteen is not idempotent under replay (`body_metrics` + `waterMlDelta`, which *adds* — correctly, or SYNC-P7 returns). That branch claims the mutation id with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING … RETURNING` inside the same transaction as the increment, so a replay is refused while distinct concurrent adds still sum. **Do not extend this to the other eighteen** — they upsert on `(user_id, date)` or a client row id and are naturally idempotent; a universal ledger would cost far more than it buys. Pruned opportunistically at 90 days off the write path. |
| Sync helpers | `lib/local-store/sync-helpers.ts`; `packages/shared/src/sync/push-retry.ts` is the one home of `MAX_MUTATION_ATTEMPTS`, `nextRetryDelayMs` and `resolveFailedOutboxIds`, used by the current app and by Preview |
| Server sync routes | `app/api/sync/pull`, `app/api/sync/push`, `app/api/sync/sleep-ids` (the ghost-night sweep's id list, #2752), `app/api/sync-health` |
| Reading back one thing a native client wrote under its own id (#2813) | `GET /api/nutrition/food-items/{id}` → `getFoodItemById` and `GET /api/supplements/{id}/log?date=YYYY-MM-DD` (or `YYYY/MM/DD`) → `getSupplementDayLog`, both in `lib/data/postgres/slices/nutrition.ts`. **Food item:** 200 with the same `FoodItem` the list route returns; 404 (`{error:'Not found'}`) for an unknown id and another user's id alike; 400 for a non-UUID. `food_items` has no `deleted_at`, so there is no tombstone state. **Supplement day:** 200 `{supplementId, date, supplementDeleted, manual, foodDerived[], total}`; `manual` is the tick the supplements page writes and removes (null once unticked), `foodDerived` are meal contributions (each with its `sourceRef`), `total` is `summariseSupplementDay`'s sum across both (null on an empty day). `date` is the user's LOCAL day and is never converted through UTC; 400 for a missing or impossible date, 404 for a supplement that is not the caller's. A deleted definition still answers, flagged `supplementDeleted`. Both: 401 without a session, 429 at 120/min/user, `private, no-store`. Tests: `app/api/nutrition/food-items/__tests__/read-by-id.test.ts`, `app/api/supplements/[id]/log/__tests__/read-day.test.ts`, `lib/data/postgres/__tests__/read-food-item-and-supplement-day.test.ts` |
| Client wiring | `components/sync-provider.tsx`, `components/pull-to-sync.tsx` |

---

## 4. Data access — the Repository (`lib/data/`)

**All DB access goes through the repository interface.** Never write raw SQL/Drizzle in
a route or component; add a method to the repo.

| Concern | Where |
|---|---|
| Get the repo | `lib/data/index.ts` → `getRepository()` / `getRepositoryAsync` |
| Interface (207 methods, sectioned) | `lib/data/repository.ts` |
| Postgres implementation | `lib/data/postgres/adapter.ts` (+ `slices/` for periodization, nutrition, oura, programs, social, body-battery) |
| Table definitions (Drizzle) | `lib/data/postgres/schema.ts` |
| Batch upsert duplicate collapse (SQLSTATE 21000) | `lib/data/postgres/collapse-conflicts.ts` → `collapseOnConflict(rows, key, merge?)` + `keepLatestNonNull`. **Any `.values(<array>)` with an `onConflictDoUpdate` arm must collapse repeats on the conflict target first** — Postgres aborts the WHOLE command when a VALUES list hits the same conflict row twice, so one duplicate discards the entire chunk (5,771 production hits on `POST /api/hr-ingest`, up to 5,000 HR points each; Q-214 → Q-280). `merge` must mirror the site's own arm: the default last-wins is exact for a bare `excluded.*` arm, but a `COALESCE`/source-rank arm needs `keepLatestNonNull` and a `setWhere`-gated arm needs a comparator, or the collapse silently drops what the arm would have kept. Applied at all 8 batch sites in `slices/oura.ts` + `adapter.ts`. |
| The energy model's plain constants — the LEAF module (Q-401, LB-43) | `packages/shared/src/health/energy-baseline.ts` → `SEDENTARY_MULTIPLIER`, `STEP_BASELINE`, `WALKING_CADENCE_SPM`, `STEPS_PER_KM`, re-exported by `daily-energy.ts` so server-side importers are unaffected. **It must keep importing NOTHING.** `daily-energy` → `workout-energy` → `oura-models/constants` reaches a node builtin, and Turbopack refuses the client chunk — which has now taken the Nutrition tab to a 500 **twice**, with `node:path` (Q-401) and `node:fs/promises` (BF-87, fetching a constant for a line of copy). A test asserts the module has no `import` and no `require`, because `tsc` would say nothing. **Do not create a second leaf module for this** — LB-43 proposed `energy-constants.ts` not knowing this one existed, and two leaf modules for one purpose is the drift the one-formula rule is about. Do not "fix" it by making `oura-models` client-safe either: it reads model files off disk on purpose. |
| Oura's own workouts in the energy budget (#3087) | `packages/shared/src/health/oura-workout-activities.ts` → `ouraWorkoutActivities(workouts, counted, tz)` turns `oura_workouts` rows into the activity entries `computeActiveEnergy` costs; `lib/health/oura-budget-activities.ts` → `ouraBudgetActivities(repo, …)` reads them for a date range, used by `energy-balance-service.ts`, `lib/achievements.ts` (past-day budgets) and `/api/body-metadata`. **Skipped:** a workout overlapping a saved activity log or a strength session (already costed), and one reviewed but not logged (the old review card's dismissal). Walks, runs and hikes map to the app's types so `pedometerStepsInside` takes their steps back out; any other activity is costed at its own Oura MET through `metIdForOuraActivity` (`workout-energy.ts`, camelCase name → the table's words), and an unnamed one earns nothing rather than the generic 6-MET fallback. **Deleted in the Oura app:** the `workout` delete event tombstones the row (`tombstoneOuraWorkouts`, `oura_workouts.deleted_at`), and the daily reconcile tombstones one gone from a day it re-read whole, never a read's last day, as the spec does not say whether `end_date` is inclusive (`tombstoneOuraWorkoutsAbsent`); `getOuraWorkouts` leaves tombstoned rows out for every reader, and Oura sending one again revives it. Training load is unchanged: OTS already sees these minutes through Oura's MET series, and the volume ACWR counts strength only by design. |
| Which logged activities' steps count where (#3090, #3082) | `packages/shared/src/health/activity-steps.ts` → `INDOOR_STEP_ACTIVITY_TYPES` (treadmill), `PEDOMETER_ACTIVITY_TYPES` (walk, run, hike), `activityStepsForDayCount(activities)`, **`dayStepCount(deviceSteps, activities)`, the one builder for a day's step count** (#3095), `dayStepCountsByDate` for a many-day reader, `withIndoorSteps(row, activities)` to fold a body row (setting `deviceSteps`, the device's own count, only when indoor steps were added), `withLoggedDeviceSteps` for an optimistic row after a logged count, and `logSeedReading`, the reading a log sheet starts from. **Indoor steps are added to the day's step count** (whether the ring already counts them is unknown, below); every surface that shows the day's steps goes through these, **except the readiness Activity input and gauge, achievements, health-insight and nutrition-goals, which still read the raw device count (#3134)**: `/api/body-metadata` (today, `recent`, `weekToDate`), `/api/cardio-week`, `/api/day-log`, `/api/daily-digest`, `/api/day-review/week-window`, `/api/weekly-review/month-window`, the chat's `getRecoveryData`, `/api/health/trends` (Health's 14-day Steps sparkline), Home's and Health's local seeds (`app/session-select/local-meta-seed.ts`), both web log sheets' seed (`components/health/log-seed-value.ts`) and post-save rebuild, and Preview's log sheets and optimistic overlay. **Whether the ring already counts treadmill steps is unknown** (#3095: it missed the session outright on 4 of 12 treadmill days, and 5 of the other 8 fit it counting most of it), so treadmill stays on the indoor list pending the owner's call (#3112); **outdoor steps are not**, because the ring already counted them, and `daily-energy.ts` takes them back out of the passive step term (`pedometerStepsInside`). Both lists are explicit and a test holds them disjoint, so a new activity type counts nowhere until someone classifies it. A leaf importing nothing, like `energy-baseline.ts`, because a client component reads it — it is not a second energy-constants module, it classifies activity types. |
| Shared Coach contracts | `packages/shared/src/coach/patch.ts` and `widgets.ts`; backend and native wrappers re-export the same schemas |
| Coach conversation scopes (LA-47) | `lib/coach/scopes.ts` → `COACH_SCOPES` / `coachScope(id)` / `pickTools(tools, allowed)`, read by `app/api/coach/route.ts` and `buildWidgetTools`. A scope names read tools, widget tools, choice sources, patch domains and a prompt section. **Scope by WITHHOLDING, never by instructing** — a prompt asking the model not to read training data is a request it will occasionally ignore; a tool it never receives is a boundary it cannot cross, and `renderChoiceList`'s `source` and `proposeChange`'s `domain` enums are rebuilt narrowed per request so an out-of-scope call fails schema validation and the SDK retries the model. `general` withholds nothing, so an unscoped caller is unchanged. An unknown id widens to `general` rather than 400ing — and uses `Object.hasOwn`, not `in`, because `in` matched `"toString"` and handed the route `Object.prototype.toString` as a scope. |
| WebView renderer death — recovery and reporting (BF-80) | `android/.../RenderProcessRecovery.java` (a Capacitor `WebViewListener`: returns `true`, stamps the death into SharedPreferences with `didCrash`, posts `activity.recreate()`) · `MainActivity` registers it and exposes `window.AndroidRenderer` · `lib/renderer-recovery.ts` → `reportRenderProcessDeaths()` collects it on the next boot into an `error_events` row, mounted on `ErrorReporter` · frozen by `scripts/check-render-process-recovery.js`. **The default is fatal, not silent** — Capacitor's `BridgeWebViewClient` already forwards the event and `WebViewListener`'s own default returns `false`, which the platform reads as "kill the app", so removing the listener does not degrade to the old behaviour, it restores process termination. `recreate()` and not `reload()`: a WebView whose renderer has gone is permanently unusable. Posted rather than called, because the callback runs on the UI thread with that WebView on the stack. Recording it natively is the only option — a dead renderer takes the JS reporter with it, which is why the fault had never produced a row. |
| App-load telemetry (BF-19) | `lib/app-load-metrics.ts` → `startAppLoadReporting(buildId)` (the only call a UI file makes; waits for `load`, reads `getEntriesByType('navigation')`, reports **once per JS context** via `sendBeacon`) · ingest `POST /api/app-load` · the admin report route `GET /api/admin/app-load-report` (p50/p95 per route, split cold vs warm) was retired in #2862, so rows are still written but nothing reads them until a report is rebuilt · table `app_load_metrics` (migration 229), pruned at **14 days** on write. **Never the outbox** — telemetry queued as a mutation would sit in front of the user's food logs on the next push. If a report returns, keep the cold/warm split: every deploy rewrites the service-worker cache name, so a pooled percentile measures release cadence, not the app. `buildId` is baked into the client bundle by `next.config.ts`, not stamped server-side, so a device on a stale shell reports the build it is actually running. |
| A clinical result from an external provider (BF-41 / BF-2) | `dexa_scans` + `dexa_scan_regions` (migration **240**; `claude_ro` views regenerated in **241**) ← `saveDexaScan`/`getLatestDexaScan`/`listDexaScans` ← `GET`/`POST /api/dexa-scans`. **Typed columns, never JSONB** — BF-2's calibration and BF-33's FFM comparison do arithmetic on named columns, which is why `measured_rmr` (migrations 225/226) is the template and this follows it. **Schema written from the real printout** in the owner’s clinical baseline (held privately since 2026-09-24, RV-199), not from a description — CLAUDE.md's external-field rule applies to a DEXA report as much as to an API. Masses stay in **grams** because that is what the report prints; `pct_fat_young_normal`/`pct_fat_age_matched` are **percentiles**, not percentages. Regions are a **child table** because a region set is N rows, and `subtotal`/`total` are **aggregates in that table**, so anything summing it must exclude them. Upsert on `(user_id, scanned_on)`; a re-save **replaces** the region set rather than merging, so a re-extraction reading ten regions leaves ten. **No source document is stored** — extract, confirm, save the fields, discard the file; there is no `bytea` here and adding one is its own decision. `source` is `manual` or `extracted` and there is no third value for a model's unconfirmed output. |
| Injury constraints in a prompt, and in a candidate list | `packages/shared/src/workout/injury-context.ts` → `activeInjuries` / `activeInjuredMuscles` / `formatInjuryContext(injuries, today)` (the one prompt line: muscle, severity, days active, the user's note) · `packages/shared/src/workout/injury-substitution.ts` → `excludeInjuredExercises(library, muscles)` (extracted out of `injurySafeAlternatives`, which now routes through it). Consumers: `/api/generate-program`, `/api/builder-chat`, the mid-workout swap sheet, `/api/coach/options`. **The exclusion belongs on the candidate list, not in the prompt** — an exercise absent from the list is one the model cannot return, and using the swap sheet's own predicate is what stops the builder programming something the swap sheet then offers to replace. `formatInjuryContext` takes `today` rather than reading the clock, so "days active" is not keyed to the server's timezone. |
| The user's weight goal — ONE column (LB-42) | `users.target_weight_kg`, read by `getUserGoals`/`getUserById` and written by `updateUserGoals`/`updateUserProfile`. **`users.weight_goal_kg` is retired** (migration 246) — no reader, no writer, kept because dropping a column is irreversible and the row-scoped audit view cannot show other accounts' values. The API field is still called `weightGoalKg` and maps onto `target_weight_kg`: keeping the name is what let the Edit Profile sheet and the Goals accordion converge with no client change, and two editors filling two columns is exactly what let the goal on screen and the goal in the nutrition-coach prompt diverge. Do not add a second column for a goal that already has one. |
| Health-data provenance (per-field precedence merge) | `lib/data/health-source.ts` → `HEALTH_SOURCES`/`sourceRank` (manual > scale_ble > oura_ble > oura_cloud > health_connect > legacy) + `mergeSet`/`initialSourceMap`. Each of `body_metrics`/`sleep_sessions`/`oura_daily` carries a `source_map` JSONB (mig 120): the upsert helpers (`upsertBodyMetrics`, `upsertOuraSleep`, `upsertOuraDaily`) merge **per field** against that field's own last-writer source, so a lower source can only fill nulls — a manual weight is never clobbered by Health Connect / Oura Cloud, yet the ring's HRV or HC's steps keep updating on the same day. Every writer passes its `source`; the param is required (compiler-enforced sweep) — including `saveSleepSession`, which since v1.250.0 delegates to `upsertOuraSleep` rather than carrying a second, provenance-free sleep write. **⚠ Reading `source_map` backwards is a trap (LA-115, 2026-09-16):** it records only the WINNING source per field, so "0 rows credited to `health_connect`" does NOT mean Health Connect produced nothing — it is equally consistent with HC producing a value and losing the merge to a higher-ranked source. Measured that day: HRV and SpO₂ both read 0 from HC, which matched a real defect, but resting heart rate read 0 too and its read path is fine. To ask whether a source is producing anything, instrument the source; this column cannot answer it. |
| Ring or Oura on a given day (the switch-day rule, #2998) | `packages/shared/src/health/source-rank.ts` → `ringValueStands(day, switchDay)`: the ring's value stands before the day the user switched to the Oura API (`getOuraCloudSwitchDay`), and on every day with no switch; from the switch day on, Oura's is read. **Every reader that chooses between the ring's value and Oura's goes through it** — `storedNight`, `dropBleNightsOuraCloudHolds`, Body Battery's stress drain, shadow readiness's daytime stress, the readiness payload's stress/recovery and `daytimeStress` chip, the coach, the health insight (#3191, #3193). Never write `day < switchDay` inline. The SQL half (`mergeSet`'s `ouraCloudFrom`) is the same rule on the write side |
| Which `oura_heartrate` rows a score reads (multi-source HR) | `packages/shared/src/health/hr-window-merge.ts` → `mergeHrSources(rows)` = `yieldAggregatorToDevice` (a Health Connect / Apple Health row is not read when a device row lies within `AGGREGATOR_COVER_MS`, 5 min) then `preferStrapBuckets` (strap beats ring per 10 s). Applied in `getHrForWindow`; `getObservedHrProfile` is its SQL mirror and `observed-hr-sql-equivalence.test.ts` holds them equal. The aggregator tier (`AGGREGATOR_HR_SOURCES`) is derived from `SOURCE_RANK`, never listed. Writes: device sources through `upsertOuraHeartrate`; an aggregator through `upsertAggregatorHeartrate`, which keeps every row (D2) but never displaces a device row on a timestamp collision, and drops cached `daily_zone_minutes` days from its earliest sample (#2168). |
| Decoding the Renpho body-composition scale (the `0x10` weight packet, the speculative stored record, the repeat and replay guards) | `packages/shared/src/devices/scale-protocol.ts` → `parseWeightPacket`, `parseStoredRecord`, `isRepeatedStableFrame`, `isNewWeighInEvidence`. TrainingAi Preview reads the scale with it (`mobile/src/features/devices/scale/ScaleSession.ts`); the Kotlin twins are `ScaleProtocol` (the layouts), `ScaleWeighInGate` (the replay guard) and the inline repeat check in `ScaleGattClient` (all in `android/app/src/main/java/com/trainingai/app/scale/`), so change a byte layout or either guard in both. **Who a weigh-in belongs to is never decided on a phone:** the band (`SCALE_WEIGHT_CLAIM_PCT`, `SCALE_WEIGHT_ANOMALY_PCT`) and body composition live once in `lib/scale-ble/composition.ts`, applied by `app/api/scale-ble/samples/route.ts`; Preview sends the raw reading and shows the verdict (#2844). |
| Decoding a chest strap (Heart Rate Measurement `0x2A37`, Battery Level `0x2A19`) and its low-battery alert | `packages/shared/src/devices/heart-rate-measurement.ts` → `parseHeartRateMeasurement`, `parseBatteryLevel`; `packages/shared/src/devices/low-battery.ts` → `decideLowBatteryAlert` (alert below 35 %, re-arm at 40 %). One implementation for the current app (`lib/live-hr/hr-measurement.ts` re-exports it) and TrainingAi Preview (`mobile/src/features/devices/strap/StrapSession.ts`). The Kotlin twins are `PolarProtocol.parseHeartRateMeasurement` and `DeviceBatteryNotifier.decide`; change the thresholds in both places. Preview's strap samples go to `/api/hr-ingest` like the current app's (#2844). |
| Adding a new data source (ring/strap/platform) — the canonical shape per data type (PS-40) | [`docs/data-source-connector-guide.md`](data-source-connector-guide.md) — transport categories, the shape of each data type (HR as a time-series list, sleep as a session + stage-interval array, body metrics as sparse daily scalars, etc.), the ranked-provenance merge, scored-vs-learning-mode isolation, and the shared ingestion-route pattern. Read before wiring any new source; a formal typed `DataSourceConnector` registry is proposed there (§7) but not yet built (plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-14-data-source-connector-interface.md`). |
| Connection pool (load-bearing — do not weaken) | `lib/data/postgres/client.ts` → `getDb()` |
| Picking a migration number or a local SQLite version (BF-211) | `node scripts/next-schema-number.js` — **the only correct answer**. Since BF-214 ② (2026-09-28) the Postgres answer is a UTC-minute prefix, `YYYYMMDDHHMM` (`minuteStamp`), and both appliers order by the leading integer (`sortMigrationFiles` in `lib/data/postgres/client.ts`, twinned in `scripts/local-db/migrate.js` and held to one order by `scripts/__tests__/bf214-numeric-migration-order.test.ts`); pure logic in `scripts/lib/migration-claims.js` → `surveyClaims(merged, branches)`. It fetches, reads every ref, and reports the next free number **plus which numbers an unmerged branch is holding**, named by branch and file. `ls … | tail -1` cannot see a reservation: an open PR's file is not in the merged tree, and that is issue #1620 / `#1608`, where a contributor derived 288/289 from the filenames they could see and `main` had already used them. A hand-maintained row in the backlog (now `docs/archive/implementation-backlog-2026-10-05.md`) answered this until 2026-09-27; a CI check pinned it to `max(merged) + 1`, so it could only restate the directory and could **never** run ahead of it to reserve anything — the reservations it claimed to carry lived in a free-text parenthetical nothing read, and the number itself had drifted eleven behind once. **It fetches on purpose:** a stale remote-tracking ref answers with what that branch held last time, and while this was being written an un-refreshed ref reported `#1608` on 284/285, numbers it had been renumbered off. `scripts/check-migration-numbers.js` is still the CI gate and is unchanged — it fails on a duplicate in one tree, which is what a branch update surfaces. |
| `claude_ro` audit views: owner at RUNTIME | `scripts/generate-claude-ro-views.js` emits `current_setting('app.claude_ro_owner', true)`, **never a baked uuid** (Q-456: the owner's id had reached 18 committed migrations, and the documented regenerate-per-table process re-published it every time). `lib/data/postgres/claude-ro-owner.ts` → `bootstrapClaudeRoOwner()` sets it at boot from `CLAUDE_RO_OWNER_USER_ID` ?? `ADMIN_EXPORT_USER_ID` ?? `WEBHOOK_USER_ID`, so a deploy configures itself; unset leaves the views returning zero rows, which is the fail-closed direction. |
| Guarding a dynamic route's id | `lib/api/route-errors.ts` → `invalidUuidResponse(id)` for a uuid key, **`numericRouteId(id)` for a `bigserial` one (BF-53)**. Ten tables in `schema.ts` have a non-uuid primary key and five repository methods take `id: number`; applying the UUID guard to one of those is a **400 for every real request**, which is how the whole pending weigh-in triage shipped dead — the correct `Number.isInteger` check sat unreachable on the next line. `numericRouteId` matches `/^\\d+$/` rather than `Number.isInteger(Number(x))`, which accepts `'1e3'`, `'0x10'`, `' 41 '` and `'0'`; it returns `{ ok, id }` so a caller cannot forget to parse. `app/api/__tests__/numeric-route-id-guards.test.ts` derives the number-keyed methods from `repository.ts` and fails if any route calls one behind the UUID guard — the sweep that caused this is the reason the rule is a test rather than a note. |
| DB footprint / reclaim numbers (BF-54) | `getOuraStorageStats` and `vacuumTableFull` (`lib/data/postgres/slices/oura.ts`) → `GET /api/oura-ble/db-stats`. **Row figures are `count(*)`, never `n_live_tup`.** That counter is a planner ESTIMATE and `last_analyze` is NULL on every table here, so it read 552 against `oura_raw_samples`'s 180,415 real rows, 0 against `rr_intervals`' 87,015 and 1 against `error_events`' 6,102. It was not only displayed — `vacuumTableFull` used it to justify the reclaim, whose own comment reads a huge size against few live rows as *pure bloat*, so the console would take an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock with timeouts lifted and reclaim nothing. **The SIZE columns are exact and unchanged** (`pg_total_relation_size` is read from the filesystem); only the row columns of `pg_stat_user_tables` are estimates, and conflating the two cost a session (Q-528). A test freezes `n_live_tup` at zero call sites outside comments. |
| Query timings for the audit role (BF-21) | `claude_ro.pg_stat_statements` (migration **242**), emitted by the same generator. **The one view here that is NOT row-scoped**, and correctly so: `pg_stat_statements` stores NORMALISED query text — literals become `$n` — so it carries shapes and timings, never a parameter value or a row. Five columns only (`query, calls, total_exec_time, mean_exec_time, rows`); a test refuses `queryid`, `userid`, `dbid` and the block-I/O counters, because widening the list is how "shapes, not values" stops being true. **Guarded on `to_regclass('public.pg_stat_statements')`** — the extension needs `shared_preload_libraries` and a restart, so it is production-only, and an unguarded CREATE VIEW would fail `ensureSchema` on cold start and take the app down. In the GENERATOR rather than a one-off migration because that file DROPs and rebuilds the schema every run: a hand-written view would survive until the next table was added, then vanish silently. |
| Postgres migrations | `lib/data/postgres/migrations/<prefix>_*.sql` (auto-applied on cold start, in leading-integer order). New files take a UTC-minute prefix from `node scripts/next-schema-number.js` — see the row above; the backlog pointer table this row used to name is gone (BF-211). |
| User preferences (server-authoritative, Q-392) | `users.preferences` JSONB (mig 206) ← `getUserPreferences`/`updateUserPreferences` ← `GET`/`PATCH /api/user/preferences`.
