Shape, the device-key/encoding map (`PREFERENCE_STORAGE`) and the deliberately-not-synced list (`DEVICE_LOCAL_PREFERENCES`) all live in `packages/shared/src/user/preferences.ts`. **PATCH merges under a row lock** — two devices each writing their own keys both survive; an explicit `null` clears a key. `localStorage` is a first-paint seed written *from* the server, never the reverse (the Q-241 goals rule). |
| Cached exercise→muscle map | `lib/data/exercise-muscle-map-cache.ts` |

Repository domains (comment sections in `repository.ts`): Users, Invites, Programs,
Scheduling, Progression Styles, Block Periodization, Workout Logging, Body & Activity,
Personal Records, Data Tools, Queries, Exercise Library, Mood, Day check-in, Nutrition,
Goal Recommendations, Friends, Seasons, AI Health Insights, Sync, Feedback, Error events,
Injuries, Supplements, AI Periodization, Oura Ring, Body Battery.

---

## 5. Auth, session & security

| Concern | Where |
|---|---|
| Session in a route | `import { auth } from '@/auth'` → `const session = await auth()`; user tz at `session.user.timezone` |
| Native confirmation and revocation | `lib/auth/mobile/native-transaction.ts`, `mobile/session.ts`, `bearer-session.ts`; isolated native tokens, account-bound consent and token-family revocation |
| NextAuth config | `auth.ts`, `auth.config.ts`, `app/api/auth/[...nextauth]/route.ts` |
| A claim the SERVER needs and the browser must not see (RV-193) | `lib/auth/session-token.ts` → `sessionTokenFrom(headers)` / `googleRefreshTokenFrom(headers)`. **`auth()` is the wrong door for one of these**: it returns what `authConfig.callbacks.session` built, and that object is exactly what `GET /api/auth/session` hands to page JavaScript — which is how the Google refresh token, long-lived and able to write to the calendar, was readable by any script in the origin. Such a claim stays on the JWT and a route reads it from the request here. **`secureCookie` is load-bearing, not cosmetic:** Auth.js derives the decryption salt from the cookie NAME, so a wrong value derives a different key, every valid token reads as invalid, and the feature dies with a plain 401 and nothing in the logs. Three places share that one convention — this module, `app/api/auth/exchange-mobile-token` (which writes the cookie) and `lib/observability/request-error.ts` (which matches the name to pick the salt) — and `bearer-session.ts` calls this rather than keeping a fourth copy. Test it with a REALLY encrypted token (`encode()` from `next-auth/jwt`), never a mocked decode: a mock passes against an implementation that decrypts nothing. |
| Mobile token exchange | `lib/auth/mobile/tokens.ts` (`createMobileAuthToken`/`consumeMobileAuthToken`), `app/api/auth/exchange-mobile-token` |
| Email identity (LA-61) | `normalizeEmail` (`packages/shared/src/validation/email.ts`) — trim + lower-case, applied inside the repository on every write (`upsertUser`, `createEmailUser`, `addInvite`) and every lookup (`getUserByEmail`, `isInvited`, `removeInvite`, friend lookup), which match by `lower(email)` so a row the migration could not normalise is still found. Unique `lower(email)` indexes on `users` and `invited_emails` (migration 296). Route handlers need no call of their own; never compare an email with a plain `eq` |
| Deleting an account (#2120) | `lib/data/postgres/slices/account-deletion.ts` → `deleteAccount` via `repo.deleteAccount(userId)` — **the one path**: `DELETE /api/account` (self, typed phrase, `.strict()`) and `DELETE /api/admin/users` both call it. One transaction: lock the users row, `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL DEFERRED`, purge the out-of-cascade rows that name the user (`db_query_log`, `rate_limits`, `invited_emails`, `email_normalisation_preimage`), then `DELETE FROM users` and let the FK cascade take everything else, soft-deleted rows included. `OUTSIDE_THE_CASCADE` classifies every table the cascade cannot reach, and `account-deletion.test.ts` fails when it disagrees with the live schema or when an FK the cascade can trip over is not DEFERRABLE. Device half: `lib/account/delete-account.ts` → server first, then `signOutAndClearDevice()`. Never touches the ring key. **Admin delete is for signups that never got in only (issue 2695):** `repo.deleteAccount(id, {onlyIfNoData: true})` refuses (`refusedHasData`, route answers 409) when `usersWithData` finds a row in any table with a CASCADE FK to `users`, ignoring the seeded defaults every signup gets; `GET /api/admin/users` returns `hasData` per inactive user for the Pending / Deactivated split |
| Native app refresh tokens — table and hashing only (#2076) | `native_refresh_tokens` (migration `202610071636`, `claude_ro.native_refresh_tokens` without `token_hash`) via `createNativeRefreshToken` / `findNativeRefreshTokenByHash` / `rotateNativeRefreshToken` / `revokeNativeRefreshToken` / `revokeNativeRefreshTokenFamily` / `listActiveNativeRefreshTokens` in `lib/data/postgres/slices/native-refresh-tokens.ts`; the stored form is `lib/auth/refresh-token-hash.ts` → `hashRefreshToken(raw)` (lowercase hex SHA-256, **`null` for anything shorter than 43 base64url chars**, i.e. under 256 bits) and the branded `RefreshTokenHash` the data layer takes, so a raw token does not compile there. **Only the hash is stored and no method returns it.** One `family_id` per sign-in on one device; `rotateNativeRefreshToken` marks the old row rotated and inserts its successor in one transaction, and only one of two concurrent rotations wins. A rotated row is kept so a replay of it can be recognised as reuse (revoke the family). Every method is scoped to `userId` except `findNativeRefreshTokenByHash`, which is how a presented token names its user. CHECKs: 64-hex hash, revoked_at ⇔ revoked_reason, a lifetime in (0, 90 days], label 1–80 chars. Server-only, excluded from the export (credentials), cascades with the account. Native sign-in uses this ledger for revocable sessions; refresh rotation, pruning and a device-session UI remain follow-ups. |
| Rate limiting | `lib/rate-limit.ts` → `rateLimit(key, limit, windowMs)` — every AI/expensive/write route gets one, match siblings |
| A body that failed Zod validation | `lib/api/route-errors.ts` → **`invalidBodyResponse(error, fallback?)`** (+ `userFacingIssueMessage(error)` for the string). **The one answer to `!parsed.success`** — 19 sites used to return `parsed.error.issues[0]?.message`, putting zod's own phrasing on screen (*"Too big: expected string to have <=80 characters"*). It surfaces a message a HUMAN wrote and replaces one the library wrote, and the discriminator is **not** `issue.code === 'custom'`: measured on zod 4.4.3, `z.string().min(1, 'Name is required')` reports `too_small` carrying the hand-written message, so that filter silently discards it. It compares the message against what zod's own error map would render instead. `invalid_type` and `unrecognized_keys` are never surfaced — they describe the request's SHAPE, and `invalid_type` cannot be compared (the issue reaching a caller has lost its `input`). Anything that throws falls back, since `z.core.locales` is not documented surface and the failure must never be a leaked internal (LA-70) |
| A caught error in a route | `lib/api/route-errors.ts` → `refusalResponse(err, fallback)` + `isRefusal(err)`. **The one answer to a `catch` in a write route.** A `NotFoundError` or `UserFacingError` (`packages/shared/src/errors.ts`) is echoed with the status it carries; anything else gets `fallback` at 500 and keeps its detail in the log and `reportServerError`. Throw `UserFacingError(message, status)` for a refusal whose text was written for the user — that marker is what separates it from a Drizzle error, whose `.message` *is* the failing statement (Q-320). `scripts/check-no-raw-error-in-response.js` enforces it |
| A dynamic route's `[id]` param | `lib/api/route-errors.ts` → `invalidUuidResponse(id)` (and the pure `isUuid`). **Every `const { x } = await params` in `app/api/**` runs it before the id reaches the repository** — a non-UUID otherwise hits Postgres as `22P02` and the route answers 500 for what is plainly a 400 (21 route/method pairs did, Q-482). 400 rather than 404 on purpose: UUID syntax is public, so it distinguishes nothing, unlike the ownership 403-vs-404 case in `errors.ts`. `scripts/check-dynamic-route-uuid-guard.js` enforces it per handler, with no baseline |
| A client-supplied FK into a user-scoped table | Validate it against the caller BEFORE the write, and scope the read that resolves it. `progression_styles` → `repo.progressionStyleIdsOwned(userId, ids)`; `phase_sets` → `listPhaseSets`; food refs → `foodLogRefsValid`. Narrow existence checks, not a full hydrate — the log-exercise path runs one on every logged set. Refuse with a 4xx on an **interactive** path; on a path reachable from the **outbox**, drop the field instead, because a 4xx there is a poison pill that quarantines the whole mutation (RV-32). Any such write reachable from `app/api/**` AND `pushMutations` puts the guard in the one shared function both call |
| Bounds on a stored goal or nutrition target | `packages/shared/src/validation/goal-bounds.ts` → `GOAL_BOUNDS`, `goalBoundSchema(field)`. Every surface that writes one builds its Zod from here — the two user routes (`/api/nutrition/targets`, `/api/user/goals`) and the AI Coach's patch schema. They used to each declare their own: measured 2026-09-03 the Coach allowed 100,000 across all seven fields while the forms enforced 20,000 / 2,000 / 30,000 / 20,000, so a model could store a number the user's own screen refuses, up to 50× out (RV-41). `int` is part of the bound, not separate — a fractional `stepsGoal` was a 400 on one path and a 500 on the other |
| The canonical UUID guard | `packages/shared/src/validation/uuid.ts` → `isUuid`. Lives in `packages/shared` rather than `lib/api/route-errors.ts` (which re-exports it) because that module imports `next/server`, and the repository layer and the rollup worker must not (RV-32) |
| Request body guards | `packages/shared/src/http/request-guards.ts` → `readJsonLimited(req, maxBytes)`, `isAllowedImageMime`. Imported as `@trainingai/shared/http/request-guards`. **Request SCHEMAS live in `packages/shared/src/validation/`** — `injury.ts` is the reference CLAUDE.md names, `progression-style.ts` the newest (LA-74). Both are `.strict()`, which `scripts/check-strict-request-schemas.js` requires: a non-strict schema DROPS an unknown key, so a renamed field becomes a successful write of the wrong thing rather than a 400 (Q-464). Strict is affordable when a shape has ONE producer; `POST /api/workout-templates` has two that disagree, which is why it is still unvalidated and still LA-74 |
| Client IP for rate limiting | `packages/shared/src/http/client-ip.ts` → `clientIp(req)`, `clientIpFromForwardedFor`, `trustedProxyCount`. Counts in from the RIGHT of `X-Forwarded-For`, because a proxy appends its peer and the leftmost hop is whatever the caller sent (Q-493). Depth via `TRUSTED_PROXY_COUNT`, default 1. Every rate-limited route keys on this — do not re-read the header at a call site. |
| Reading a request body | Always `readJsonLimited`, **never bare `req.json()`**. A Zod schema after the read bounds what is *stored*, not what is transferred and parsed — measured 2026-08-18, a 20 MB body to `auth/register` was read, buffered and fully parsed before being answered 400. `scripts/check-bounded-request-body.js` ratchets the conversion, shrink-only per file, with the three session-less routes (`auth/register`, `auth/exchange-mobile-token`, `health-connect/ingest`) already at zero (Q-322 / Q-498) |
| Fetching a user-supplied URL | `lib/net/safe-fetch.ts` → `fetchPublicUrl(url, opts)`, `isPublicAddress(addr)`. **The only sanctioned way to fetch a URL a user typed.** https-only, port 443, no embedded credentials, every resolved address checked against the private/loopback/link-local/CGNAT blocks, each redirect hop re-validated, response bounded by bytes/timeout/content-type. Failures return a `reason` code — the caller maps it to a message and never echoes it. Residual risk stated in the file: DNS rebinding is not closed out |
| PKCE (OAuth) | `lib/auth/mobile/pkce.ts` → `computePkceChallenge`, `verifyPkce` |
| Oura OAuth state | `lib/oura/cloud/connect-ticket.ts` (#2858); see §8. The Oura webhook signature is `lib/oura/cloud/webhook.ts` → `verifyOuraSignature` (#2859): HMAC-SHA256 of `x-oura-timestamp` + body with the client secret, constant-time, stale timestamps refused, fail closed. |
| Server error reporting | `lib/observability.ts` → `reportServerError(err, context)` → `POST /api/client-error` |
| Client error boundary | `components/error-reporter.tsx`, `app/error.tsx` |
| Structured logging | `packages/shared/src/logger.ts` → `errorLog`, `infoLog` |
| Native ingest-URL allowlist | `android/app/src/main/java/com/trainingai/app/IngestUrlPolicy.kt` → `isAllowed`, `normalize` — the one place deciding where the Oura/scale/Polar services may post frames (RV-196). Pure, no Android deps, JVM-tested. |
| Android backup exclusions | `android/app/src/main/res/xml/backup_rules.xml` (API 23-30) + `data_extraction_rules.xml` (API 31+) — the WebView session cookie is kept out of backup and device transfer (OR-159). So is the Oura ring key (`sharedpref` `oura_ble.xml`), per OR-160: the owner holds his own copy and keeps Google out of its custody. |

Security defaults (fail-closed, verify-before-lookup, Zod-at-ingest, no bare `JSON.parse`
of LLM text) are in `CLAUDE.md` "AI & Security Defaults".

---

## 6. Domain math / formulas — one implementation each

**Grep before writing any formula** (`CLAUDE.md` "One Formula, One Place").

| Formula / concept | Where |
|---|---|
| **Named inputs and the source cascade (#2448)** — device-agnostic `steps/min`, `MET/min`, `HR/min`, `HRR60`, `HRV/night`, `RHR/night` | `packages/shared/src/inputs/cascade.ts` → `INPUTS` (the registry: each input's routes by rung — provided → derived → estimated), `resolveSlots` / `resolveMinutes` (best rung per slot; an unfilled minute comes back `missing` with `value: null`, never 0), `coverage`, `sumFilled` (null when nothing filled), `baselineValues` (drops low-wear slots and filters by source — baselines stay per source) · `packages/shared/src/inputs/adapters.ts` → one adapter per route (`stepCandidates`, `metGridCandidates`, `minuteMeanCandidates`, `activeKcalMetCandidates`, `ringMotionMetCandidates` (the ring's `0x47` motion through the fitted default map `RING_MOTION_MET_MAP_V1`, #2781; the byte read is `motionLowIntensity` in `lib/oura-ble/decode.ts`), `cadenceMetCandidates` (through `cadenceToMet` in `health/cadence.ts`), `ringStillMetCandidates`, `sleepSessionMetCandidates`, `compendiumMetCandidates`, `hrr60Candidates`, `nightlyCandidates`), each reusing the existing formula rather than restating it. HR→MET was dropped (#2115 v4). **A scorer reads this layer, never a device table; a new device writes an adapter, never touches a scorer** (readiness-tree → *Inputs are device-agnostic*). **`accel_met` (#2495, built, NOT wired):** `packages/shared/src/health/accel-met.ts` → `accelMinuteIntensity` (scale-free: each `oura_accel_chunks` chunk normalised by its own median magnitude, per-minute mean absolute deviation in g), `accelMetPoints` (needs a fitted `AccelMetModel`; there is no default), `fitAccelMetModel` and `accelMetCalibration` (leave-one-day-out error against `device_met`); the production report is `scripts/tuning/accel-met-calibration-2495.cjs`. Wiring it in means feeding `accelMetPoints` to `minuteMeanCandidates(…, 'accel_met', …)` once the report clears. Training load (#2115 v4) reads `MET/min` through it via `packages/shared/src/health/met-input.ts` → `resolveDayMetPerMinute`; #2226, #2299 and the #2377 shadow engine are next |
| 1RM, AMRAP, rep-max, bodyweight rescale | `packages/shared/src/1rm.ts` (`calc1RM`, `calcAmrap1RM`, `calculate1RM`, `repMaxFromOneRm`, `estimateOneRm`, `bestSetOneRm`, `rescaleBodyweightReps`, `resolveBodyweightStyle`, `mround`). **One estimator for every path** (server log, device, edit, live readout): a prescribed set is scored against the bar it was given, via `withPrescribedBars(style, bars, basis)` (issue 2200; device `styleWithPrescribedBars` in `components/workout/utils.ts`, server `prescriptionBasisKg` in the log payload, else `getPrescriptionBasisBefore`); a styleless set is undiscounted (issue 2357); the AMRAP discount is a straight line between its anchors and above 30 reps counts at 30 (issue 2193). Stored styleless 1RMs are re-derived by `rederiveStylelessOneRm` (`lib/workout/rederive-styleless-one-rm.ts`, planner `packages/shared/src/workout/styleless-one-rm-rederive.ts`); stored bodyweight 1RMs are re-encoded onto the smooth discount by `reencodeBodyweightOneRm` (`lib/workout/reencode-bodyweight-one-rm.ts`, planner `packages/shared/src/workout/bodyweight-one-rm-reencode.ts`, #2760), and both write through `applyOneRmRewrite` · `barShareOfOneRm` — what a loaded bar actually is of a kg 1RM, for display beside a rounded weight (#2378; never print the prescribed % next to a rounded-up bar) |
| The bar the app prescribes for a set (after plate rounding) | `components/workout/utils.ts` → `prescribedBarWeights(ex, sets)` (`mroundStepUp(1rm × pct/100, step)` per style set, `null` where no style percentage sets it) and `plannedWeightsPayload`. The workout screen opens each set on it, and every set-log path stores it as `set_logs.planned_weight_kg` (#2445: server `logExerciseFromPayload`, local store, pull delta, stranded replay). **Use the stored bar, not `planned_pct`, for what was loaded** — the ceiling round makes the bar heavier than the pct says (#2200); the 1RM estimate does, through `withPrescribedBars`. Rows before 2026-10-07 are NULL and keep the planned-percentage arithmetic |
| Whether a set's RPE was rated or left at the pre-fill | `packages/shared/src/workout/rpe-source.ts` → `RpeSource` (`'expected'` \| `'rated'`), `rpeSourcesPayload(rpeValues, rated)` (the log payload's `rpeSources`, index-aligned with `rpeValues`, `null` beside a missing RPE) and `asRpeSource` (reads a stored value; anything else is unknown). The tap is tracked in `lib/stores/workout-store.ts` → `rpeRated` (set by `setRpeValue`, cleared by `initRpeValues`). Every set-log path stores it as `set_logs.rpe_source` (#2450: server `logExerciseFromPayload`, local store, pull delta, stranded replay; edits never touch it). **A source is only ever stored beside an RPE.** Per the owner's rule on #2253, `expected` means "went as predicted" — only `rated` moves a plan. Rows before 2026-10-07 are NULL (unknown, never assumed either way) |
| **The expected effort of a set — ONE formula** (#2253) | `packages/shared/src/ai-periodization/expected-rpe.ts` → `expectedRpe(pct, reps)` everywhere: the picker's pre-fill (`expectedRpePrefill(pct, plannedReps)`, rounded onto the 6–10 picker, 7 with no %; the screen calls it through `rpePrefill(ex, style, i)` in `components/workout/utils.ts`), the in-set back-off nudge (`components/workout/rpe-load-suggestion.ts`, against reps done, and only on a `rated` set), next week's per-exercise delta (`perExerciseRpeDelta`: **`expected` sets are left out**, and an exercise needs `RPE_DELTA_MIN_RATED_SETS` = 2 rated sets) and the emergency-deload trend (`rpeTrendFromSets`: an `expected` set counts as exactly on plan, delta 0, because a rated-only mean would be biased toward the trigger by a lifter who taps only hard sets). **`defaultRpeFromPct` (`floor(pct/10)`) is deleted**: it ran 0.71 below `expectedRpe`, so every untouched set read as "felt easy" and caused the phantom pushes. A pre-#2450 row (null source) is read as reported. |
| A set's measured one-minute HR recovery (HRR60) and the stored rest verdict | `packages/shared/src/workout/hrr60.ts` → `deriveHrr60(readings, setEndMs)` and `setRestAnchorMs(setEndMs, loggedAt)` (the one rest anchor: the timed `set_end_ms`, else `logged_at`; the live writer and the backfill both read it, #3238) (a reading within 3 s of the set end and of +60 s, no gap over 5 s between them, else `null`; reads the merged series and never names a device) and `restAdequateFromHrr60(bpm, bar)` (`null` with no measurement, else the bar). **The bar is the person's** (`personalHrr60Bar`, #2299 step 2): the median of their measured HRR60 over the 30 days before the session, clamped 5–15 bpm, the population 15 until 20 sets exist; `computeWorkoutHr` fits it and hands it to both the stored verdict and the recap. `computeSetHrStats` stores `set_hr_stats.hrr1_bpm` / `rest_adequate`, written as computed rather than COALESCEd so a stale verdict clears (#2457). **The drop curve takes the same rule at every offset** (`deriveHrrAt(readings, end, offsetMs)`, #2601): `drop_30s`…`drop_120s` exist only where the series can see that offset and inside the rest window, so `drop_60s` equals `hrr1_bpm` wherever both exist and a ring-only set has no drop curve; they are written as computed too. **The live recap** (`withHrr60Recovery`) replaces `analyseHrRecovery`'s nearest-reading `hrr1`/`adequate` with HRR60 against the bar, so the screen and the stored verdict agree and `workout_hr_stats.hrr1_best` (`summariseWorkoutHr`'s max) is the best HRR60. `hrr60Candidates` (the `HRR60` input) reads `hrr1Bpm`. Historical fill: `lib/workout/backfill-set-hrr1.ts` → `backfillSetHrr1` (planner `hrr1-backfill.ts` → `planHrr1Backfill`, which also gates stored drops and applies each session's bar), behind `POST /api/admin/backfill-set-hrr1` and agent job `backfill-set-hrr1`; then `lib/workout/recompute-hrr1-best.ts` → `recomputeHrr1Best` (planner `hrr1-best-recompute.ts`) behind `POST /api/admin/recompute-hrr1-best` and agent job `recompute-hrr1-best`. Both dry run unless `dryRun=false`; run in that order after a snapshot (docs/admin-actions.md). **The 14-day HR-recovery trend** (`/api/health/trends` → `hrr1Bpm`) reads HRR60 per set through `packages/shared/src/workout/hrr-trend.ts` → `setHrr60Values`, then `rollupDailyBestHrr`. The nearest-reading `hrr1` turned two idle ring readings a minute apart into a 0 bpm point (#2234); a ring-only day is a gap now |
| **Displaying** a stored 1RM — and comparing two | Same file. A bodyweight `estimated1rm` is a `BW_REF`(100)-relative index, NOT kilograms, so both rendering and ranking need the basis: `displayOneRm`/`oneRmUnit`/`oneRmLabel`/`displayOneRmDelta`/`displayOneRmSeries` (render, finding Q-12), `describePersonalRecord` (one PR as a phrase — digests, Q-19), `pickHeadlinePersonalRecord` (the "biggest PR" of a set — a plain `max` ranks a pull-up above a 96 kg bench press, 2026-08-03). Never hardcode a `kg` suffix on a 1RM |
| **A food's brand beside its name** | `components/nutrition/food-name-line.ts` → `foodSecondaryLine(brand, rest)`. **The name leads and the brand rides `FoodRow`'s secondary line, on all six surfaces that render one** (RV-208 ⑤). Two put the brand FIRST — and both were search lists, where the user typed the food name, so leading with the brand pushed the matched term into `line-clamp-2`. A third had its own `[brand, serving].join(' · ')`, right by coincidence; a fourth dropped the brand entirely, so the library's `Search` tab did not identify a food its own `Recent` tab did. Treats `''` and `'   '` as absent, because the column is nullable AND free text from `review-step.tsx`'s input. |
| **Display formatting — kg, minutes, h/m, pace** | `packages/shared/src/format/units.ts` → `formatKg(kg, {decimals, unit})`, `formatMinutes(min, {unit})`, `formatHoursMinutes(totalMinutes)`; pace stays in `packages/shared/src/health/vdot.ts` → `formatPace(secPerKm)` (`5:12/km`) and `formatPaceValue` (the bare `5:12`, for a site that renders its own unit element). **These decide rounding AND unit spacing together, which is the point** — the same weigh-in used to read `82.45 kg`, `82.5 kg`, `82.45kg` and `82kg` on four surfaces because each site made both choices separately (RV-90). `formatKg` rounds by exponential shift, not `toFixed`: `(1.005).toFixed(2)` is `1.00`, and the artefact is in the multiply, so `toPrecision` does not fix it. **`formatPaceValue` rounds the TOTAL seconds before splitting** — every hand-rolled pace formatter in the tree split first and printed the literal `5:60` for any value in `[5:59.5, 6:00)`. One documented exception: `components/health/hypnogram.tsx` keeps its own `h/m`, because a chart stage label reads better as `2h` than the helper's padded `2h 00m`, and it shares no surface with the four columns that use the helper. |
| A count with its noun ("1 set", "3 sets") | `packages/shared/src/format/plural.ts` → `plural(n, one, many)`. The plural is spelled out, never derived (`entry`/`entries`). Used by the native app, `lib/sign-out-pending.ts` and `packages/shared/src/nutrition/heart-response-view.ts`; it replaced three private copies (#2902). **Never write `` `${n} sets` `` for a count that can be 1.** |
| How long ago something happened ("about 1 month ago") | `packages/shared/src/format/time-ago.ts` → `timeAgo(at, now?)`. A clock-injectable `date-fns` `formatDistance` with the suffix, so the native app and the web friend feed read the same sentence; `null` for a value that is not a date. Used by the native Friends panel (#2936); the web feed calls `formatDistanceToNow` directly, which gives the same text. A private "41 days ago" formatter in a screen is the bug. |
| ACWR (volume) — canonical | `packages/shared/src/ai-periodization/acwr.ts` → `loadRatio(sessions, shortDays, longDays, asOf, { tz })` is the ONE training-load ratio (issue 2340): `computeVolumeAcwr` is its 7:28 call (acute window 7 local days, today inclusive, issue 2194) and `computeBlockTrend` its 28:90 call (a trend only, gates nothing; `blockTrendBand`, `BLOCK_TREND_THRESHOLDS`). `computeBlockTrendSeries` returns `BlockTrendSeries` (ratio, band, `spanDays`, ten weekly points) and is what `/api/training-load`'s `blockTrend` field serves. Windows are `LOAD_WINDOW_DAYS`; pass the user's `tz` so a window starts at a local midnight. **The band a user is shown is `trainingLoadBand(load, program, todayMid)` (OR-210)**, which the Health route and the chat tool both call: insufficient data, then baselining, then the band. **Program-age baselining is `acwrBaselineDaysRemaining(program, asOf)`** (`startedAt ?? createdAt`, 28 days, `ACWR_BASELINE_DAYS`) and every `computeVolumeAcwr` consumer applies it, which `or210-training-load-one-band.test.ts` enforces by scanning for new ones. Do not hand-roll program age or band a raw ratio. |
| Weekly per-muscle set target (BF-59) | `packages/shared/src/ai-periodization/volume-targets.ts` → `volumeLandmarks(goal, muscle)` (the per-muscle MEV/MAV/MRV table × a goal multiplier), plus `weeklyVolumeTarget(goal, muscle, phases)` and `phaseVolumeScale(phases)` / `PHASE_VOLUME_MULTIPLIER` (accumulation 1.0 · intensification 0.8 · realisation 0.6 · deload 0.5 — an owner calibration, chosen 2026-09-01, not a formula). **`/api/weekly-muscle-sets` DERIVES the displayed target from these and does not read `program_volume_targets.target_sets_per_week`** — those rows were seeded once and never corrected, so production holds a flat 14/10 binary the landmark table's own comment says it does not do, and which ignores the program's goal entirely. The stored rows are now the **roster** (which muscles a program trains); re-seeding their numbers would recreate the second source of truth this removed. **The phase mix comes from the sessions actually TRAINED in the week**, weighted per workout session, because the bar compares this week's logged sets — an empty week scales by 1. **`signals.ts` still reads the stored number** for the AI's `volumeBudgetPerMuscleGroup`, so engine and screen currently disagree; that is BF-59's first `Keep:` and needs a device pass because it changes prescribed sets. |
| Colour of a muscle's weekly sets against its target (#2554) | `packages/shared/src/health/volume-band.ts` → `muscleVolumeColor(sets, target, verdict?)` (and `targetBand` / `targetBandColor`): green at or above target, amber from 60% (integer compare, not `target * 0.6`), red below, grey for none logged; no target → the goal-landmark verdict, else the generic 10-set band (blue from 15). **The bars in `weekly-muscle-sets-card.tsx` and the body map above them both read it** — the card computes it once and passes `color` per muscle to `MuscleHeatmap`'s `volumes`, whose key follows via `volumeKey`. The old green `VOLUME_TINT_STEPS` ramp is gone, so there is no second rule to drift. Colours are theme tokens (`var(--accent-green)` etc.), valid in the SVG `fill` attribute; **never append a hex alpha to one**. An injured muscle is outlined (`injuredMuscles`), never filled, because the fill is the volume reading. `scripts/check-contrast.js` holds all three tokens at 3:1 against the untrained fill in both themes (RV-101) |
| Push / pull / legs / other split of weekly sets | `packages/shared/src/health/movement-balance.ts` → `movementBalance(...)`, `PATTERN_ORDER`, `PATTERN_LABEL`. Classification is `movementPattern` in `packages/shared/src/muscles.ts`, never re-derived. The web card (`components/health/movement-balance-card.tsx`, which re-exports `components/health/movement-balance.ts`) and the native Training panel (`mobile/src/features/health/data/training-view.ts`) both read it |
| Gaps in a day-by-day series (which points stand alone, how many days have a reading) | `packages/shared/src/health/series-gaps.ts` → `seriesShape(values)`. A chart draws an isolated point as its own dot rather than a zero-length segment; the caption's "n of m days" comes from the same call |
| The words for an AI Periodization phase and a session's recency | `packages/shared/src/ai-periodization/status-labels.ts` → `PHASE_LABELS` (typed by `PeriodizationPhase`, so a new phase is a compile error) and `lastTrainedLabel(daysAgo)`. Used by `components/health/ai-periodization-status-card.tsx` and the native `training-view.ts` (#2900) |
| An activity's title as shown ("EXERCISE_TYPE_OPEN_WATER_SWIMMING" → "Open Water Swimming") | `packages/shared/src/format/activity-title.ts` → `formatActivityTitle(title)`; anything not a Health Connect type constant is returned untouched |
| An activity row's text (date · clock time · minutes; distance and kcal) | `packages/shared/src/format/activity-row.ts` → `activityRowText(log)` → `{ subtitle, facts }`. Used by `components/health/activity-history-card.tsx` and the native Health Training card |
| The delta beside a strength bar ("+3 reps", "-2.5 kg") and its two colours | `packages/shared/src/health/strength-progress.ts` → `strengthDeltaText(metric)` and `TREND_COLOR`. The native app maps better / worse to its own theme tokens (`theme.progress`), seeded from `TREND_COLOR` in dark |
| Strength rows grouped by session (1RM only, empty sessions dropped) | `packages/shared/src/health/strength-progress.ts` → `strengthGroups(exercises, mode)`; the web `strength-progress-card.tsx` and the native Progress panel (re-exported from `progressPresentation.ts`) both call it |
| Which points of a series get a dot (the latest, and any with no neighbour) | `packages/shared/src/health/series-gaps.ts` → `standaloneOrLatest(values)`; read by the web `gapDataset.pointRadius` and the native `dotMask` |
| Leading empty days of a day-by-day series | `packages/shared/src/health/series-gaps.ts` → `trimLeadingGaps(rows, read)`; `null` when no day has a reading. The only definition: `lib/__tests__/shared-trim-and-capitalize-only-definitions.test.ts` fails on an inline `findIndex(… != null)` trim |
| The generic muscle-volume band's constants and legend words (`GENERIC_HIGH`, `MAX_TARGET`, `GENERIC_LEGEND`; its "approaching" floor is read off `targetBand`) and first-letter `capitalize` (every screen calls it; the same test fails on an inline `charAt(0).toUpperCase()` / `[0].toUpperCase()` copy) | `packages/shared/src/health/volume-band.ts`, `packages/shared/src/format/capitalize.ts` |

Sign-out and account-switch clearing is `accountScopeDecision` in `status/sync-decisions.ts`, applied by `WorkoutStatusAccountGuard` above the remounting screens. | Test files are typechecked (LB-37) | `tsconfig.tests.json` (extends `tsconfig.json`, drops only the `**/__tests__/**` exclusion) + `scripts/check-test-typecheck.js` + `scripts/test-typecheck-baseline.json`. **Shrink-only per-file baseline, 320 errors across 90 files at 2026-09-01**; a file not listed must have zero, and a row for a file that is now clean must be deleted or the list rots into an allowlist. `pnpm typecheck:tests` locally (`ci:local` includes it); the CI step is in the **Build** job, **not Custom Rules** — that job has no `setup-node` and no `pnpm install`, which is what keeps it at ~25 s, and this check needs `tsc`. **Do not drop the exclusion from `tsconfig.json` itself**: that is the project `next build` reads, and it would put 320 errors in front of Build. |
| **Data-quality review charter** | [`docs/data-quality-review-charter.md`](data-quality-review-charter.md) — self-contained brief for a standing session that hunts wrong *numbers* (not wrong code) using the read-only prod endpoint: access + limits, the triangulation method, codebase-specific hazards (renormalising models, centi-°C baselines, frozen Cloud fields), the calculation surfaces worth auditing, findings already logged, and the report/queue conventions. Hand this to a fresh session rather than re-explaining. |
| Read-only prod query access (beta only) | `lib/data/postgres/readonly-client.ts` (isolated `max: 2` pool, never the app's `max: 10`) + `app/api/admin/db-query/route.ts` (POST = one SELECT, GET = schema discovery). Surface is the generated `claude_ro` view schema (`lib/data/postgres/claude-ro-views.sql`, written by `scripts/generate-claude-ro-views.js` and applied by `ensureSchema` after the migrations whenever its hash changes — BF-214; it was a numbered migration until then) — **default-deny**; the columns it withholds are NOT listed here (a hand-kept list drifted, issue 2641) — read them from `SELECT * FROM claude_ro._meta_withheld_columns` or the `DENY` object in the generator, and `scripts/check-withheld-columns-docs.js` fails the build if this row lists them again. Read-only is a Postgres GRANT, proven by a real-DB test (`claude-ro-readonly-role.test.ts`), not by SQL inspection. Audit row per query in `db_query_log` (migration 143). **Re-run the generator, overwriting that file, when adding or dropping a table or column** — `claude-ro-views-file.test.ts` fails otherwise. |
| **The agent action log — what an agent ran, and who approved it (#2381)** | `agent_action_log` (migration `202610071523`, `claude_ro.agent_action_log`) via `startAgentAction(input)` / `finishAgentAction(id, result)` / `getAgentAction(id)` / `listAgentActions(filter)` in `lib/data/postgres/slices/agent-actions.ts`. One row per run: `job` (a stable id from `docs/admin-actions.md`, lowercase `[a-z0-9._:-]`), `parameters`, `actor` (agent name or `owner`), `approval_ref` (link to the owner's approval; the route requires it for destructive jobs, the table only refuses a blank one), `target_user_id` (null = global job), start, end, `outcome` (`running \| succeeded \| failed \| refused`), `affected_rows`, `days_moved`, `error` (first line only, ≤ 2,000 chars). **Append-only, enforced at the database:** trigger `agent_action_log_append_only` refuses every DELETE, any change to a finished row and any change to a running row beyond its finishing columns; `finishAgentAction` also matches only `outcome = 'running'`, so a second finish returns null. The trigger's one exception is the account-deletion FK: `target_user_id` is `ON DELETE SET NULL` (never CASCADE), so the record outlives the account, unlinked. **`parameters` never holds a secret:** `startAgentAction` runs `redactActionParameters` (`lib/agent-actions/redact.ts`) inside the write, dropping every key naming a secret, token, key, password, authorization, cookie, credential, bearer or signature at any depth; personal data stays out by the writer's discipline (ids, dates, table names, flags). claude_ro shows the owner's runs and the global ones, never a run on another account. Server-only, not exported (ops). Written by the agent key (below). |
| **The agent key — an agent runs an allow-listed job itself (issue 2381)** | `POST /api/agent-actions` (+ `GET` to poll a slot job). The ONE reader of `AGENT_ACTIONS_SECRET` is `authorizeAgentRequest(req)` in `lib/agent-actions/guard.ts` (synchronous, no DB: per-IP rate limit, then `safeCompare`; unset or < 32 chars refuses all; never the session cookie). The allow-list and its strict body schema are `AGENT_JOBS` / `AgentActionRequest` in `lib/agent-actions/jobs.ts`; each job calls the code its admin route runs and never carries its own: `rederiveBodyBattery` (`lib/health/rederive-body-battery.ts`), `tagBaselineSessions` (`lib/admin/baseline-phase-tag.mjs`), `startStressBackfillJob` (`lib/oura-ble/stress-backfill-job.ts`). Owner account only (`ADMIN_EXPORT_USER_ID` ?? `WEBHOOK_USER_ID`, must be admin, must be named in `targetUserId`). A data-overwriting run needs `approval` (a comment link). Catalogue and call shape: `docs/admin-actions.md` section 12. Never add a job that touches the ring key, the raw archive, users, money or AI generation. |
| Admin DB snapshot (Q-530) | `lib/export/db-snapshot.ts` (drift gate, PK discovery, keyset pagination, `bulk=<days>` window resolution) + `app/api/admin/db-snapshot/route.ts` (NDJSON stream, auth mirrors `day-review`) + `scripts/local-db/snapshot.js` (`pnpm db:snapshot`, the restore round-trip). Reads the SAME `claude_ro` schema the read-only query endpoint reads — no second scoping map, so nothing to drift. The four bulk tables (`oura_raw_samples`, `oura_heartrate`, `rr_intervals`, `error_events`) are excluded by default (`bulk=0`); `bulk=all` or `bulk=<days>` opts them in. Every table pages on its primary key, so a table needs one: `oura_heartrate` pages on `(user_id, timestamp)` since #2079 re-keyed it off the never-scanned `id`. **The keyset cursor is Postgres's own `::text` of each key column (`__pk0`…, stripped before a row is emitted), never the driver value** — a `timestamptz` arrives as a millisecond `Date` and a cursor rebuilt from it re-returned a microsecond-keyed row on the next page (issue 2645); a key with sub-millisecond digits is also emitted as a microsecond ISO string so a restore cannot collapse two of them. `ADMIN_SNAPSHOT_SECRET` is a separate secret from `ADMIN_EXPORT_SECRET` — this returns the whole database, that returns derived scores. The restore script's local-target guard is regex-based, not `new URL()` — the Unix-socket `DATABASE_URL` form (`postgresql://user:pass@/db?host=/tmp&port=5433`) has an empty host component the WHATWG URL parser rejects outright, which would have made the one guard standing between a mistaken run and TRUNCATEing a real database throw a confusing error on the sandbox's own default connection string. |
| Migration runners (the two of them) | `ensureSchema()` in `lib/data/postgres/client.ts` (every boot, every vitest worker) and `scripts/local-db/migrate.js` (local setup + the CI **Migration Check** job). They must agree about what a failure is: the SQLSTATEs meaning "already there" live in `lib/data/postgres/idempotent-sqlstates.json` and **both read that file** — never re-inline a copy, a test fails on one. Neither records a migration that failed idempotently, deliberately: the collision fires on the first statement and the rest may not have run. |
| Rebuild a local database from migrations (OR-194) | `pnpm db:rebuild` → `scripts/local-db/rebuild.js`: drops and recreates the database named by `LOCAL_DATABASE_URL`, runs `migrate.js`, loads `seed.sql`. Cross-platform, refuses non-local hosts, never touches `.env.local`. Run it at the start of a **local** agent session; procedure in `docs/local-agent-environment.md`. |
| Content-Security-Policy | `lib/security/csp.ts` → `buildCsp(isDev)` — the ONE definition, imported by `next.config.ts`. It was inline in that config until 2026-08-20, where nothing could import it and so nothing tested it (Q-546). Tested in `lib/security/__tests__/csp.test.ts`; change the policy there, never in the config. |
| Constant-time secret compare | `lib/security/constant-time.ts` → `safeCompare(a,b)` — the ONE implementation; used by `health-connect/ingest` and `admin/day-review`. Never compare a shared secret/bearer token with `===`. |
| **Is a self-reported scale an ANSWER?** | `packages/shared/src/health/self-report.ts` → `answeredMorningScales(row)` (TN-57). The morning sheet seeds `perceivedRecovery`/`sleepQualityFeel` from a neutral constant and records whether the lifter moved each one; **the value alone cannot tell a self-report from the seed, and the seeded value is 3, which is also a legitimate answer.** Every reader of those two columns goes through this — `admin/battery-recovery-calibration`, `admin/sleep-feel-calibration`, `health-trends` subjective-recovery, `score-audit/build-day-audit`, `ai-periodization/signals`, and since LA-136 **Home's sleep line** (`lib/hooks/use-morning-sleep-feel.ts`) — the first USER-FACING reader, where the untouched seed would print as *"OK · 3/5 · Your rating, not a score"* and re-create the fabricated `Sleep: OK` that entry removed. Measured 2026-09-22: 78 of the owner's 97 rows carried a value and **0** were ever touched. **On the two write paths it runs AFTER `dayCheckinHasAnswers` (Q-465), never before** — nulling first makes an untouched save carry no answers, which is a 400 on the web route and a no-retry poison pill in `pushMutations`. |
| **What the morning check-in WRITES for sleep, and whose answer it is** | `packages/shared/src/checkin/save-sleep-value.ts` → `saveSleepValue(correction, verdict)` (TN-82), with `verdictToStoredFeel()` in `packages/shared/src/health/sleep-verdict-copy.ts`. The sheet no longer ASKS — it announces the night's verdict and takes a one-tap correction — so this is the one place deciding the written value and its `touched` flag. **Only a correction is touched**; an auto-fill that flagged itself would destroy the variable and re-create TN-57. ⚠ **It must also always return a NUMBER:** `dayCheckinHasAnswers` (row-neighbour above) rejects a body with no answer — a 400, or a no-retry poison pill in `pushMutations` — and it never fired before only because both writers seeded from `NEUTRAL_SCALES`, which TN-82 deleted. Not writing a row is not an escape either: auto-open re-prompts until one exists. |
| **What he said about last night's sleep, on a screen** | `lib/hooks/use-morning-sleep-feel.ts` → `useMorningSleepFeel()`, rendered by `components/home/sleep-feel-line.tsx` (LA-136). The stored→label/dot mapping is split into `lib/hooks/morning-sleep-feel-scale.ts` → `sleepFeelFromStored(stored)` so it is **React-free and unit-testable by calling it** — the scale is stored 1 = great … 5 = terrible while its labels run the other way, and no source-matching guard can tell `6 - stored` from `stored`. Reads through `answeredMorningScales` (row above). **LOCAL-FIRST** — the morning sheet writes `upsertDayCheckin` + `queueMutation`, so an offline rating exists only on the device until the outbox drains and a server read would show nothing; `useCachedValue` cannot own that, so it uses `useInvalidationRefetch` + a `readCacheSync` seed. The key is `day-checkin:<date>:morning` — the `:morning` suffix matters because `day-checkin:<date>` is already the EVENING payload's key, and riding the shared `day-checkin:` prefix is what puts it in every group that clears it, whether or not the push succeeded. |
| Score bands (readiness etc.) | `packages/shared/src/health/score-band.ts` → `scoreBand(score)`, `scoreBandByLabel(label)` and the `SCORE_BAND_COLOR` map. **Returns theme tokens, not hex (RV-99)** — the same good/warning/bad triad existed twice, and in dark the hex green `rgb(34,197,94)` and the token `rgb(86,238,102)` are different colours rather than shades; `recovery-band.ts` and `body-battery-band.ts` agree with it now. Always render the band's label/icon, not colour alone — `components/health/score-band-legend.tsx` is the shared three-swatch key. **Two traps when passing one of these colours anywhere:** a canvas resolves no `var()` and silently paints black, so a Chart.js caller must go through `resolveColor()` (`packages/shared/src/chart-colors.ts`); and `accentCardStyle()` has a **hex** branch and a **token** branch that produce genuinely different colours (`rgba()` from parsed components vs `color-mix(in oklch)`) — it used to drop the gradient and border entirely for a non-hex input with no error. |
| Body Battery, one day | `lib/health/body-battery-day.ts` → `computeBodyBatteryDay({ repo, userId, tz, date, until, computeReadinessIfMissing })` and `BODY_BATTERY_MODEL_VERSION`. The live `/api/body-battery` asks for today up to now; `rederiveBodyBattery` (`lib/health/rederive-body-battery.ts`, behind `POST /api/admin/rederive-body-battery` and the agent key) asks for a finished day, midnight to midnight, keeping its frozen anchor (TN-72). The day's wake is the end of `nightWokenFrom` (`sleep-night.ts`), never an evening window that opens the following night (#2230). The constants live here and nowhere else, `WAKING_REST_OFFSET_BPM` (the v7 charge ceiling, resting HR + 9 bpm, issue 2235) among them, and the walk itself is `walkBodyBattery` in `packages/shared/src/health/body-battery-walk.ts`, beside `restThresholdFromOffset(offsetBpm, reserve)`, the one place the bpm offset becomes the reserve fraction the walk takes (the tuning replay `scripts/tuning/body-battery-replay.cjs` bundles that same function). The anchor rule is `lib/health/body-battery-anchor.ts`. |
| **Activity score, one day (#2435)** | `packages/shared/src/health/activity-day.ts` → `scoreActivityForDay(input)` (pure; every window bounded at the day's end), `restingHrBaseline(bodyMetrics, ouraByDate)`, `activityContributorsRow`, `activityFinalisedAt`, `finalisedPreTaper`; `activity-day-load.ts` → `loadActivityDay(repo, userId, tz, date)` (read-only); `lib/health/activity-finalise.ts` → `finaliseActivityDay` + `ACTIVITY_FINALISE_DAYS`. `buildReadinessPayload` scores today with it **live-only**; a finished day is stored once, stamped `activity_contributors.finalisedAt`, from the payload's 7-day look-back (`staleFinishedDays`, `packages/shared/src/health/finished-day-recheck.ts`, shared with the training-load re-check #2400) | **Never persist today's Activity score**: it is partial until local midnight, and storing it on read is the bug. | The Activity score's rolling strength window (#2739) | `packages/shared/src/health/activity-score.ts` → `strengthWindowEndingAt(sessions, dayStr, tz)`: **7 local days, the day itself included** (from local midnight six days before it), the ONE source of `sessions7d` / `volume7dKg`. `scoreActivityForDay`, readiness's prev-day contributor, the day audit and the AI insight all call it; it used to start seven days back (eight days) and the insight anchored on UTC midnight. Goals (5 sessions, 26,000 kg) were calibrated on calendar weeks. |

Both numbers were set by backtesting the walk over the real production HR series, not reasoned — see `docs/body-battery-tuning.md`. |
| **Body Battery walk (the charge/drain integration)** | `packages/shared/src/health/body-battery-walk.ts` → `walkBodyBattery(samples, params)`. The per-sample arithmetic `/api/body-battery` runs from wake to now: charge below the rest threshold, drain above it, daytime stress on top, gap-hold and per-sample dt cap. Extracted from the route in TN-2's enabling refactor — it was welded into a ~200-line function doing eight DB reads, so the arithmetic could not be driven without a database and a calibration could only be replayed in SQL. **The constants stay declared in the route and are passed in**, so the route remains the one place they are chosen and this cannot drift from them. `restThreshold` is a reserve *fraction*: TN-2's explicit bpm offset becomes `offsetBpm / reserve`, which is arithmetic-only — `__tests__/body-battery-walk.test.ts` pins that substitution and that it is immune to `hrMax` re-estimation, which the fraction form is not. |
| **Score model specs (weights / curves / thresholds, serialisable)** | `SLEEP_MODEL` (`packages/shared/src/health/sleep-score.ts`), `ACTIVITY_MODEL` (`packages/shared/src/health/activity-score.ts`), `READINESS_MODEL` (`packages/shared/src/health/readiness-composite.ts`) — read-only exports of each model's own constants so tooling can present a score next to the model that produced it **without copying any of it**. Shared gates that used to be inline in `app/api/readiness-score/route.ts` now live with their model: `SLEEP_HRV_BASELINE_MIN_NIGHTS`, `CHECKIN_ENERGY_SCORE`/`checkinScoreFromEnergy`. Add new constants here rather than at a call site. |
| **Per-day score audit (admin day review)** | `packages/shared/src/health/score-audit/` → `buildDayAudit({repo,userId,date,tz})` — assembles, for ONE arbitrary local day, every raw signal behind Sleep / Readiness / Activity / Heart Rate plus each contributor's sub-score, effective weight and points contributed, what was missing and why, and the value persisted at the time (`storedMatchesRecompute` flags drift). Calls the same `computeSleepScore`/`computeActivityScore`/`computeReadinessComposite` the app serves from — it gathers inputs for a date, it does **not** re-implement a formula. Served by `GET /api/admin/day-review` — `?date=` for one day, `?from=&to=` for a range (max 31 days, walked **sequentially**: ~12 queries/day vs a `max:10` pool, so a range must never fan out). Auth is an admin session **or** `Authorization: Bearer $ADMIN_EXPORT_SECRET` (fail-closed, still admin-gated — see CLAUDE.md env vars). Surfaced as Admin → Day Review. Use it before re-tuning any score. |
| **Model-vs-self-report calibration (admin)** | `packages/shared/src/health/model-report-calibration.ts` → `buildModelReportCalibration({from,to,modelByDate,ratingByDate,labels,copy})` — THE one answer to “does this model order days the way the owner does?”: Spearman rank correlation, per-rating buckets, model-vs-rating spread, worst-disagreement ranking, and the note rules (agreement band, compression, non-monotonicity). **Rank-based on purpose** — a 1–5 self-report and a 0–100 model score are not commensurable, so a raw difference says nothing. Pure: it never computes the model value it is checking. Two thin adapters: `sleep-feel-calibration.ts` (Sleep Score vs `sleep_quality_feel`, Q-16) and `battery-recovery-calibration.ts` (end-of-day Body Battery vs `perceived_recovery`, Q-79 — paired **same-date**; the next-morning lag was measured and finds nothing). Routes `GET /api/admin/{sleep-feel,battery-recovery}-calibration`, one shared panel `components/admin/calibration-card.tsx`, both under Admin → Day Review. Rating labels come from `storedOrderLabels(key)` (`types/day-checkin.ts`) — `MORNING_SCALES.labels` is screen order (worst → best) and the column stores 6 − position, so never restate a reversed copy. |

Two secondaries per session is the owner's normal (10 of his 22 sessions), which is why only primaries are capped. |

The TIME model is deliberately NOT aligned — `transitionSecForEquipment([])` still charges the barbell worst case, which is LA-65's to settle. |

Both are written by `components/workout-screen.tsx`: `interExerciseRestSec` runs from the previous exercise's last set end to the first Start press, and `prepSecRef` is computed inside that same `handleStart` from the ready-screen baseline, so it is the tail of the same interval. Verified against the independent `set_start_ms`/`set_end_ms` clock over 171 transitions: `inter` alone matches to a median **0.05 s**, `inter + prep` overshoots by **136 s**. The first exercise of a session has no `inter` (nothing precedes it) and its `prep` — mean 286 s, the warm-up ramp — is the right actual there. Consumers: `packages/shared/src/workout/time-audit.ts` (uses `inter` alone, correct) and `app/api/workout-sessions/[id]/timing/route.ts` (`inter ?? prep`; used to read `prep` alone and compare it against a constant modelling the whole transition). |

The consequence the decision leaves behind is that nothing said which set to use: it is lucide, and phosphor is frozen to those six files. Re-runs once when a shard exits non-zero carrying `Closing rpc while "onUserConsoleLog" was pending` **and** reports zero failing tests and zero failing files — upstream [vitest#11153](https://github.com/vitest-dev/vitest/issues/11153), open, present in 4.1.11 and 5.0.0 alike. **It cannot turn a red green:** a false positive costs one extra run, because a real failure recurs on the retry, and a second occurrence in the same job fails it. Every retry prints a greppable `[vitest-retry]` notice so sightings stay counted. **Do not generalise this into a blanket CI retry** — the narrowness is the whole safety argument. |

Freshness comes from the rollup running on every BLE ingest, not from a write-through read. **LA-112: the series excludes recorded sleep, and the filter sits BEFORE `scoreStressPoints`** — that function calibrates on the day-MEDIAN dhrv across whatever it is handed, and a sleeping bucket's low heart rate means a high imputed dhrv, so leaving sleep in raises the median and every waking bucket is scored below baseline. Filtering the summary instead would drop the sleeping buckets from the count and leave the waking ones mis-scored. `sleepWindows` is a REQUIRED parameter with no default, because `= []` reads as "no sleep here" at a call site that forgot. The rollup gets its windows from `RollupIO.readSleepWindows`, never from the pass's own `sleepRows` — those cover only the nights that pass reconstructed, which would make a day's stress depend on how wide the pass was — and filters on every window overlapping the day, since sleep rows are keyed by WAKE date and the night starting tonight is filed under tomorrow. **The SQL column is `bucket_mid` and holds the bucket MIDPOINT** (`t = bStart + bucketMs/2`). It was `bucket_start` until LA-114 renamed it on 2026-09-28. A join on the :00/:30 grid must subtract 15 minutes, because it returns ZERO rows otherwise, which reads as missing data. |

Admin session, or the agent key's `stress-backfill` job (owner decision on issue 2381, 2026-10-08). Depth is bounded by the packed raw tier and by which nights have a summary row. |
| Correcting a scale's body fat against a DEXA (BF-2) | `packages/shared/src/health/body-fat-calibration.ts` → `pairScansWithReadings`, `deriveBodyFatCalibration`, `correctBodyFatPct`, `PAIR_WINDOW_DAYS` (3), `DEFAULT_CALIBRATED_SOURCE` (`scale_ble`); read via `repo.getBodyFatCalibration(userId, source?)`. **The pairs are DERIVED, never stored** — `dexa_scans` joined to `body_metrics` in TS, keyed by `source_map->>'body_fat_pct'` — because a stored pair is a stored counter and would drift the moment either side were re-entered; a second scan becomes a second pair with no entry step, and there is **no table and no migration**. **Offset, not ratio**: at n=1 they agree on the observed point and diverge everywhere else, and only the offset makes no claim about readings never observed. **Per instrument, never global** — a `null` or foreign source is left alone, which is two-thirds of the owner's history (provenance predates `source_map`), so `corrected` is carried **per reading** rather than per response and a chart must mark where the calibrated span starts. A calibration with no pairs is `null`, never a zero offset: "not corrected" and "corrected by 0.0" are different claims. A correction leaving `PLAUSIBLE_BODY_FAT_PCT` is refused. **Step 3 IS built** — the correction is applied **per consumer**, never inside `listBodyMetrics`, and `scripts/check-body-fat-correction.js` (Custom Rules) fails CI on a consumer that forgets. **The reason it cannot go in the shared read is a laundering path, not a preference:** the health screen seeds its body-fat log field from `/api/body-metadata`, which POSTs the value straight back at source `manual` — a rank that OUTRANKS `scale_ble` — so a corrected value returned there would let the user overwrite their own raw reading by saving an untouched field, and collapse the next calibration toward zero. Corrected: `energy-balance-service`, `nutrition-goals/recommend` (which is how it reaches `personalRmr`), and `persistBodyCompFromMetrics`. Deliberately raw with the reason in the script: `body-metadata` (the edit seed), `day-log` and `health-sections` (display, pending step 4's `corrected` flag), `build-day-audit` (an audit must report what was stored), `progress-summary` (the first-ever reading, which predates `source_map`). `BodyMetrics.bodyFatSource` carries the provenance and is **read-only** — `upsertBodyMetrics` maps an explicit column list, so it cannot be written back. **Step 4 is built:** `/api/body-metadata` and `/api/day-log` carry `bodyFatCorrected` + `bodyFatIsCorrected` per reading, and `body-metadata` adds `bodyFatCalibration: { offsetPct, pairCount, source } | null`. **`bodyFat` stays raw and is what the log sheet seeds from** — the three fields are separate on purpose, and `bodyFatIsCorrected` is not `corrected !== raw` because an offset can round to zero. **LA-45 shipped 2026-09-01 and the screens read it through ONE rule:** `packages/shared/src/health/body-fat-display.ts` (re-exported by `components/health/body-fat-display.ts`) → `displayBodyFat` (corrected ?? raw), `isCorrectedReading` (never inferred from the values differing), `correctedSpan` (how much of a window is corrected, for the mixed-instrument boundary), `latestDisplayedBodyFat` (the newest reading with a value to show **and whether that row was corrected** — the two together, because `.map(displayBodyFat).find(...)` gets the number right and loses the row, which is what left Health's BMI band unable to say it rested on a calibrated figure, BF-113). Seven surfaces use it; `check-body-fat-correction.js` counts importing it as handling the correction, alongside the calibration module itself. `components/health/__tests__/body-fat-display-sites.test.ts` pins every site **and the inverse** — the log sheet must still seed the raw value. |

| Ring-clock ↔ wall-clock conversion | `lib/oura-ble/clock.ts`. **A FIXED 100 ms/ds slope with one robust offset per epoch — it does NOT interpolate, and that is load-bearing.** The slope bracket interpolation derives, `Δutc / Δds`, is not a property of either clock: while the ring drains buffered history ds outruns the wall clock and the ratio collapses (Q-139 measured 17,094 ds in 95 s, which put 1,555 steps in one 60 s block; a later nine-night run shifted every night 10–48 min later). **Do not re-attempt interpolation — three measurements now say the same thing.** The offset is the 10th-percentile *lag* (`LAG_PERCENTILE`), so one stray anchor cannot define an epoch. `resolveDsToMs(ds, anchors, epoch?)` / `resolveMsToDs(...)` take the anchor series; **`dsToMs` / `msToDs` take a `ClockOffsets` instead and are what a caller that merely converts timestamps should use** (RV-182 ②) — `repo.getOuraClockOffsets(userId)` computes the per-epoch order statistic in SQL, one row, where `repo.getOuraClockAnchors` returned 12,582 and was 9.4% of all database time. Both directions go through one `offsetForEpoch`, so `msToDs` is `dsToMs` solved for `ds` (LA-141 — it used to interpolate while claiming symmetry, and round-tripped 26.9 min of ring time out). Anchors stay append-only observations grouped by `epoch`; never resolve across an epoch, return `null` rather than guess. Deliberately no `epochForDs`: after a reset the counter restarts low, so a bare ds is ambiguous — rows carry their own `epoch`. |

What exists is the library port in the row below — `lib/oura-models/steps-motion-decoder.ts` → `runStepsMotionDecoder(input)`, golden-verified but explicitly **not yet wired** into the BLE motion-frame decode or the step-counter pipeline. This row describes that wiring, i.e. planned work. Original intent, retained: a byte-exact port of Oura `steps_motion_decoder` (dequantizes the 27 `unpack27` columns into `strideFrequency` Hz etc. via vendored constants; reshapes to 3×30-s sub-windows). Pure codec; column-order-vs-`data_columns` is validated on-device (Sub-plan D-2) before feeding the step count. |

The ONE place these three are defined — extracted out of `aggregateOuraRawSamples` on 2026-08-03 so the on-device rollup (Q-29 / D2 Task 5) calls it instead of re-deriving. Pins three rules a re-derivation gets wrong: HRV is a gated **median of the ring's `0x5d` rmssd_ms** (never a mean, never an IBI recompute); resting HR is the **lowest 5-min BIN AVERAGE** (never a per-beat min — the decoder's 2000 ms cap makes one missed beat look like exactly 30 bpm); and **one MET exclusion feeds both**. `nightlyHeartRate` also returns its bins, because the Recovery Index needs that exact series. Equivalence to the pre-extraction code is pinned by a fuzzed oracle (`__tests__/night-vitals-extraction-oracle.test.ts`, 400 randomised nights vs a frozen copy) — delete that oracle in the same PR as any deliberate change to these definitions |

Density-gated (≥90 beats/≥4 min → nulls, neutral). Wired into `sleep-staging.ts` as the `lfhf`/`W_LFHF` REM term (frequency-domain, independent of the time-domain RMSSD/HR-spread/breathing terms; high ⇒ REM, self-neutralising on sparse nights) and computed per-epoch in `aggregateOuraRawSamples`. Visible in the `/admin/oura-ble` epoch debug dump (`lfhf` column) for `W_LFHF` tuning. Heuristic lever, not an Oura-parity claim. |
| Is a night still filling in? (BF-83) | `lib/sleep/provisional.ts` → `isNightProvisional(sleepEndMs, coverageEndMs)` + `PROVISIONAL_COVERAGE_MARGIN_MS`, fed by `repo.getSleepCoverageEnd(userId)` (the rollup watermark resolved through the clock anchors). `/api/sleep-sessions` returns `provisional` per row, and only for a night the ring's rollup derived (`isRingRollupNight`, `oura_id` `ble:…`): an Oura Cloud, Health Connect or typed-in night is final as stored, because the watermark stops moving once the ring's data comes from the Cloud feed (#2895). **Measure the ROLLUP's reach, never the raw table's** — the batch covering a missing 82 minutes was ingested two minutes before the screenshot that showed the night short, so the row was stale rather than the data absent, and `max(oura_raw_samples.measured_at)` would have called it settled. The margin is the sensing-span bridge gap, imported rather than restated: while coverage sits within one bridgeable gap of the end, a run arriving in the next drain still extends the night. Derived per request — a stored flag goes stale exactly when it matters, because what it describes changes without the row changing. |
| Sleep trend (recent-3-vs-baseline) | `packages/shared/src/health/sleep-trend.ts` → `sleepDurationTrend(sessions, tz)` + `sleepScoreTrend(sessions, tz)` — THE only recent-3/older-7 ratio for sleep. Duration and quality (over `computeSleepScore`) variants; **both** run their input through `nightSessions` first, so naps and split nights can't enter the window, and a row with no duration is skipped rather than counted as 0 hours (Q-76 — the duration variant carried that "legacy parity" behaviour until v1.261.0 and it manufactured the deficit it was meant to detect). Imported by periodization signals + `adapter.ts:getNextSession` (One Formula, One Place — replaced two inline copies). |
| VO₂max derivation | `packages/shared/src/health/vo2max.ts` → `deriveVo2Max(inputs)` — the ONE VO₂max estimate. Uth–Sørensen (`15.3×HRmax/HRrest`) primary, Jackson non-exercise (BMI) fallback when RHR missing; clamped `[10,100]`. Import, never re-derive. |
| Training Stress Score (OTS) | `lib/oura-models/inference/ots.ts` → `runTrainingStressScore(input)` — faithful TS port of the algorithmic TorchScript model `training_stress_score_0_2_1` (0 params), **golden-verified** vs the captured `.pt` vector (`goldens/`, fixture in `onnx/__fixtures__/…golden.json`). Constants via `getOtsTypedConstants()`. Assembly `packages/shared/src/health/training-stress.ts` → `computeTrainingStress(...)` reads derived readiness + derived VO₂max + the day's `MET/min` (resolved through the input cascade, #2115 v4) and gates (never fabricates) while readiness learns. A minute nothing measured is capped and renormalised by `lib/oura-models/inference/ots-gap-fill.ts` → `fillOtsGaps` (≤ 20% of the recency-weighted last 720 minutes empty, else `insufficient_met`; the rest filled so the weighted mean is the measured one), so the vendored `ots.ts` stays untouched; served by `GET /api/training-stress`, persisted to `oura_daily_derived.training_load_ots/high`. |
| Stress-resilience | `lib/health/stress-resilience.ts` → `runStressResilience(input)` — faithful TS port of the algorithmic TorchScript model `stress_resilience_2_2_1` (0 params), **golden-verified** vs the captured `.pt` vector (`onnx/__fixtures__/…golden.json`). Constants via `getResilienceConstants()`. Orchestrator `computeResilienceForDay(day, priorIndices)` assembles the model input from a day's sleep-score/readiness contributors + the daytime-stress series (limits from `daytimeStressScalingParams` in `daytime-stress.ts`) + the trailing 14-day persisted daily-index window; a provisional (learning) contributor yields no daily index rather than a fabricated one. The rollup (`aggregateOuraRawSamples` `resilience` step) computes + persists per-night indices/level to `oura_daily_derived.resilience_*`; `GET /api/readiness-score` returns the latest resolved level as `ownResilienceLevel/Band/Confidence`, surfaced by `components/health/resilience-tile.tsx`. |
| BDI (breathing-disturbance index) | `lib/oura-models/sleepnet-assemble.ts` → `bdiFromApnea(apneaWin, codes)` — THE only BDI math (disturbed asleep-epochs per hour of sleep; apnea flags on awake epochs dropped). A free byproduct of the moonstone SleepNet staging pass — `sleepNetStages5Min` returns `{ stages, bdi }`, the rollup (`aggregateOuraRawSamples` `bdi_derived` step) persists `bdi.perHour` to `oura_daily_derived.bdi_derived` (null on heuristic-fallback nights). Observational, NOT a diagnosis. Read via `getOuraDailyDerived().bdiDerived`; user-facing display deferred until on-device validation. The frozen Cloud `oura_daily.breathing_disturbance_index` is never reused. |
| HR zone-minutes + Session Load | `packages/shared/src/health/zone-minutes.ts` → `accumulateZoneSeconds`/`edwardsTrimp`/`zoneBreakdownFromReadings` — THE time-in-HR-zone primitive (zones from `hr-zones.ts` only; never re-band). Edwards-TRIMP = "Session Load" (per-workout, distinct from whole-day "Training Stress (OTS)"). Per-workout view is client-side derive-on-read (`components/health/zone-breakdown.tsx`, mounted in the activity detail sheet + workout done-screen). Day/week/month stats read the reconcile-on-read rollup cache `daily_zone_minutes` (mig 129) via `getZoneMinutesRange` (`slices/oura.ts`) → `GET /api/zone-minutes` → `components/health/time-in-zone-card.tsx`. Server-derived cache, not synced (absent from RECONCILE_TABLES). |
| **Activity's intraday-HR contributors, and when a heart-rate source counts** (#2437) | `packages/shared/src/health/intraday-hr-contributors.ts` → `intradayHrContributors({ hrRows, maxHr, restingHr, tz, dateIso })` returns zone minutes, move-every-hour and its goal for one day; the live payload (`lib/health/readiness-payload.ts`) and the day audit (`score-audit/build-day-audit.ts`) both call it. `dropSparseHrHours(rows, tz)` is the density rule (decided 2026-10-06, "missing is missing, never zero"): a source in `DENSITY_GATED_HR_SOURCES` (Health Connect, Apple Health) counts in a local hour only with ≥2 readings and no gap over `HR_DENSE_MAX_GAP_MS` (the ring's 5 min, +30 s) across the worn part; a sparse hour's readings drop out and, for move-every-hour, the hour leaves the goal. With no dense HR left both contributors are null and the score renormalises. The ring and strap are never gated, so the owner's days do not move. Oura Cloud is not gated either, although its series is not the dense BLE stream; how these lanes should read an Oura-only day is the owner's call (#3114). `computeDayZoneSeconds` (the zone-minutes and cardio trends) applies the same rule. |
| Step counter | `lib/oura-models/inference/step-counter.ts` → `runStepCounter(input)` — faithful TS port of `step_counter_1_3_0`: a hybrid model with algorithmic preprocessing (column select + nearest-timestamp merge of the two motion streams) and postprocessing (interval resample) around a neural core exported to ONNX (`onnx/step_counter_1_3_0_core.onnx`, bit-exact vs the `.pt`; regen via `scripts/oura-models/export-step-counter-core.py`). **Golden-verified end-to-end** (all 14 window timestamps + steps match the `.pt`). Server-only (onnxruntime-node), returns null on failure. Now **fed real ring data** via `lib/oura-ble/step-counter-pipeline.ts` (see below). |
| Step-counter real-data pipeline | `lib/oura-ble/step-counter-pipeline.ts` → `runStepCounterPipeline(stepFrames, motionFrames, anchor)` — assembles the step models' inputs from stored raw BLE frames and runs them: `0x7e/0x7f` → `pairStepFeatures`/`unpack27` (27 quantized cols) → `runStepsMotionDecoder` (11 physical gait features) → `runStepCounter` (+ motion `0x47`, or a zeroed 8-col stream when absent) → step counts. Converts ring-ds → ms via a shared `measuredAtMs` anchor (diff-invariant). The admin route `GET /api/oura-ble/step-counter-export` and its `StepCounterExportConsole` were retired in #2862. **It was a VALIDATION tool, not a trusted count** — the `unpack27`→`data_columns` order and the 0x47 motion mapping are unconfirmed on-device; trust the golden-verified decoded stride-frequency (~1.5–3 Hz walking) + the Tier-1 gate estimate (`packages/shared/src/health/step-estimate.ts`) as cross-checks. |
| Oura admin device metrics (BLE-derived) | Four compute-on-read ring metrics over stored `oura_raw_samples`, admin-only (no user surface). Pure helpers: `packages/shared/src/health/daytime-hrv.ts` → `daytimeHrvCurve(samples, sleep)` (5-min RMSSD outside sleep — Oura only shows nighttime), `packages/shared/src/health/intraday-temp.ts` → `intradayTempCurve(samples)` + `SKIN_MIN_C=31` (continuous skin-temp vs Oura's single nightly deviation), `packages/shared/src/health/intraday-spo2.ts` → `intradaySpo2Curve(samples)` (continuous SpO₂ % from `0x8b` R via the shared `spo2PctFromR` — Oura shows one nightly average), `completenessForDay(...)` in `packages/shared/src/health/wear-confidence.ts` (worn-bins %, longest gap, last-sample age — One-Formula with the wear-time concept). Assembled by `getOuraRawSamplesForTags(userId, tags, days)` (repo, windowed on `measured_at`) → admin-gated `GET /api/oura-ble/device-metrics` (tz-correct day bucketing via `dateStrMidnightInTz`, sleep excluded via `listSleepSessions`) → `components/oura-ble/device-metrics-panel.tsx` in the `/admin/oura-ble` tester. Server/JS only, no migration/native; back-fills for all stored history. Native battery half of Part A remains device-gated. |
| AWHR profile selector | `lib/oura-models/inference/awhr-profile-selector.ts` → `runAwhrProfileSelector(input)` — faithful TS port of `awhr_profile_selector_1_0_1`: a **neural** hybrid model. Selects columns + nearest-timestamp-merges the ring's stepmotion (11 cols) + motion (8 cols) streams into 19 features, runs an MLP→bidirectional-LSTM→FC core exported to ONNX (`onnx/awhr_profile_selector_1_0_1_core.onnx`, rebuilt from the `.pt` state-dict + bit-exact; regen via `scripts/oura-models/export-awhr-profile-selector-core.py`), then softmax + zero-row NaN-mask + mean-aggregate → a main activity ecore-id and a resampled per-block activity series. Activity id→ecore map `{0:14, 1:5, 2:12}`. **Golden-verified end-to-end** (main id + 7 resampled timestamps + per-block ids match the `.pt`). Server-only (onnxruntime-node), returns null on failure. Library port (not wired into any surface/rollup). |
| Meal timing (window clustering) | `lib/oura-models/meal-timing.ts` → `runMealTiming(unixTimestamps, unixTimezones)` — faithful TS port of the 0-param TorchScript model `meal_timing_0_1_0` (histogram meal logs into 48 half-hour bins → frequent-bin clustering with day wrap-around → `[minutesPastMidnight, durationMinutes]` windows + a consistency flag), **golden-verified** vs the captured `.pt`. Library port (not yet wired into a nutrition surface). |
| Steps motion decoder | `lib/oura-models/steps-motion-decoder.ts` → `runStepsMotionDecoder(input)` — faithful TS port of the 0-param TorchScript model `steps_motion_decoder_2_0_0`. Dequantizes the ring's compact 30 s motion frames (`[N][27]` quantized columns) back into gait features via per-column linear dequant (optionally through a log/sqrt transform, `encode_zero` reserving code 0 for "no value"), then expands each frame into three 10 s sub-rows (`[3N][11]`) with interpolated timestamps (frame gaps outside 25.5–34.5 s treated as 30 s). **Golden-verified** (both outputs bit-exact vs the captured `.pt`). Constants via `getStepsDecoderConstants()`. Library port — NOT yet wired into the ring's motion-frame decode (`lib/oura-ble/decode.ts`) or the step-counter pipeline; feeding real ring frames is the follow-on. Repro oracle: `scripts/oura-models/dump-steps-motion-decoder-intermediates.py`. |
| ASTD event detection | `lib/oura-models/astd-event-detection.ts` → `runAstdEventDetection(input)` — faithful TS port of the 0-param TorchScript model `astd_event_detection_0_1_0`. Detects sustained daytime **stressed** (dsa ≤ −0.5) / **restored** (dsa ≥ 0.5) events from a day's 15-min ASTD bins: thresholds each bin, slides a fixed 4-bin window (accepting on a NaN/other/fully-count rule), then stable-sorts + merges adjacent same-type windows (gap ≤ 30 min). Returns per-type event counts + total minutes + the raw event list. **Golden-verified** vs the captured `.pt` (the golden is the zero-events case; the collect/sort/merge/duration path is pinned against synthetic vectors captured from the same `.pt` — `…__fixtures__/astd_event_detection_0_1_0.scenarios.json`). Constants via `getAstdConstants()`. Validator reduced to a pragmatic gate (empty result on invalid input, never throws). Library port (not wired into any surface/rollup). Oracle + scenario generator: `scripts/oura-models/dump-astd-event-detection-intermediates.py`. |
| Daily short-term baselines | `lib/oura-models/daily-baselines.ts` → `runDailyShortTermBaselines(input)` — faithful TS port of the 0-param TorchScript model `daily_short_term_baselines_1_1_0` (Gaussian-weighted dHRV/skin-temp/HR-min baselines + a physiologically-filtered `torch.median` night-HRV baseline), **golden-verified** vs the captured `.pt` vector. Library port (not yet wired into the readiness/illness baseline path — the app currently uses its own `oura_daily_summary` baselines). Golden→JSON harness: `scripts/oura-models/golden-to-json.py`. |
| Cumulative stress (ChronicStress) | `lib/oura-models/cumulative-stress.ts` → `runCumulativeStress(input)` — faithful TS port of the 0-param TorchScript model `cumulative_stress_1_2_2` (27 inputs → chronic-stress score + 5 signed contributors + 5 UI levels + raw latest-night intermediates + cluster probabilities). Preprocesses ~2 weeks of nightly biometrics (fever-outlier drop, SFI, HRV/IQR/temp-wake normalisation), then a 9-feature factor-analysis → 5-cluster softmin classifier; score = `round(sum(positive-cluster proba)×100)`. **Golden-verified** (all 19 outputs `<1e-3` vs the captured `.pt`). The `§4` pair to stress-resilience. Constants via `getCumulativeStressConstants()`. Uses a true-median `torch_median` + robust `torch_huber` scale (distinct from daily-baselines' lower-middle median — do not share the helper). Library port (NOT wired into any surface/rollup — `chronic_stress_score`/`chronic_stress_contributors` columns exist from migration 123; wiring is the follow-on). Stage-verify oracle: `scripts/oura-models/dump-cumulative-stress-intermediates.py`. |
| Running prescription engine | `packages/shared/src/running/` — deterministic next-run coach (F3 Phase-1 core, no LLM number gates any action). `prescription.ts` → `prescribeNextRun(ctx, gateInputs, frameworkKey)`: a swappable `RunFramework` (`framework.ts` registry; MVP `frameworks/polarized.ts` = Seiler 80/20) proposes the ideal run, then `recovery-gate.ts` → `applyRecoveryGate` downgrades it (proceed→soften→rest) on interference-effect (heavy legs via `lower-body.ts`), low/provisional readiness, high ACWR (`ACWR_THRESHOLDS`), or short sleep. HR bands from `hr-targets.ts` (reuses `computeHrZones` — One Place); `fitness-snapshot.ts` resolves baseline-first with age fallback. **Phase 1 shipped (v1.167.0):** engine (22 tests) + persistence (`running_plans`/`prescribed_runs`, mig 132, SQLite v15) + offline-first `prescribed_run` domain (shared `packages/shared/src/validation/prescribed-run.ts` schema + `updatePrescribedRun` repo fn; `getSyncDelta`/pull mirror) + `GET/POST /api/running-plan`, `PATCH runs/[id]`, `explain` (best-effort Gemini prose) + `/running` UI (`components/running/*`, local-first). Cache: `invalidateRunningPlan()` / `RUNNING_PLAN_TTL`. Phase 2 (volume adaptation + multi-week look-ahead) backlogged. **Choose-your-run (v1.246.0):** `assemble-plan-context.ts` holds the shared `assembleInputs`/`resolveSnapshot`/`resolvePushContext` signal-assembly, imported by both `/api/running-plan` and the new `POST /api/running-plan/override` (`prescribeOverride()` in `prescription.ts` — same gate/retarget pipeline, starting from a user-picked type/duration instead of the framework's own pick). `recommend-run-type.ts` → `recommendRunType(quota)` is the one place the "which run type would help most this week" scoring lives — deterministic, scores each type by open remaining zone-minutes from the same `ZoneQuota` the cardio hub uses; check here before writing a second recommendation heuristic anywhere else in the app. |
| **Did a prescribed run get done AS A RUN?** (LB-179) | `packages/shared/src/running/run-completion.ts` → `completedAsRun(r)`: `status === 'completed'` and `completedAs !== 'walk'`. `prescribed_runs.completed_as` (`'run' | 'walk' | null`, null = before tracking, i.e. a run) is set on every status write and reset by a regenerated prescription. Every planner-side reader (hard-run gate, weekly 80/20 sequence, run-type stats) asks this, never `status === 'completed'` alone. |
| Rest duration prefs | `lib/rest-duration.ts` |
| Fuzzy exercise search | `packages/shared/src/exercise-utils.ts` → `fuzzyScore` |
| Health analytics | `lib/health/` — `correlation.ts`, `strength-progress.ts`, `strength-projection.ts`, `sleep-consistency.ts`, `wear-confidence.ts`, `workout-density.ts`, `hypnogram.ts`, `long-term-goal-progress.ts`, `hr-sleep-band.ts`, `hr-recovery-profile.ts` (peak-HR-band recovery aggregation — cross-workout, intensity-normalised; sources: `set_hr_stats` via `episodeFromSetHrStats` + completed-workout cooldowns via `hr-episode-detection.ts`, served by `/api/health/hr-recovery-profile`), `hr-episode-detection.ts` (`detectWorkoutCooldownEpisode` — one recovery episode per completed `ouraWorkouts` row; within-run interval-rep detection is explicitly NOT implemented, see backlog HRP-2b), `hr-recovery-trend.ts` (`aggregateHrRecoveryTrend` — month-over-month median recovery rate per band), `compute-hr-recovery-profile.ts` (`computeHrRecoveryProfile` — the ONE fetch+detect+aggregate path shared by `/api/health/hr-recovery-profile` and the `getHrRecoveryProfile` AI-chat tool, mirrors `computeWorkoutHr`'s shared-compute pattern), `hr-change-display.ts` (shared ↓/↑ direction-arrow formatter — used by both this and the per-exercise Heart & Recovery card) |
