| Nutrition | `lib/nutrition/` — `log-food.ts`, `log-meal.ts`, `scan-totals.ts`, `adherence.ts`, `tdee-adaptation.ts`, `goal-recommendation.ts`, `day-insight.ts`, `day-checkin-prefill.ts`, `macro-colors.ts` (P/C/F palette — import, don't redefine) |
| **A plan meal's meal type** (LA-172) | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/meal-type-for-time.ts` → `planMealTypeId(meal, mealTypes)`: tag → the window containing `suggestedTime` → the NEAREST window (hours have gaps). Applied on plan create/replace and when an untyped meal's time is edited, never over a stored type. Pure module, safe on the server. `mealTypeForHour` (logging, first-bucket fallback) lives beside it. |
| The calorie bar, drawn | `components/nutrition/calorie-progress-bar.tsx` → `CalorieProgressBar`. **The one place the bar is drawn** — the Nutrition tab, Home's nutrition card and Home's Energy Balance card all render it (Q-401 happened because two surfaces showed two different budgets). Q-323 made it a *progress* bar: x-axis is intake from 0 to `budget + OUTER_KCAL`, notch at the budget. `components/nutrition/calorie-zone-bar.tsx` wraps it with the provenance line. `budgetProvenance()` splits the budget into the rest-day floor and what movement earned; it lives in a `.ts` because vitest cannot parse JSX |
| Marking a day's food log complete | `components/nutrition/food-logging-complete.tsx` → `FoodLoggingComplete`, last element of the Nutrition day scroll. Writes `POST /api/food-logging-complete` (`{date?, complete}`; `complete:false` is the Undo), the **only** writer of `day_checkins.food_logging_completed_at` — the check-in sheets COALESCE it. Gates the maintenance calibration: `estimateMaintenance` counts only flagged days (Q-387). **Native app:** `mobile/src/features/nutrition/components/FoodLoggingComplete.tsx`, after the last meal group; it writes through `NutritionStore.queueHttp('complete', …)`, which queues the same POST in the outbox and reads it back from `/api/day-checkin` (#2898) |
| The finished-logging counter ("N of 10 days marked · N more days to calibrate") | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/calibration-progress.ts` → `calibrationProgress(daysLogged, calibrated, minDays, gapMessage)`, used by both apps' cards. The days-still-needed sum is `loggedDaysRemaining` in `adaptive-tdee.ts`, which `maintenanceGapMessage` also reads. Once enough days are marked but the estimate still declines, it shows the server's `gapMessage` instead of "0 more days" (#2898) |
| Copying a planned meal into My Meals (Q-398) | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/save-plan-meal.ts` → `savePlanMealToLibrary` (one meal) and `savePlanMealsToLibrary` (a batch, which also stamps each result onto `meal_plan_meals.saved_meal_id`). **The one plan→meal copy path** — the plan card and the setup sheet's "meals you already eat" ticks both call it; the setup sheet used to have its own, which created food items with a bare POST and stamped nothing, so a meal ticked there and saved again from the card produced two copies. Per-ingredient conversion is `ingredientToEntry` from `log-plan-meal.ts`, exported rather than copied, so a meal saved and the same meal logged mint identical food items. **Idempotence is the existing column, not a new key** — it survives a regenerate, and the FK's `ON DELETE SET NULL` brings the offer back if the saved meal is deleted. Provenance (`From plan` in My Meals) is derived by `lib/hooks/use-plan-saved-meal-ids.ts`, never stored |
| **The day's calorie budget — ONE function (#2071)** | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/calorie-budget.ts` → `calorieBudget({rmrKcal, deficitKcal, movementKcal, stepCreditKcal})` and `goalDeficitKcal({goal, currentWeightKg, targetWeightKg})`, `GOAL_RATE_PCT_PER_WEEK`, `GOAL_EASE_BAND_KG`. The owner's spec (2026-10-05, answers 2026-10-07): **RMR − deficit + daily living + movement (`computeActiveEnergy().total`)**, floored at max(RMR, 1,200). **Daily living** is RMR × (`SEDENTARY_MULTIPLIER` − 1) LESS `stepEnergyKcal(profile, STEP_BASE_CREDIT)` — the same per-user credit the formula resting base takes — because movement counts steps from the first one ("only steps above ~3,000 count"). The deficit is derived, never typed: recomp 0.3%, cut 0.5%, bulk −0.25% (a surplus) of the **smoothed 30-day trend weight** a week (÷7, ×7,700), easing linearly inside 2 kg of `target_weight_kg` to 0 at the goal for every goal; no goal weight → 0. The trend weight is `computeTrendWeightKg` (`health/long-term-goal-progress.ts`): the Body screen's Weight Trend fit (`computeWeightRateFit`, `weightTrendWindowStart`) read at its latest weigh-in, same three-reading floor; with no trend the latest weigh-in is used (`deficitWeightSource`). `computeEnergyBalance` rounds the deficit once and puts `deficitKcal`, `stepCreditKcal`, `budgetKcal`, `deficitWeightKg`/`deficitWeightSource` on the balance; **every client reads the budget as `budgetProvenance(balance).total`** (`calorie-balance.ts`), which calls this. The typed `calorie_goal` is never shown as a budget — no surface falls back to it, with ONE exception that is itself inside this function (issue 2622): **the user's own target.** `calorieBudget({..., ownTargetKcal})` returns `ownTarget ?? derived` (`ownTarget`, `workedOutKcal`, `workedOutStillDayKcal` say which and what the other would be); the target is NOT floored (the write path bounds it to 800–10,000 kcal with `ownCalorieTargetReason` in `validation/plausibility.ts`). It is stored in the existing `users.calorie_goal` flagged by `calorie_goal_type = 'own'` (no migration; `daily`/`weekly`/null rows are retired typed goals and drive nothing) and read in ONE place, `ownTargetFromGoals(goals)`; the service puts `ownTargetKcal` on the balance, `budgetProvenance` carries `ownTarget`/`workedOutTotal`, and every surface (Nutrition, Home, log-food sheet, end of day, digest, coach, meal plans via `budgetProvenance(...).base`) reads it from there. Written by Profile → Goals (`components/profile/calorie-budget-section.tsx` → the goals route `app/api/user/goals/route.ts` with `{calorieGoal, calorieGoalType: 'own'}`; clear = both null) and by the coach's `user_goals` `calorieGoal` change (`lib/coach/domains/goals.ts`). Contract test: `lib/__tests__/one-calorie-budget-everywhere-2071.test.ts` |
| Calories in vs out (zones + net) | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/calorie-balance.ts` → `computeCalorieBalance()`, `balanceZone()`, `barProgress()` (which replaced `barBands()`/`barPosition()` in Q-323 — those are deleted). **The one place that decides what "on target" means.** Bands measure deviation from the *goal's* target net (`GOAL_DAILY_DELTA`, re-exported from `goal-recommendation.ts`), not from zero — so eating at maintenance while cutting is correctly "over". |
| "kcal left" on a Preview card (#3282) | `mobile/src/features/nutrition/data/energy-model.ts` → `nutritionEnergyModel()` (the Nutrition energy card: budget, else the calorie target) and **`budgetEnergyModel()`** (budget alone, never the target — what the current app's Home energy card, Body Energy Balance and End of Day show; with no budget they show nothing). Home's donut and Energy Balance widget, the Body tab's `EnergyBalanceCard` and End of Day's `dayTotalFigures` all call it, and so does the Nutrition widget's "kcal left" (#3268; its macro targets read `nutritionEnergyModel`, as the Nutrition screen does). Intake is the recorded figure, else the logged total. **Do not subtract a budget by hand in a card.** |
| Home-screen widgets on Android (Preview, #3268) | Three widgets (Today 4×1 → 4×2, Next workout, Nutrition and water) in the local Expo module `mobile/modules/home-widgets/`: plain `AppWidgetProvider`s with RemoteViews layouts, no server call and no library. **JS words every value, Kotlin only lays it out**: `mobile/src/features/widgets/snapshot.ts` builds the one versioned snapshot from Home's own models (`homeScoreChips`, `homeRecommendationModel`, `budgetEnergyModel`, `batterySummary`, `waterText`) and `WidgetSync.tsx` writes it through `integrations/widgets/` (`guarded.ts` blocks writes after sign-out). The water row carries a table of its wording after 0…8 taps of +250 ml, so the widget redraws when the app is closed without formatting a litre. Taps queue natively (`PendingWater.kt`) and `pending-water.ts` records them via `HealthStore.addWater(ml, date, id)` (the id makes a re-read tap a no-op), then acknowledges. A tap on a widget reaches the app as an intent extra (`launch-target.ts`, `useWidgetLaunch.ts`), never a URL. Colours are `res/values` and `values-night`, pinned to the theme tokens by `tests/home-widgets.test.ts`. |

| One food, drawn one way | `components/nutrition/food-row.tsx` → `FoodRow` (Q-406). Name · grey secondary line · calories right-aligned in a **fixed-width** column · optional chevron. Scalar props: it renders inside `.map()` where a hook cannot live, so call sites wrap it in a small memoised child rather than passing object literals. **The diary row and the external-search row are NOT converted yet** — see Q-406 for why each is waiting |
| The zone bar, drawn | `components/nutrition/calorie-zone-bar.tsx` → `CalorieZoneBar`. **The one place the five-band scale is drawn** — the Nutrition tab and Home's nutrition card both render it (Q-401, which happened because two surfaces showed two different calorie budgets). `packages/shared/src/nutrition/calorie-balance.ts` → `budgetProvenance()` splits today's budget into the rest-day floor and what movement earned, for the line under the bar; it is a `.ts` file because vitest cannot parse JSX, and it belongs in `calorie-balance.ts` once Lane A's half of Q-401 lands |
| Calories in vs out (bands + net) | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/calorie-balance.ts` → `computeCalorieBalance()`, `balanceZone()`, `barBands()`/`barPosition()`. **The one place that decides what "on target" means.** Bands measure deviation from the *goal's* target net (`GOAL_DAILY_DELTA`, re-exported from `goal-recommendation.ts`), not from zero — so eating at maintenance while cutting is correctly "over". |
| Calibrated maintenance (real TDEE) | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/adaptive-tdee.ts` → `estimateMaintenance()`, `resolveMaintenance()`, `maintenanceGapMessage()`. Mean logged intake vs weight slope over a rolling 28/14-day window of **completed** days. Gated: an unlogged day is a gap (never a zero), and coverage / weigh-in-span / plausibility failures return null with a reason rather than a number. Falls back to Mifflin-St Jeor × sedentary. **`activityFactor(totalKcal, bmr)`** (LB-50) is the one place a factor is computed; `/api/nutrition/energy-balance` carries it as `maintenance.activityFactor` — `calibrated` (null until calibrated, with `gapMessage`) and `measuredMovement` (formula resting base + window movement). |
| Recipe URL to a meal (Q-409) | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/recipe-parse.ts` → `extractRecipeJsonLd(html)`, `parseRecipeYield(v)`, `extractReadableText(html)`, `sliceAroundIngredients(text, cap)`. Read schema.org `Recipe` JSON-LD **first** — most sites carry it, it is exact and free, and it gives `recipeYield`; the model is the fallback and only ever sees extracted text, never markup. Served by the `url` branch of `app/api/nutrition/scan/route.ts`, which fetches through `lib/net/safe-fetch.ts` and divides the model's whole-recipe estimate by the yield **in code**. A missing yield returns `recipeYield: null` rather than assuming 1 — assuming 1 is a 4x calorie error that looks plausible. **⚠ The route DIVIDES before it answers, which is the fact every client gets wrong:** a page stating *makes 12* arrives as one slice, so a caller that then sets `servings: 12` makes `oneServingItems()` divide a second time and logs a twelfth of a slice. `components/nutrition/recipe-import.ts` → `recipeBuilderPatch` is where Build a Meal settles it (always `servings: 1`), with tests, because both divides are correct in isolation and the failure is silent. Recognising a link is `components/nutrition/recipe-url.ts` → `asHttpsUrl`/`hostOf`, shared by the plan picker and the meal builder — `https:` only, matching what the route accepts. |
| Meal-plan macro split | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/meal-split.ts` → `suggestMealCount()` (per-meal protein distribution, NOT a claim that meal frequency matters), `splitMacrosAcrossMeals()` (carbs weighted toward the meals bracketing training, protein even, fat away from pre-workout). Splits reconcile to the daily target **exactly** — rounding each share independently drifts by up to n/2 grams. |
| Meal-plan portion sizing | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/meal-split.ts` → `scaleIngredientsToTargets()` / `dominantMacro()`. The model states per-100g reference values; the **gram weights are decided here**, per variant, clamped to 0.4×–2.5× of the suggestion. A fatty protein (salmon, eggs) is a protein source at ≥30% of energy — energy-dominance alone files it under fat and leaves protein uncorrected. |
| Meal top-up (server) | `lib/nutrition/meal-top-up.ts` → `scaleWithTopUp()`. Scaling only ever *resizes what is there*, so a meal with no source for a macro cannot reach it at any factor — this asks the model for ≤3 additions and re-scales, keeping the result only if `fitDistance` improves by `TOP_UP_MIN_IMPROVEMENT`. Used by both generate routes and by `PATCH .../meals/[mealId]` under `scaleToTarget`. Never widen the portion clamp instead: the ice-cream case proved the clamp was not the limit. |
| Macro-vs-target fit | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/meal-macro-fit.ts` → `mealFit()` / `macroFit()` / `sumMacroTotals()`. The one place deciding "on target" (10% of target, absolute floor below that) — every surface showing a plan's drift imports it. |
| Macro/calorie reconciliation | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/goal-recommendation.ts` → `reconcileDailyMacros()` / `carbsFromRemainder()`. Saved macros need not sum to the saved calorie goal; anything planning against both must reconcile first (calories win, carbs take the remainder) or it is solving an unsatisfiable problem. |
| Saved meal → plan ingredients | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/saved-meal-ingredients.ts` → `savedMealToIngredients()` / `oneServingItems()`. `oneServingItems()` is the single place that divides a batch recipe by `saved_meals.servings` — both `logMealItems` and the plan conversion call it, and a meal that makes one serving is returned untouched. A saved meal's `totals` stays the whole recipe on purpose. Converts a library meal's items into the `NutritionIngredient` shape via **per-100g densities**, not the item's own totals — densities are the only form `scaleIngredientsToTargets()` can resize, and resizing is the point of putting a meal you already eat into a plan. |
| Open Food Facts parsing | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/open-food-facts.ts` → `offProductToNutrition()` / `servingSizeGrams()` / `OFF_FIELDS`. The one place turning an OFF product into this app's nutrition shape — shared by the barcode route and the text-search route (`/api/nutrition/food-search`) so they cannot drift on which fields they read. Two rules baked in: a product with no usable kcal returns `null` rather than a zero-calorie ingredient, and a serving-size unit must be followed by a non-letter (`"1 glass (200 ml)"` used to read the "g" of "glass" and call the serving one gram). **The Open Food Facts licence credit is `OFF_ATTRIBUTION` in the same file**, rendered by the web About (`components/more/about-panel.tsx`) and TrainingAi Preview's About (`mobile/src/features/settings/screens/AboutScreen.tsx`); never retype the wording (#3021). |
| Ingredient validation | `packages/shared/src/validators/nutrition-ingredient.ts` → `NutritionIngredientSchema`. One definition, used both as the schema an LLM is forced to fill and as the guard on a client body before it reaches Drizzle. It existed in four copies before this. |
| Meal-plan persistence | `lib/data/postgres/slices/meal-plans.ts`. Variants/meals carry no `user_id`, so every write joins back to `meal_plans` (two levels for a meal). One active plan per user is a **partial unique index**, not app code. |
| **How much was eaten today** — the day's intake, on every calorie surface (#2863) | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/counted-intake.ts` → `countedIntakeKcal({ loggedKcal, estimates, loggedMealTypeIds? })`: logged food plus the plan meals counted as estimated, each half rounded on its own so `loggedIntakeKcal + estimatedKcal === intakeKcal`. `estimates` are `countedEstimates` rows (`{ mealTypeId, kcal }`), served on the energy balance as **`balance.estimatedMeals`** (read with `readEstimateRows`, which drops anything unplaceable) and built server-side by `countedEstimatesFor` (one day) and `countedEstimatesForRange` (a run of days in two range reads, which is what `/api/body-metadata` uses) in `lib/health/energy-balance-service.ts`. `loggedMealTypeIds` drops an estimate whose meal type has food logged since the rows were built: the device's unsynced logs, or the food the logger is about to log. Readers: the server balance, `/api/body-metadata` (Home's Calories tile and Goals, today and week-to-date), the day screen's energy caption (`buildEnergyTimeline` totals; the curve stays logged-only), the logger's "Today after logging" (`assign-step.tsx`), the end-of-day card (`day-summary-card.tsx`, estimates through `useCountedEstimateRows`, share striped), and the native app's `recordedIntake` and `FoodLogger`. **Never add a logged sum to a budget without it**: that was four surfaces saying 600 under a ring saying 1,250. |
| Energy-balance assembly (server) | `lib/health/energy-balance-service.ts` → `computeEnergyBalance(repo, userId, tz, date)`. **Assumed plan meals (#2122 step 8′(3)):** the day's `estimated` plan-meal answers join `intakeKcal` only, reported as `balance.estimatedKcal`, through `countedEstimates` (`packages/shared/src/nutrition/meal-estimate.ts`) summed by `countedIntakeKcal` (`counted-intake.ts`, #2863), which drops an estimate once its slot's meal type has food logged; they never enter `intakeByDate`, so the maintenance estimate learns from logged food alone. **The striped mark (#2072):** `components/nutrition/estimated-kcal.tsx` holds `ESTIMATE_STRIPES` (token `--estimate`), `StripedRingArc` (the ring wedge) and `EstimatedKcalLine` ("incl. N estimated · lunch"); `CalorieProgressBar`'s `estimatedKcal` stripes the bar's tail, so every bar surface (Nutrition, Home, Profile budget) draws it the same way. The Nutrition ring reads the estimate local-first through `useEstimatedMeals` (`app/nutrition/use-estimated-meals.ts`), which uses `countedEstimates`; the logger preview and the day screen read the same local rows through `useCountedEstimateRows` and Home reads `balance.estimatedMeals`, each added up by `countedIntakeKcal`. The single server-side assembly — `/api/nutrition/energy-balance` **and** the AI coach's `getEnergyBalance` tool both call it, so the coach cannot contradict the widget. Client hook: `useEnergyBalanceToday()` in `app/health/hooks/use-health-calcs.ts` (cache-seeded). |
| Achievements / rarity | `lib/achievements.ts`, `packages/shared/src/achievements-calc.ts` (`calorieDayHitsGoal`, `computeEntryStreak`), `packages/shared/src/rarity-colors.ts`. **#2690: the calorie-goal achievements judge each day against THAT day's budget** — `pastDayCalorieBudgets` (`lib/achievements.ts`) reads the span once and calls `calorieBudgetsByDay` (below); a day with no budget keeps the typed `nutrition_targets.calories`. Production impact replay: `scripts/tuning/calorie-award-replay-2690.cjs` |
| **A day's budget inputs, assembled once (#2690)** | `packages/shared/src/nutrition/day-budget.ts` → `budgetMetricsStart`, `dayBody`, `restingRateFor`, `budgetDeficitFor`, `activeEnergyForDay`, `missingBudgetProfileFields`, and `calorieBudgetsByDay` for past days. `computeEnergyBalance` and the achievements both build `calorieBudget`'s inputs here, so a past day's budget equals the live balance's for that date — except that a past day uses only a measured RMR that existed on it, where the live balance uses the latest (pinned in `lib/data/postgres/__tests__/calorie-achievements-day-budget-2690.test.ts`). Goal, goal weight and own target have no history, so past days use today's. The DEXA body-fat calibration build is `lib/health/body-fat-calibration-build.ts` (`buildBodyFatCalibration`, called by the adapter) |
| Workout | `lib/workout/` — `complete-workout.ts`, `delete-session.ts`, `log-exercise.ts`, `hr-analysis.ts`, `hr-summary.ts` (durable per-workout HR snapshot scalars — review H-3/Lever W), `compute-workout-hr.ts` (one path shared by `/api/oura/hr-data` + the admin backfills; persists `workout_hr_stats` mig 135 **and** `set_hr_stats` mig 139), `set-hr-stats.ts` (**the single per-SET HR formula** — peak/avg during the set, rest-bounded drop curve 30/60/90/120s, three time-to-recover models pre-set/RHR/%HRR, coverage gate; reuses `analyseHrRecovery`, never re-implements hrr1; the stored HRR60 and rest verdict come from `hrr60.ts`), `exercise-hr-trend.ts` (per-exercise rollup of `set_hr_stats` → per-session points + per-%1RM buckets via `aggregateExerciseHrTrend`, plus `summarizeHrByExercise` cross-exercise overview; derive-on-read; served by `/api/workout/exercise-hr-trend` **and** the `getWorkoutHrTrends` AI-chat tool), `hr-session-state.ts`, `injury-substitution.ts`, `rest-adherence.ts` (`restAdherenceSets` is **the one place a past set's prescribed rest is chosen** — the logged `planned_rest_sec` first, the live style only where a set has none; feeds the Trends rest bars and the session recap, #2181), `superset-order.ts`, `time-audit.ts`, `year-review.ts`, `known-styles.ts` |
| Post-completion HR pipeline | `lib/workout/post-completion-hr.ts` → `syncAndAttributeSessionHr(userId, workoutSessionId, tz?)` — the one thing every completion path does with heart rate: pull the Oura window for the session (`syncHrForSession`), then attribute it (`computeWorkoutHr` → `upsertWorkoutHrStats` + `upsertSetHrStats`), in that order so attribution sees what the sync just stored. Called by `POST /api/complete-workout` and the outbox's `complete_workout` branch. Replaced a server-to-self `fetch` that failed 9 times in production (Q-122). Best-effort by design — the ring usually drains later, so the coverage-aware admin backfills remain the safety net. `reattributeWorkoutsHrInWindow(repo, userId, from, to, tz)` is the late-HR half (#3078): after an Oura Cloud heart-rate write inserts or changes points (`upsertOuraCloudHeartrate` returns which), it re-attributes every completed session whose HR window they overlap (`listCompletedSessionsOverlapping`, oldest first, at most `REATTRIBUTE_MAX_SESSIONS`), each session isolated, idempotent through the fuller-wins upserts. Every Oura Cloud HR writer takes it as `workoutHr` (`workoutHrReattributor(repo)`, via `reattributeAfterHeartRate` in `lib/oura/cloud/ingest.ts`): the webhook catch-up, the connect backfill, the daily reconciliation and the `oura-cloud-backfill` agent job. |
| Workout Review (AI drop/adjust a session to fit budget) | `packages/shared/src/workout/review/` — `schema.ts` (AI action schema keep/adjust/drop), `prompt.ts`, `reconcile.ts` (`reconcileReview` — pure guards: keep ≥1 primary, protect under-target-muscle coverage, %/role-floor clamp, recomputes projected duration + weekly-volume impact). Routes: `app/api/workout-review/session/[sessionId]` (generate proposal) + `/apply` (overlay = adjusts + this-cycle drops via `AiPrescription.droppedExerciseIds` at status `accepted`; permanent drop via `repo.removeSessionExercise`). This-cycle drop filtered in `app/api/workout-data/route.ts` + `app/api/next-session/route.ts`. UI: `components/workout/review/workout-review-sheet.tsx`, triggered by the per-session **Review** button in `components/config-screen.tsx` (More → Workout, active program only). The sheet shows actionable drops/adjusts AND guard-protected rows (exercises the time budget wanted to drop but a rule kept), and frames time against the full session budget (warmup + working). |

### Canonical display sources — one source + label per physiological metric

The display analogue of One-Formula-One-Place (review 2026-07-16 §4). Any new card showing one of these metrics uses this source and label — annotate the aggregation window whenever it differs.

| Metric | Canonical source | Canonical label |
|---|---|---|
| Daily HRV | `body_metrics.hrvMs` (verbatim copy of `sleep_sessions.averageHrvMs`, the quality-gated overnight median — adapter `:4144-4151`) | "HRV (overnight)" / unit "ms rMSSD · overnight" |
| HRV baseline deviation | `/api/readiness-score` `recentHrv`/`baselineHrv` | "HRV · 7d vs 28d baseline" |
| Daily resting HR | `body_metrics.restingHeartRate` | "Resting HR" |
| Overnight lowest HR | `sleep_sessions.lowestHeartRate` | "Lowest HR" (never "RHR") |
| Sleep score | `/api/readiness-score` `sleepScore` (`ouraToday.sleepScore ?? computeSleepScore()`); stored `sleep_sessions.sleep_score` is Cloud-only (NULL on BLE nights) — fallback display only | "Sleep" score badge/chip |
| Readiness | `/api/readiness-score` `readinessDisplayScore` (null = insufficient data → hide, never show the ungated `score`) | "Readiness" |

---

## 7. AI

| Concern | Where |
|---|---|
| Recent-range maths for the Heart & Recovery range bars | `packages/shared/src/health/range-stats.ts` → `rangeStats(values)` ({min, avg, max}), `rangeZones(optimal)` (the green/amber/red zone widths), `rangePositions(value, min, max, avg)` (marker and average ticks, 0-100). Web `components/health/metric-scale.tsx` and the native `RangeBar` both read it; moved here from `metric-scale.tsx` (#2888). Never re-derive. |
| Round axis ticks for a chart | `packages/shared/src/ui/nice-ticks.ts` → `niceTicks(lo, hi)`: about three ticks on a 1/2/5 step, and `[lo]` for a flat range. The current app's heart-response chart (via `heart-response-view.ts`) and Preview's `shared/ui/chartScale.ts` both re-export it (#3041). |
| The displayed sleep window (bed to wake times) | `packages/shared/src/health/actual-sleep-window.ts` → `actualSleepWindow(row)` — onset trimmed to the first non-awake block, end is the ring's recorded wake (never base + epochs). Web sleep card and metric sheet and the native Body sleep card call it; moved out of `lib/sleep` (#2888). |
| Which night the Body Sleep card shows, and its date label | `packages/shared/src/health/sleep-card-night.ts` → `pickSleepCardNight(rows, today)` → `{night, label}` (newest night dated today or yesterday, label `Night ending 18 Oct` / `Last recorded 15 Oct`), `sleepNightDateLabel(date)`. Web `app/health/health-content.tsx` and the native `BodySleepCard` call it; never re-derive which night is "last night" (issue 2906). |
| Sleep-score coverage line | `packages/shared/src/health/sleep-coverage-note.ts` → `sleepCoverageNote(coverage)` — "Partial data" / "Less complete: no …" under the sleep score. Both apps. |
| The weight a day's activity goals are built from (#2926) | `packages/shared/src/health/latest-weight.ts` → **`latestWeightKg(rows, onOrBefore)`**: the weigh-in with the MAX `date` on or before the day, whatever order the rows arrive in. Postgres `listBodyMetrics` returns newest first, so the old `[...rows].reverse().find(weight > 0)` handed `getDailyGoals` the OLDEST weight in the window. Read by `buildReadinessPayload`, the day audit's `goalProfile`, the health insight's past-day goals and `/api/cardio-week`. A same-date tie keeps the first row seen (strict `>`, like `latestRestingHrRow`); `body_metrics` is unique on `(user_id, date)`, so a repository read cannot tie. The nutrition budget's weight is `dayBody` in `packages/shared/src/nutrition/day-budget.ts`, already order-independent. |
| BMI and its category label | `packages/shared/src/health/bmi.ts` → `bmiClassification({weightKg, heightCm, bodyFatPct, sex})` — band from body fat % when a reading exists (a muscular build is not "overweight"), standard BMI bands otherwise. Web `useBmiClassification` and the native Body panel call it. |
| "vs last week" change on a 14-day trend | `packages/shared/src/health/trend-delta.ts` → `weekOverWeekChange(values)` — today against the first seven days of the window, `{direction, amount, text}`. Web `trend-sparkline.tsx` and the native Body trend charts call it. |
| What a heart-rate screen says about a stand-in max or resting rate, and the no-data wording | `packages/shared/src/health/hr-source-copy.ts` → `maxHrSourceNote`, `restingHrSourceNote`, `noHrDataCopy`. The web `components/health/hr-source-copy.ts` re-exports it; the native Heart rate profile card (`RecordedHealthCards.tsx`) and the Cardio Hub heart profile card (`CardioCards.tsx`) read them directly. |
| What a 14-day trend card says when its metric has no reading | `packages/shared/src/health/trend-empty-copy.ts` → `TREND_EMPTY`. Re-exported by the web `components/health/trend-empty-copy.ts`; the native Heart rate screen reads it directly. |
| One-line note under a Trends card view (e.g. what the meal-timing time is) | `packages/shared/src/health/trend-view-notes.ts` → `TREND_VIEW_NOTE[view]`. Read by web `components/health/trends-section.tsx` and native `ProgressPanel.tsx`. |
| The heart-health activity card's wording and the This-week day labels | `packages/shared/src/running/heart-health-copy.ts` (`CARD_TITLE`, `RATIONALE`, `OUTCOME_LABEL`, `activityLine`, `countedMinutesLine`...). Re-exported by the web `components/cardio/todays-cardio-copy.ts`, which also holds the web-only `OUTCOME_COLOR` (CSS variables); the native Cardio Hub reads it directly and colours the outcomes from its own theme. Whether a day counted is still decided only by `heartHealthVerdict`. |
| "To go" line on the weight and body-fat cards | `packages/shared/src/health/long-term-goal-progress.ts` → `goalRemaining(current, target)` → `{diff, reached}` (one decimal; reached at or under target). Both apps. |
| Personal RMR from a clinical measurement | `packages/shared/src/health/body-composition.ts` → `personalRmr`, `MeasuredRmr` — re-scales a measured RMR to today's fat-free mass by keeping the person's residual from Cunningham, so it ages by body change rather than expiring on a date (BF-33) |
| Retry wrapper for AI calls | `lib/ai/retry.ts` → `withAiRetry`, `isRetryableAiError` |
| Cap on live AI requests off production | `lib/ai/dev-call-budget.ts` → `assertDevAiBudget`, `recordDevAiCall`, `takeDevAiCall`, `DevAiBudgetError` — every key shares production's one prepaid Gemini balance (#2816). Applied once in `withAiLogging` and `loggedStreamText`, so every route is capped without asking; counts each request (a retry, each Coach step). A direct `@google/genai` call outside `instrument.ts` must call `takeDevAiCall()` itself, as `lib/exercise-image-gen.ts` does |
| Gemini billing / quota refusal (depleted credits) | `lib/ai/provider-paused.ts` → `isAiBillingError`, `aiPausedResponse(err)` (a `503 {code:'ai_paused'}`, or `null`), `noteAiBillingError` — the signal is raised once in `withAiLogging` and `loggedStreamText` (an `error_events` row under the **owner**, at most hourly per process, message starting `ai_provider_paused`); a route only adds `aiPausedResponse(err) ??` where it would answer 5xx, after any `degradedFromFacts` fallback. A bare 429 is a rate limit and is not billing (#2815). **The client half is `packages/shared/src/ai-paused-notice.ts`** (re-exported from `lib/ai-paused-notice.ts`) → `aiPausedMessage(status, body)`, `readAiPausedMessage(res)`, `aiPausedMessageFromError(err)` (for `useChat`): a screen that calls an AI route reads the notice with these or shows the server's own `error` text, never a fixed "try again" toast (#2890). The native app reads it through `aiFailureText(error, fallback)` in `mobile/src/features/account/api/client.ts` (#2957). It lives outside `lib/ai/` on purpose, so importing it does not mark a component as a model caller for `check-llm-json-parse` |
| Wall-clock ceiling on every AI call | `lib/ai/deadline.ts` → `AI_CALL_BUDGET_MS` (30s), `runWithDeadline`, `AiTimeoutError` — applied once in `withAiLogging`, so every route is bounded without asking. The budget is **total across the retry**, not per attempt (RV-70); `withAiRetry` takes a `deadlineAt` and skips the retry when the backoff alone would cross it |
| Deterministic fallback for a failed AI prose call | `lib/ai/degrade.ts` → `degradedFromFacts(subject, facts)` — turns a route's own `Label: value` block into one sentence, returning `null` when there are no facts. Used by the workout recap (RV-69); `daily-digest` left in #3286 (it has no model now); **`weekly-digest` left too, in RV-201's second half**; **never persist or client-cache what it returns** — the insight cache keys on a context hash that does not move. **`ai/health-insight` no longer uses it, and was its reference implementation until RV-201 removed that route's model call**: a route with no model has no failure to degrade from. |
| The health insight, written from the numbers | `app/api/ai/health-insight/insight-text.ts` → `buildInsightText(parts)`. Renders the headline and its band, the weakest contributor, today against the recent values (median, and today is excluded from its own baseline), and one sentence naming anything not measured. **No model** — RV-201, under the owner's 2026-09-25 prefer-logic decision; it was the most-called prose route at 28 calls in 30 days, and 16% of 117 audited insights carried a superlative or an imperial unit. A template cannot. Metric assembly stays in `metrics.ts` (`metric`/`splitMeasured`), which is what keeps an absent reading out of the readout entirely. |
| Streaming text response + error marker | `lib/ai/stream.ts` → `textStreamResponse`, `splitStreamError`, `AI_STREAM_ERROR_MARKER` |
| Cached-AI-insight freshness (hash the prompt, serve only on match) | `lib/ai/insight-cache.ts` → `hashInsightContext`, `readFreshInsight` — every `ai_health_insights` read goes through this; there is deliberately no hash-less read (Q-293) |
| The daily digest, the session-explain text and the goal-recommendation explanation, written from the numbers (#3286) | `packages/shared/src/health/daily-digest-text.ts` → `buildDailyDigestText(facts)` (the route fills a `DigestFacts`), `packages/shared/src/session-explain/insight-text.ts` → `buildSessionInsightText(parts)`, `packages/shared/src/nutrition/goal-recommendation-text.ts` → `buildGoalExplanation(input)` and `suggestActivityLevel(current, sessions)` (the one judgement the model made: 4+ sessions a week against a stated sedentary/light level moves to moderate). **No model**, under the owner's 25 Sep prefer-logic decision; the routes keep auth, Zod and rate limits. The same-day AI-context helper (Q-291) went with the digest, its only reader, so the digest no longer sees earlier AI prose. |
| Shared prose guards for user-facing AI text | `lib/ai/prompt-guards.ts` → `PROSE_GUARDS` (whole-output prose: metric units, quote-don't-recompute, no superlatives, no diagnosis) · `PROSE_FIELD_GUARDS` (a text field inside a structured result: units, superlatives and no diagnosis) · the four rules are also exported individually. **The split is load-bearing, not tidiness (PS-32):** `nutrition-goals/recommend` returns the calorie and macro targets and `workout-review` returns sets/reps/%1RM, so "never state a number that is not above" contradicts their job — that clause is why the guard reached 5 of 10 routes rather than all of them. `lib/ai/__tests__/prose-guards.test.ts` holds both lists and asserts only the prose set carries the quote rule |
| Free text the user typed, going into a prompt | `packages/shared/src/ai/untrusted-text.ts` → `sanitiseUserText(v)`, `userTextBlock(values)`, `USER_TEXT_NOTE`. Strips control characters (the newline is what lets injected text own a line) and angle brackets (which is how the fence would be forged), then wraps the values in `<user_text>…</user_text>`; `USER_TEXT_NOTE` goes in once per prompt to say the tag holds data. **Mitigation, not a boundary** — the coach-scope row above says scope by withholding, and a field the prompt needs cannot be withheld. Live before PS-32: a 71-char `excludedFoods` entry renamed the meal plan and every meal in it. A field the model is MEANT to act on (the meal-rewrite `instruction`) takes `sanitiseUserText` alone — fencing it would ask the model to ignore the request it was sent to serve. **Fenced so far:** the two meal-plan prompts (PS-32) and the injury path (LA-69) — `formatInjuryContext`'s `notes` AND `muscleName`, which is `z.string().min(1).max(100)` rather than a picker and reaches four prompts from a stored row (generate-program, builder-chat, `ai-periodization/prompt.ts`, `workout/review/prompt.ts`). `soreMusclesInSession` beside it comes from a picker and is deliberately not fenced. **A menu is not a preference (LA-73):** exercise names must come back from the model verbatim to match the library, so a fence invites the tag into the answer. `promptSafeLine(value)` is their treatment — control characters and whitespace runs out, **angle brackets kept**, applied in the `POST /api/exercises` schema as a `.transform()` (with a `.refine()` after it, because `.min(1)` runs first and a name of only control characters would otherwise arrive empty). Measured before shipping: none of the 155 exercise, 5 program, 22 session or 25 style names carried one, so it is a guard rather than a repair. The other three tables have no request schema at all to hang it on — LA-74 |
| What `/api/export` covers, and what it deliberately doesn't | `lib/export/export-map.ts` → `EXPORTED`, `EXCLUDED`, `SOFT_DELETED`, `WITHHELD_COLUMNS`, `DERIVED_DOMAINS` — exhaustive over `schema.ts`; a new table fails `scripts/check-export-coverage.js` until it is classified (Q-288), and so does a `repo.<method>()` line in `full-export.ts` (today only `goals`) not declared in `DERIVED_DOMAINS` with `sourceTables` that are themselves exported (#2427). **The file's last line is the completeness signal**: `{"_complete":true}` or `{"_error":{code,message}}` from `lib/export/trailer.ts`; any other last line means the download was cut off. Written by `app/api/export/route.ts`, which pulls the generator with backpressure |
| **How often the Coach offers a write, and what happened to it** (#2245) | `lib/coach/proposal-rate.ts` → `coachProposalRate(threads)` over stored `coach_messages.parts`: widgets shown by kind, proposals shown / applied / cancelled / stale / unanswered, and answered choice lists directly followed by a proposal (a completed swap counts once; an abandoned flow never). Counts widgets, not messages, because the client appends a post-answer turn to the same stored assistant message. Run it with `node scripts/coach-proposal-rate.mjs [--since YYYY-MM-DD] [--schema public]` (read-only; the default `claude_ro` schema is row-scoped to the owner). |
| AI dynamic periodization engine | `packages/shared/src/ai-periodization/` — `generate-prescription.ts` (`generatePrescriptionForSession` — the shared, request-free generation+persist core, run by the `/prescribe` route). Generation is never triggered at completion — completion just marks the slot `consumed`. It is **warmed from Home** (`POST /api/ai-periodization/session/[id]/warm`, #2155, owner 2026-09-30) when the slot is consumed or the plan has aged out (`isAiPrescriptionPending`, `prescriptionAgedOut` in `reevaluate.ts`) and today is not already trained, through the same single flight as `workout-data`'s tab-open trigger, which stays as the fallback. A `pending` prescription (phase transition/deload/stay) never auto-expires or auto-dismisses — only an explicit accept/dismiss/transition changes its status. `signals.ts` (`aggregateSignals`), `prompt.ts`, `prescription-schema.ts`, `reconcile-prescription.ts`, `apply-prescription.ts`, `autoregulation.ts`, `time-budget.ts`, `volume-targets.ts`, `expected-rpe.ts`, `confidence.ts`, `emergency-deload.ts`, `per-exercise-deload.ts`, `phase-guards.ts`, `explain.ts`, `goal-ranges.ts` |
| AI chat context + tools | `lib/ai-chat/context.ts`, `lib/ai-chat/tools.ts` |
| **AI Coach — in-chat widgets + the only AI write path** | `/coach` route (`app/coach/`, `useChat` from `@ai-sdk/react`) · `lib/coach/` — `widgets.ts` (the closed `CoachWidget` union), `tools.ts` (widget tools have **no `execute`**, which is what makes them client-side and suspends the turn for the user), `patch.ts`, `consequences.ts` (measured server-side; the model has no field to author one), `apply.ts`. Routes: `/api/coach` (UI message stream — **not** the plain-text stream `/api/ai-chat` uses), `/api/coach/preview`, `/api/coach/apply`, `/api/coach/apply/[id]/undo`. `threads.ts` for 30-day conversation history (pruned on write — no cron layer). Coach alone runs `COACH_MODEL_ID` (`gemini-3.6-flash`) with `google.tools.googleSearch({})` grounding; every other AI route stays on `AI_MODEL_ID`. Components in `components/coach/`. Backlog **Q-157**; plans in `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-08-ai-coach-*.md`. Two traps recorded there: do **not** extend the `<sheet_chart>` in-text block pattern to input widgets, and the SDK's `needsApproval` flow cannot carry per-row toggles (`ToolApprovalResponse` is binary) |
| Session-explain ("Why this?") | `packages/shared/src/session-explain/` (`build-explain-data.ts`, `group-signals.ts`) |
| Chart-block parsing in chat | `packages/shared/src/parse-chart-blocks.ts`, `components/chart-message.tsx` |
| Validators | `packages/shared/src/validators/saved-meal.ts`, `packages/shared/src/validators/nutrition-ingredient.ts`. (`chat.ts` and `tts.ts` were deleted by LA-145: nothing had parsed them since Q-189 removed their routes.) |

All structured LLM output uses `generateObject`/response schemas — never `JSON.parse` of
free text (`CLAUDE.md`).

---

## 8. Oura Ring integration — `lib/oura/`

Full spec: `docs/oura-ring-data-reference.md` and the `oura-api` skill. See also the
Oura section in `CLAUDE.md`.

**The Oura Cloud API is back as the ring's source (owner, 2026-10-11, #2808).** It was removed on
2026-08-13 (Q-224), when the ring was on our own BLE key. Part 1 (#2858) is the connector in
`lib/oura/cloud/`: the only module that may call Oura Cloud, held by
`lib/oura/__tests__/cloud-calls-only-from-connector.test.ts`. Part 2 (#2859) adds webhooks and the history backfill (rows below).

| Concern | Where |
|---|---|
| `DataSourceConnector` shape + the Oura Cloud entry | `packages/shared/src/health/source-connectors.ts` → `DataSourceConnector`, `OURA_CLOUD_CONNECTOR`, `DATA_SOURCE_CONNECTORS`. Driver-free. The other sources get entries with PS-40's registry |
| Oura Cloud settings, URLs and scopes | `lib/oura/cloud/config.ts` → `ouraOAuthConfig()` (`OURA_CLIENT_ID` / `OURA_CLIENT_SECRET` / `OURA_REDIRECT_URI`; `null` = switched off, answer 503), `buildAuthorizeUrl`, `OURA_SCOPES` (the pinned spec's names) |
| Oura OAuth `state` / CSRF | `lib/oura/cloud/connect-ticket.ts` → `createConnectTicket` / `readConnectTicket` (JWT on `AUTH_SECRET`, own issuer, 10 min, nonce bound to the `ta_oura_connect` cookie), `connectReturnUrl` (web Devices page, or the native scheme from `NATIVE_CALLBACKS` + `/oura/callback`) |
| Who may commit an Oura grant (#2883 review) | Only a request authenticated as the ticket's user. Web: the callback needs that user's session (`reason=signed_out` otherwise). Native: the callback seals the grant into a handle (`lib/oura/cloud/pending-grant.ts` → `sealPendingGrant` / `openPendingGrant`, AES-256-GCM under its own HKDF label, 10 min) and the app finishes at `POST /api/oura/connect/complete` with its bearer; another user's handle is revoked, never stored. The nonce cookie proves the same browser came back, not whose it is |
| Oura token encryption at rest | `lib/oura/cloud/token-cipher.ts` → `encryptOuraToken` / `decryptOuraToken` (AES-256-GCM, key = HKDF-SHA256 of `OURA_TOKEN_KEY`, AAD = user + column; a value that does not open reads as `null`; missing key throws `OuraTokenKeyMissingError`) |
| Oura HTTP calls | `lib/oura/cloud/client.ts` → `exchangeAuthorizationCode`, `refreshAccessToken`, `revokeAccessToken`, `fetchOuraUserId`. Imported only inside `lib/oura/cloud/` |
| An Oura access token for a user (refreshes first) | `lib/oura/cloud/tokens.ts` → `getOuraAccessToken(store, config, userId)`, refreshing inside `REFRESH_MARGIN_MS`, single-flight per user (Oura rotates refresh tokens); `connectOuraAccount`, `getOuraConnection`, `disconnectOuraAccount`. Storage: `lib/data/postgres/slices/oura-cloud-tokens.ts` (ciphertext only). Routes: `/api/oura/connect`, `/api/oura/callback`, `/api/oura/connect/complete`, `/api/oura/connection`. `exchangeOuraCode` / `storeOuraGrant` / `discardOuraGrant` are the steps the callback and complete route compose |
| Oura Cloud documents → our rows (#2859) | `lib/oura/cloud/documents.ts` → one Zod schema per spec model (`OURA_DOCUMENT_TYPES`, keyed by the webhook's `data_type`; `vo2_max` reads `vO2_max`), `mapOuraDocuments` (one `oura_daily` + one `body_metrics` row per day, sleep periods, workouts, deleted-period ids), `mapHeartRateRows`. **Enum fields are lenient (#3081):** read as strings and checked against `OURA_ENUMS` (the pinned spec's lists, one place); a value off the list keeps its document, stores as null (a sleep type as sent, which only `long_sleep` can make a main period) and is counted in `unknownValues`, which `writeOuraCloudData` reports as `document:unknown-value` (field names and counts). `OURA_DELETE_TYPES` is which `delete` events the webhook acts on and subscribes to. Oura's readiness, sleep and activity contributors and the stress `day_summary` go to `oura_cloud_daily`, never `oura_daily`, because stored there they would replace ours on the Sleep and Activity screens (#2895 review, #2982) |
| Reading a user's Oura data | `lib/oura/cloud/data.ts` → `fetchOuraDocument` (one by id), `fetchOuraDocuments` (a date range, following `next_token`), `fetchOuraHeartRate` |
| Writing Oura Cloud data | `lib/oura/cloud/ingest.ts` → `writeOuraCloudData`, all as `oura_cloud`. Per-field merge for `oura_daily` / `body_metrics` (BLE and manual outrank it); `upsertOuraCloudSleep` decides an overlapping night by the ladder per night through `settleNights`, the one night-ladder helper all three night writers use (`nightSourceRank` in `packages/shared/src/health/source-rank.ts`, #3080, #3115): the ring before the switch day keeps it and the skip is reported (`sleep:skipped-overlap`), a manual, Health Connect or stale Oura row yields by tombstone, and `settleNights` keeps a Health Connect or Apple Health re-sync from reviving it. `saveSleepSession` (`/api/sync-health`) and the native rebuild go through it too: an incoming night is kept only if no live overlapping night outranks it (a tie with another source keeps the stored one; the same source keeps the longer night, except Oura Cloud, whose re-issued period replaces it), and it tombstones what it beats; the legacy client sends one app per night (`oneAppPerNight`, `lib/health-connect-sync.ts`); it follows a moved start (its end and day too: `upsertOuraSleep(…, { replaceOwnCloud: true })`), and a move blocked by a tombstone gives the id to that tombstone; a `rest` retype is tombstoned keeping its period type; each touched day's HRV and resting HR come from its main period (`syncOuraMainSleepBodyMetrics` → `getOuraMainSleepPeriods`, `clearOuraCloudBodyField`); `upsertOuraCloudHeartrate` skips points within 5 min of a ring/strap row; `upsertOuraWorkouts` keeps `reviewed`; `tombstoneOuraCloudSleep`; `sleep_time` fills `oura_daily.recommended_bedtime_*` in minutes from the day's local midnight, with its status and recommendation (#3077); `oura_sessions` is read by the day timeline (`listOuraSessions`, which skips a tombstoned session; Oura sending it again revives it), as `oura_tags` has no writer. Slice: `lib/data/postgres/slices/oura.ts`. Never touches `oura_raw_samples` / `oura_raw_packed` |
| Oura webhook | `app/api/oura/webhook/route.ts` (verification `GET`, signed event `POST`; never 410, which would cancel the subscription) → `lib/oura/cloud/webhook.ts` → `handleOuraWebhookEvent` (routes by `oura_tokens.oura_user_id`, `getUserIdByOuraUserId`). A `delete` only ever tombstones (`OURA_DELETE_TYPES`): a sleep period (#2859), a workout (#3087) and a session (#3153, `tombstoneOuraSessions`, `oura_sessions.deleted_at`); daily documents need their ids stored first (#3153). The daily reconcile catches a missed workout or session delete with the one sweep in `backfill.ts` (`tombstoneAbsent`: a row gone from a day it read whole, never a read's last day). Oura has no heart-rate webhook, so a `sleep`, `daily_activity` or `workout` event also starts `startOuraCloudHeartRateCatchUp` (after the answer, ≤1×/5 min per user, from the newest `oura_cloud` point via `getLatestOuraCloudHeartrateAt`, at most 7 days back). Signature refusals, renewal failures, a heart-rate catch-up failure and a connect backfill that did not finish go to `error_events` through `lib/oura/cloud/report.ts` → `reportOuraCloudIssue` (≤1×/h per kind, outcome only) |
| Webhook subscriptions | `lib/oura/cloud/subscriptions.ts` → `ensureOuraWebhookSubscriptions` (client credentials; renews inside 7 days, creates missing); `lib/oura/cloud/renewal.ts` → `startOuraWebhookRenewal` (production, 5 min after boot then every 12 h, under a **session-level** advisory lock with no transaction: the pool's 15 s idle-in-transaction timeout would kill a transaction-scoped one mid-pass), agent job `oura-webhook-subscriptions` |
| History backfill | `lib/oura/cloud/backfill.ts` → `backfillOuraCloud` (date range, dry run, idempotent), `startOuraConnectBackfill` (90 days, started by `storeOuraGrant` after every connect), agent job `oura-cloud-backfill` (a write answers 202 and finishes its log row later). A 429 with a Retry-After ≤ 60 s is waited out (3 times a run at most); a longer one stops the run |
| Daily reconciliation read (#3079) | `lib/oura/cloud/reconcile.ts` → `reconcileOuraCloud` re-reads the last `RECONCILE_DAYS` (3) local days of every document type and heart rate for each user from `listOuraCloudConnectedUserIds`, through `backfillOuraCloud`; `startOuraReconcile` (instrumentation, production only) runs it 20 min after boot and every 24 h under `withSessionLock` (`renewal.ts`, shared with the webhook renewal) |
| A late, revised or deleted Oura night re-scores its day (#3079) | `lib/health/rescore-nights.ts` → `withNightRescore(repo, userId, write)` brackets every Oura sleep write (`writeOuraCloudData`'s `rescore`, the webhook delete). Days in the last `RESCORE_WINDOW_DAYS` (7) whose canonical night changed: Sleep Score recomputed (`scoreNightForDate`, the readiness read's own scoring) or cleared; today's readiness cleared (`clearNightDerivedScores`) and, only when the night arrived in that write, its Body Battery anchor released (`releaseBodyBatteryAnchor`); a finished day's readiness kept and stamped `model_versions.readinessNightRevisedAt` for the Orchestrator's re-derive. Each bracket runs under a per-user lock (`withOuraSleepWriteLock` → `lib/data/postgres/user-lock.ts`). Period types are written inside it, before the sleep rows. Readiness and the Sleep Score persist from a read only when the night is today's (`readiness-payload.ts`), both paths scoring through `packages/shared/src/health/sleep-score-for-date.ts` → `scoreSleepForDate`. Training stress re-evaluates a finished day once more when its Oura MET changed after the verdict (`staleTrainingStressDays`' `metChangedAtByDay`, from `getOuraCloudMetChangedAt`) |
| ~~HR-sync / sync throttle~~ | **Deleted 2026-08-13.** The Cloud feed is webhooks plus the backfill above |
| Types | `lib/oura/types.ts` — `OuraWorkout` only; `oura_workouts` is written again by the Cloud ingest (#2859) |
| Contributors mapping | Labels (`labelFor`): `packages/shared/src/health/contributor-labels.ts`. Worst-first ranking and the weakest pick (`rankedContributors`, `weakestContributor`): `packages/shared/src/health/contributor-ranking.ts`. `lib/oura/contributors.ts` re-exports them and holds `formatContributors`. The native card's "Weakest:" line calls the same ranking. |
| Which readiness inputs a score is missing, in words | `packages/shared/src/health/readiness-input-labels.ts` → `ReadinessInputKey` (re-exported by `lib/health/score-availability.ts`) and `limitedInputsNote(missing)`. The label map is `Record<ReadinessInputKey, string>`, so a new input is a compile error until it has words. Web Readiness and the native one both call it. |
| Week-over-week caption ("↑ 2.0h on 5.0", "Level with 60") and its good/bad cue | `packages/shared/src/health/week-delta.ts` → `weekDelta(week, prior, { unit, decimals, higherIsBetter })`. Called by the web `WeekMetricCard` and the native Week screen; the web strings are pinned in `packages/shared/src/health/__tests__/week-delta.test.ts`. Flat only at an exact zero. |
| Native Week in review wording (range, weekday initials, fixed decimals, muscle rows, weakest factor) | `mobile/src/features/health/data/week-view.ts`. The range prints the web's `d MMM` from a fixed month array, not a locale. |
| BLE freshness | `lib/oura/ble-freshness.ts` → `isBleDataFresh(...)` |
| Cloud re-key freshness | `lib/oura/cloud-freshness.ts` → `OURA_CLOUD_REKEY_DATE` (the single 2026-07-07 constant) + `isPreRekey(day)` (fail-closed). Gate for every "is this frozen Cloud data?" check — readiness route day-summary/bedtime gating, display honesty markers. One Formula, One Place. |
| Primary sleep session pick | `lib/sleep/primary-sleep.ts` |
| **Median and quantile** | `packages/shared/src/stats.ts` → `median(values)`, `quantile(values, q)`. THE order statistics, domain-neutral so health, workout and device code share one rather than each keeping a copy. Average of the two middles on an even count; **`null` on empty, never 0** — the copy this replaced in `health/hr-smoothing.ts` returned `0`, and a zero bpm reads as a measurement rather than an absence. Rounding is the CALLER's (cadence rounds to 1 dp, the live-HR readout to a whole bpm) — a path that wants an integer rounds at its display boundary instead of keeping its own median. LA-148 consolidated six and LA-151 the remaining nine, so **this is now the only general median in the repo** — the census is `grep -rn "length % 2" packages/shared/src lib app` plus a scan for `function median*`, and both earlier counts (four, then eight) were low only because the grep was scoped. **`lowerMedian(values)` lives here too**: the LOWER of the two middles, which is `torch.median`'s definition and an actually-observed value rather than one invented between two. Three callers need exactly that — `oura-models/daily-baselines.ts` and `cumulative-stress.ts` mirror a model whose goldens pin it, and `/api/oura-ble/step-counter-export` (retired in #2862) was a console that must not report a stride frequency the ring never decoded. **Reach for `median` unless you can name which of those two applies.** **`iqrBand(values, k)`** is the outlier band (`median`, `p25 − k·IQR`, `p75 + k·IQR`, `null` on empty) shared by the sleep and readiness verdicts, so the two rules cannot drift into two definitions of one band. Four copies stay private on purpose and LA-151 records each reason: `sleep-staging.ts` (nearest-rank, plus its own `quantile`), `hrv-5min.ts` (pinned citation), `temperature-baseline.ts`'s `median7` (fixed 7-slot ring buffer in a hot loop), and `daytime-stress.ts`'s `hrMinMedianMax` (positional, not a median at all). |
| **"Which rows are the night?"** | `packages/shared/src/health/sleep-night.ts` → `nightSessions(sessions, tz)` (+ `groupSleepPeriods`/`nightForDate`/`latestNight`/`aggregateNight`/`isNightWindow`). THE answer — classify by circadian midpoint first (naps never enter the night band), then merge windows ≤ `MAX_INTRA_NIGHT_GAP_HOURS` apart, then collapse each night into one scoreable session. **Any read site that treats "one row = one night" must go through this**, because production stores 66 rows for 54 nights: 12 are daytime/evening bouts and one real night arrived as two rows. Sorting by `date`/`sleepEnd` and taking the first is the bug it exists to prevent (F-1, Q-1, Q-76). Deliberately NOT used where naps are wanted — the day timeline, the sleep list, and the daytime-HRV sleep-exclusion windows all read raw rows on purpose. **The night a date is graded on** is `canonicalNightForDate(nights, date, tz)` / `canonicalLatestNight(nights, tz)`: the longest night on the date, never a window that `opensFollowingNight` (midpoint on its own wake date at or after 21:00, i.e. tonight's onset or an evening nap) unless it lasts `ALWAYS_NIGHT_MIN_HOURS` or more, which is a main sleep on any clock (readiness-tree edge case 13). `isDatesNight` is that test (#2456). The sleep score, readiness, the day audit and the derived-score backfill read these. The raw-session pickers apply the same test to each period inside `nightPeriodsByDate(periods, tz)`, so `nightForDate`, `latestNight` and the BLE rollup's per-date night (`oura_daily_summary`) agree with them (#2487). **Which night a date woke from** is narrower: `nightWokenFrom(nights, date, tz)` skips every `opensFollowingNight` window however long, and Body Battery's wake anchor uses it (#2230). |
| **Sleep subsystem reference** | [`docs/sleep-system.md`](sleep-system.md) — staging pipeline (`packages/shared/src/health/sleep-staging.ts`), Sleep Score (`packages/shared/src/health/sleep-score.ts`), window/merge helpers (`lib/sleep/*`), what's reliable vs approximate, the calibration log, and open levers. **Read before any sleep-staging/scoring work.** |
| Routes | `app/api/oura/*` (`sync`, `token`, `webhook`, `hr-*`, `stats`, `connect`, `callback`, …) |
| Direct-BLE (Phase 2 spike) | `lib/oura-ble/plugin.ts` (guarded JS wrapper) + `android/.../oura/` (Kotlin: `OuraProtocol`/`OuraAuth` pure, `OuraGattClient`/`OuraRingService`/`OuraBlePlugin` native) + `/admin/oura-ble` tester screen. Native ring connect: scan/auth/live-stream/history-drain + reconnection metrics. Separate from the Cloud API above. |
| **Ring link counters (#2469)** — connects, drops, connected time | `lib/oura-ble/link-stats.ts` → `linkStatsPayload(status, nowMs)` (pure; null for a stopped service or a missing counter, never a fabricated zero) and `reportRingLinkStats()` (open, resume and hourly from `components/sync-provider.tsx`, ≤1×/15 min) → `POST /api/oura-ble/link-stats` → `oura_ble_link_stats` (`claude_ro.oura_ble_link_stats`). Read-only: it calls `getStatus()` and nothing else, so the link and the history cursor are untouched. **Counters are cumulative since `service_started_at` (device now − uptime) and reset with the service** — group rows by that instant with a seconds tolerance and difference within a group; Δ`total_connected_ms` / Δ`recorded_at` is the share of a gap the link was up. Posts only while the WebView runs |
| Direct-BLE decode + store (Phase 3+4 MVP) | `lib/oura-ble/decode.ts` (pure event decoders ported byte-exact from open_oura's `events.rs`: IBI/HR, temp, HRV, SpO₂, battery, sleep phases) → server-side **ingest route** `POST /api/oura-ble/samples` (decodes forwarded frames, dedups into `oura_raw_samples`, Postgres migration 114) + `GET /api/oura-ble/samples/summary`. Architecture = ingest path like the Oura Cloud sync, **not** an offline-first outbox domain. Durability = the confirm-before-advance cursor rule: the persisted resume cursor only moves once a batch is durably committed — since 2026-07-27 to the phone's own `oura_raw.db` in one transaction with its rows, with the server POST a best-effort backup (the server-2xx `confirmStored(ds)` gate of v1.117.5 survives only as the fallback when `oura_raw.db` cannot open); since v1.119.0 the native service POSTs drained batches itself — no WebView in the data path. Ops manual (failure matrix, cadence policy, maintenance playbook): `docs/oura-ble-operations.md`. |
| Step estimate (Tier 1) + live-counted merge (Tier 2) | `packages/shared/src/health/step-estimate.ts` — the ONLY walk-window/step-estimate formula (`estimateSteps`, col14 walk gate) and the Tier-2-wins merge (`mergeStepSources`: live-counted windows override the gate estimate for the ds span they cover). Live windows persist in `step_live_windows` (migration 119, `user_id, start_ds` unique) via `POST /api/oura-ble/live-steps` (product write, user-scoped — not admin-gated like the ingest routes above; accepts EITHER `{startDs, endDs}` directly OR wall-clock time converted via `dsFromMeasuredAtMs`, since accel frames carry no ring ds of their own but gate frames — history events — do, via `historyEventFromHex`). Two producers: the manual "Save result" button in `components/oura-ble/live-step-test.tsx`'s tester (wall-clock, accel-only), and the auto-triggering JS state machine `lib/oura-ble/step-orchestrator.ts` (ds-based, gate-triggered — pure decision core in `step-orchestrator-core.ts`, unit-tested; mounted native-only from `components/sync-provider.tsx`). Both the orchestrator and the walk-detection service consume the shared paired-`0x7e`/`0x7f` gate-window pipeline `lib/oura-ble/gate-feed.ts` (`subscribeGateFeed`, unit-tested: pairing, out-of-order dedup, non-gate-tag ignore, disconnect) rather than each re-pairing frames. Yields to live-HR bursts via `LiveHrManager.isRunning()` (radio courtesy — same physical BLE link). App-open coverage only; a native always-on port (Chunk C) is planned but not built. |
| **Cadence (steps/min) — ring + strap** | `packages/shared/src/health/cadence.ts` — the ONE place cadence is derived for BOTH sources. `cadenceFromStrideHz` (ring: unit conversion of the decoded `stride_frequency`; factor is 60 **or** 120 — the open D-2 units question, both exposed via `RING_STRIDE_INTERPRETATIONS` for the calibration console to decide), `detectCadence` (strap: our own DSP, since the H10 publishes no cadence over BLE), `bandAutocorrPeak` (the shared band-autocorrelation primitive — sub-sample parabolic interpolation + octave correction; `gaitBandAutocorr` below now DELEGATES to it with both refinements off, preserving its calibrated fixtures), `summarizeCadence` (persisted avg + 10 s-binned series), `compareCadence` (ring-vs-strap agreement; flags a ~2x split as a units/octave error), and `supportsCadence`/`CADENCE_ACTIVITY_TYPES` (walk/run/hike/**treadmill** — deliberately NOT `is_distance_based`, which excludes the treadmill and wrongly includes cycle/swim). Live fusion in `lib/activity/cadence-tracker.ts` (strap leads when fresh, ring covers the rest; pure tested staleness reducer). Strap samples come from the native PMD stream (`PolarProtocol`/`PolarGattClient`/`PolarStrapService`, magnitudes batched ~1/s to JS, opt-in and bounded to an activity — never all-day). Persisted on `activity_logs.cadence_spm/_series/_source` (migration 140). Calibration console: `/admin/cadence`. ⚠ device-gated — no BLE in the sandbox. |
| Own daytime-HRV (D5) — replaces Oura's `dhrv_imputation` ONNX model | `packages/shared/src/health/daytime-hrv-model.ts` — the ONE place a per-user daytime-HRV regression is fit/evaluated: `extractNightlyTrainingSamples` (night-time `0x5d`→(hr,temp,rmssd) tuples, sleep-window-restricted), `fitDaytimeHrvModel` (closed-form 3×3 OLS, `ln(rmssd) = a+b·hr+c·temp`, `MIN_TRAINING_SAMPLES` floor), `evaluateDaytimeHrvModel` (MET-gated point eval — MET is NOT a fit feature, see the plan doc), `daytimeHrvEstimatesPerBucket` (shared bucket-iteration, used by both the production stress series and the D6 harness adapter above). Persisted one-row-per-user in `oura_daytime_hrv_model` (migration 149) via `getDaytimeHrvModel`/`upsertDaytimeHrvModel`. Refit is throttled (24h, `fittedAt`-gated) and runs from `aggregateOuraRawSamples`'s own `maybeRefitDaytimeHrvModel` step — never on a live request path. Production consumers: `lib/health/daytime-stress.ts`'s `buildDaytimeStressSeriesFromModel` (the ONNX `computeDaytimeStress`/`buildDaytimeStressSeries` stay golden-tested but unreachable from production until D7) via `adapter.ts`'s resilience rollup and `app/api/body-battery/route.ts`. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-27-d5-own-daytime-hrv.md`. |
| Gait-gated accel step count (Tier 2 accuracy) | `lib/oura-ble/gait-step-count.ts` — the ONE place walking is separated from non-walk hand motion in the raw accel stream. `countGaitGatedSteps(magnitudes, fs)` peak-counts only inside a sustained walking rhythm; `gaitBandAutocorr` scores 1.4–2.8 Hz periodicity per window. Rejects the false positives that broke the col14 gate and the naive peak counter (calibrated on real captures 2026-07-13: walk-30 → 31, handwave-0 [114 naive] → 0; unit-tested with both fixtures). Requires the ring's automatic measurements (DAYTIME_HR/SPO2/REAL_STEPS) OFF for the `0x33` stream to flow — they preempt the accelerometer otherwise. Later narrowed on-device: only REAL_STEPS blocks the stream — DAYTIME_HR/SpO₂ keep recording while accel streams. Drives the tester's gated readout AND the continuous-capture ingest below; the rollup cutover making it the day's step source is Chunk 2 (not yet wired). |
| Continuous daytime step capture (production, Chunk 1) | `lib/oura-ble/continuous-capture.ts` — the default-OFF singleton (`ta_ring_continuous_capture`) that streams the ring's `0x33` accel 06–22 local with REAL_STEPS off, yields to live-HR, watchdog/reconnect re-arms, and posts ~2-min raw magnitude chunks to `POST /api/oura-ble/accel-chunks` → `oura_accel_chunks` (migration 122, raw kept 7 days, ingest-time prune) → server-side `countGaitGatedSteps` → `step_live_windows` (source `continuous-accel`). Battery/diagnostics logged every 5 min (exportable from the `/admin/oura-ble` card). Exactly ONE step path owns the radio: `sync-provider` starts this when the toggle is on, else the gate orchestrator. WebView-alive only (native port is Chunk 3). |

Health Connect (Tasker ingest) sibling integration: `lib/health-connect-sync.ts`,
`app/api/health-connect/ingest`.

| Concern | Where |
|---|---|
| **Health Connect per-interval steps, active kcal, cadence (#2462)** — feeds `steps/min` and the `MET/min` cascade (#2115) for watch and phone users | `lib/health-connect-sync.ts` → `flattenIntervalRecords('steps' \| 'active_kcal', records)` and `flattenCadenceRecords(records)` (pure; a record with no `metadata.id`, unparseable span or non-finite value is dropped, never sent) read the plugin's `Steps` / `ActiveCaloriesBurned` / `StepsCadenceSeries` records inside `syncHealthConnect`, posted newest first in chunks (`INTERVAL_UPLOAD_CHUNK`, `INTERVAL_UPLOAD_MAX_CHUNKS`) as `activityIntervals` to `POST /api/sync-health`, which judges each row with the helper that already owns it (`isPlausibleStepWindow`, `activityImplausibleReason`'s kcal/min, `isPlausibleCadence`) → `repo.upsertHealthConnectIntervals` → `health_connect_intervals` (`claude_ro.health_connect_intervals`; keyed `(user_id, kind, record_id, start_at)`, so a re-read window is idempotent). **Every app's overlapping rows are kept** — de-duplicate on read through `stepCandidates` (`dedupeOverlappingWindows`), never at ingest. Read with `repo.getHealthConnectIntervals(userId, kind, from, to)`. Server-only by design (Health Connect is the device copy). Cadence reads under READ_STEPS, so it is gated on the `Steps` grant; the plugin converters live in `patches/@devmaxime__capacitor-health-connect.patch` (APK-gated) |

## 9. Notifications & push

| Concern | Where |
|---|---|
| Rest-complete local notification | `lib/notifications.ts` |
| Meal / workout / supplement reminders | `lib/meal-reminders.ts`, `lib/workout-reminders.ts`, `lib/supplement-reminders.ts` (each `computeXAction` + `reconcileX`) |
| Health anomaly alerts (illness / high-stress / low-readiness) | `lib/health-alerts.ts` → `computeHealthAlertActions(input, notifiedToday)` (pure fire/skip, illness `elevated`/`fever` only, stress `highMinutes`≥threshold else `current`≤`STRESS_HIGH_LEVEL`, readiness-low suppressed when illness/stress fires; per-type per-day dedup) + `reconcileHealthAlerts` (Capacitor, ids 9300–9302). Fired from a native-only `sync-provider.tsx` effect (open+resume) reading warmed readiness-score + body-battery; gated by `ta_pref_health_alerts`. On-device local-notification design (no cron layer, §0); delivery is APK-only. |
| Reminder reconciliation wiring | `components/sync-provider.tsx` |
| ~~Web push~~ | **DELIBERATE: gone, 2026-08-25 (Q-285).** Owner decision, taken with the count in hand: `push_subscriptions` held 0 rows across two measurements eight days apart, and `sendPushToUser`'s only caller was its own test route. Deleted whole — `lib/push.ts`, `lib/push-client.ts`, both `/api/push/*` routes, the settings toggle, the table, and the service worker's `push`/`notificationclick` handlers. **Do not re-add it:** every notification this app actually sends is native Android or a Capacitor local notification, neither of which goes through a service worker, and there is still no cron layer to trigger a server-side send (§0). |
