## 11. UI primitives, hooks & background system

**Before writing a tab strip, dialog, empty state, collapsible, sparkline, or carousel,
check `components/ui/` first** (`CLAUDE.md` visual-consistency rules).

| Primitive | File |
|---|---|
| Home greeting / the name to show for a profile user | `homeGreeting(user, tz)`, `profileDisplayName(user)` (`packages/shared/src/greeting.ts`) — `displayName?.trim() \|\| name?.trim()`, whole, then the time-of-day period. Used by the current app and Preview, and by every place that shows a profile user's name (a bare `displayName ?? name` keeps a whitespace-only name); never re-implement in either (#2904). |
| Button (tap-target floors live here) | `components/ui/button.tsx` |
| Progress bar fill | `components/ui/progress-fill.tsx` → `ProgressFill` (RV-72). Renders the **fill only**, at `transform: scaleX(pct)` with `origin-left` — never `width`, which forces layout every frame *and* reflows the label and numbers beside the bar. It deliberately does NOT own the track: each caller's track already carries `role="progressbar"` and its ARIA values, its own background, a height from 1.5 to 2.5 and in one case an absolutely-positioned tick. **The track must set `overflow-hidden rounded-full`** — `scaleX` scales the fill's own radius horizontally, so a rounded fill goes oval at low percentages; the fill stays square and the track clips it. **One deliberate non-caller:** `components/nutrition/calorie-progress-bar.tsx` stays on `transition-[width]` because its fill clips a gradient ramp with a hand-computed `backgroundSize`, and scaling it would change which colour the leading edge shows. Pinned by `e2e/rv72-progress-bars-composite.spec.ts` (computed style, not class strings) and the exclusion by `e2e/calorie-progress-bar.spec.ts`. |
| Sheet / Dialog / Confirm / Popover / Collapsible | `sheet.tsx`, `dialog.tsx`, `confirm-dialog.tsx`, `popover.tsx`, `collapsible.tsx` |
| Tabs / segmented | `segmented-tabs.tsx`, `tab-panels.tsx` |
| Sparkline | `sparkline.tsx` (SVG; geometry in `sparkline-geometry.ts` → `sparklinePoints`, extracted so the projection is testable in node), `sparkline-chart.tsx` (**chart.js — never import it into a hot top-level screen**). **`valuePadding` defaults to 0.5 and that changes what a chart says**: on a 0.5 kg spread it halves the visible amplitude, so pass `0` for exact min/max. Other props, all defaulted so the call sites predating them are untouched: `pad` (uniform inset on both axes), `strokeWidth`, `gridLines`, `emphasizeLast`, `valueLabel`. Q-154 converted `exercise-history-sheet` and `health-metric-sheet` onto it; **`workout/active-workout-screen` stays inline on purpose** — asymmetric padding, uniform dots, no fill, a dimmed stroke and an end-anchored label are four more props no other caller would use. The three time-axis charts (`day-sections`, `exercise-review-sheet`, `body-battery-card`) are EXEMPT because they project x by *time*. `scripts/check-sparkline-primitive.js` holds the line. **The 14-day health trend chart is a separate component** — `components/health/trend-sparkline.tsx` (chart.js, lazy via `trend-sparkline-lazy.tsx`, 11 call sites across heart-rate, sleep, nutrition, workout-density and the score details). How it draws a day with no reading lives in `trend-sparkline-gaps.ts` → `gapDataset` (TN-53): it never spans a gap, dots any reading whose neighbours are both absent, and returns an "N days missing" note. `spanGaps` is typed to the literal `false` there so reverting it cannot be a one-character edit in the component. **The 24-hour HR chart is a third thing again** — `components/health/hr-day-chart.tsx` (chart.js, minutes-into-day x axis, band shading via a `beforeDatasetsDraw` plugin). TN-3b added the day's stress on a hidden second scale; the projection is `hr-stress-overlay.ts` → `stressOverlay`, which reuses `body-battery/stress-day.ts`'s `toSegments` rather than re-splitting gaps. |
| A day's timeline (client, any date) | `lib/hooks/use-day-timeline.ts` → `useDayTimeline(date)`, `dayTimelineKey(date)`, `DAY_TIMELINE_KEY_PREFIX`. The key is `home-day-timeline:<date>` — a CHILD of Home's bare key **on purpose**: `invalidateCache` matches `key LIKE 'prefix%'`, so the six groups already clearing `home-day-timeline` cover it for free. A fresh `day-timeline:` prefix would be a second invalidation contract six writers must remember. Pinned by `stress-day-timeline-key.test.ts`. |
| Stress read against a day's events (TN-35) | `components/body-battery/stress-at-events.ts` → `placeEvents`, `levelAt`, `measuredCount`. Returns `null` when no bucket sits within `BUCKET_HALF_MIN` of the event — **absent, never calm**; a zero there is an invented number. Excludes the dead `tag` lane. |
| The user's HR profile (server) | `packages/shared/src/health/hr-profile.ts` → `resolveHrProfile(repo, userId, tz)`. The ONE resolver for max HR, target anchor and resting HR — it replaced three that disagreed, and `maxHr` (the ceiling for effort-%) is deliberately a different number from `targetAnchorMax` (the anchor for reachable targets). The observed half comes from **`repo.getObservedHrProfile(userId, from, to)`** (`lib/data/postgres/slices/oura.ts`), which computes the corroboration-gated min/max/mean over a window **in SQL, returning one row** — RV-181, where fetching the 90-day window instead was 51% of all database time. It reproduces `computeObservedHr` exactly, chest-strap bucket merge included, and `lib/data/postgres/__tests__/observed-hr-sql-equivalence.test.ts` runs both paths over the same rows to keep it that way. **Need order statistics over an HR window? Call that, never `getHrForWindow` + `computeObservedHr`** — the rows are only for callers that need the series itself (a chart, zone-minute accumulation, per-workout recovery). Resting HR is a FIXED 28-day mean, never the caller's range. |
| The resting HR that HR zones are built from (#3216) | `packages/shared/src/health/resting-hr-mean.ts` → **`meanRestingHr(rows)`** over **`restingHrWindow(todayIso)`** (today and the 28 days before it). The ONE resting-HR mean: `resolveHrProfile` counts zone minutes with it, the running plan's `resolveSnapshot` builds its zones and run targets from it, and `/api/cardio-week` takes the profile's value and uses the same function for its 30-day now-vs-prior card delta. Readings ≤ 0 are no reading; none gives null, and callers default to 60. Order-independent. The newest single night is `latestRestingHr` (`latest-resting-hr.ts`), which only readiness's last-night pick reads. |
| Which device sources a user has connected (ring / strap / Health Connect) | `packages/shared/src/health/connected-sources.ts` → `connectedSources(facts)` + `ConnectedSources`; served as the OPTIONAL `connectedSources` field of the readiness payload (`lib/health/readiness-payload.ts`). **Absent means unknown, never false** (a cached older payload lacks it; so does a build whose lookup failed). Facts, no new detector: `ring` = `repo.hasOuraBleSamples` (raw BLE samples exist, any age); `strap` = an `oura_heartrate` row with `source='chest_strap'` in the last `CONNECTED_SOURCE_WINDOW_DAYS` (30) days (strap pairing is phone-only, so this means *strap-sourced heart rate seen recently*, not *paired now*); `healthConnect` = a `health_connect` heart-rate row or a `health_connect_intervals` row in that window. The two windowed facts come from `repo.getRecentSourceFacts(userId, since)`: one `EXISTS` each, scoped by `user_id`. Booleans only, no timestamps. `ouraCloud` = an `oura_tokens` row with an access token (`repo.getOuraCloudTokens`), read by both phones' Health Connect imports for the origin filter below (#3115). No UI reads it yet (empty-state copy naming the device is a follow-up to issue 2613) |
| Health Connect records that copy a first-hand source (origin denylist) | `packages/shared/src/health/health-connect-origin.ts` → `FirstHandFilter` (the one gate: `admit`/`keep`, counts per type, a record counts once per pass), `knownFirstHandSources(connectedSources, payloadDate, today)` (the one cold-start rule: known only from today's readiness payload, unknown keeps Oura's copies), `isFirstHandCopy`, `dropFirstHandCopies`, `OURA_APP_PACKAGE`. While `connectedSources.ouraCloud` is true, a record whose `metadata.dataOrigin` is `com.ouraring.oura` is left out before upload, whatever its type; any other app, or no origin, is kept. Legacy client: every record read goes through `readFiltered` in `lib/health-connect-sync.ts` (the only `readRecords` call; a test fails on a second), flag from `cachedFirstHandSources(tz)`, counts in `SyncResult.skippedFirstHand`, kept per sync by `rememberSyncSkipped`/`readLastSyncSkipped`; skipped windows are read again by a cold sync once Oura reads as disconnected (`SKIPPED_SINCE_KEY`). Preview: `HealthConnectImportStore.append` is the only way a record reaches the upload queue, flag from `loadFirstHandSources` (`mobile/src/features/health/data/first-hand-sources.ts`), per-sync counts in `skippedFirstHand`, a rescan from `firstHandSince` once disconnected. Daily aggregates (steps, distance, total kcal) cannot be split by app and are not filtered. Oura only: Renpho and Polar join once their ids are read from a device (#3115, #3197) |
| The user's HR profile (client) | `lib/hooks/use-hr-profile.ts` → `useHrProfile()`. Owns the `hr-profile` key, the URL and `HR_PROFILE_TTL` for the workout flow (RV-64). **`LiveHrChart` takes the profile as a PROP and must not read it** — the active workout renders that chart on `workoutPhase === "rest"`, so a read of its own ran once per rest period, ~20 times in a 5x4 workout, against a 20-per-60s route limit. The screens that stay mounted call the hook. **⚠ Not a `freshWithinTtl` candidate:** the payload is a 90-day observation window, and live BLE samples land in that window during the workout with nothing in `lib/live-hr/**` invalidating anything — only `invalidateOuraSync` and `invalidateBodyMetricWrite` clear the key. (RV-181 moved the computation into SQL; it did **not** change what the key depends on, so this still stands.) Pinned by `rv64-hr-profile-read-is-hoisted.test.ts`. |
| A day's stress series (client) | `lib/hooks/use-stress-day.ts` → `useStressDay(day)`. Owns the `stress-day:<day>` key, the URL and `BODY_BATTERY_TTL` for every reader — the standalone strip (`body-battery/stress-day-chart.tsx`) and the HR chart's parents. Returns `{ data, failed }`; `failed` exists because `cachedFetch` swallows `!res.ok`, this route's own rate limit included. |
| Collection art (sprites, coats, scenes) | Generated by `scripts/collection-art/build.mjs` into `public/cats/`; `preview.mjs` renders a gallery. Paths via `classArt(cls, tier, variant)` / `tierArt(faucet, tier, variant)` in `packages/shared/src/collection/sprites.ts`. Preview bundles the still pose of the four ladder classes and the meadow backdrop via `mobile/scripts/build-cat-art.mjs` (a test fails if `catArt.generated.ts` drifts from `public/cats/`). Inventory: `docs/domains/app-shell/cat-collection-design-catalog.md`. |
| Collection cat names | `packages/shared/src/collection/names.ts` → `spawnName(id)`, `blendName(oldest, newest)`. Deterministic; the lineage fold in `ladder.ts` names every cat with them, so a name is the same on every read and device. |
| Collection pen (Home card) | `components/home/collection-pen.tsx` → `CollectionPen({ collections, scene })`: every held cat wandering over a backdrop scene; the motion is CSS (`ta-pen-*` in `globals.css`) plus each SVG's own tail/paw/blink loop. Selection is the pure `penCats`, and where each cat walks is the pure `penCatMotion`, both in `packages/shared/src/collection/pen.ts` (Preview animates the same numbers in `mobile/src/features/collection/components/CollectionPen.tsx`). |
| Collection cat sprite (BF-126) | `components/home/cat-sprite.tsx` → `CatSprite({ faucet, tier, size })`. Draws `public/cats/<class>-<tier>.svg` and falls back to the glyph on a failed load, including one that failed before hydration. The art is generated: edit `scripts/collection-art/cat.mjs` / `scripts/collection-art/gear.mjs` and re-run `build.mjs`; a test fails on drift. |
| Swipe carousel | `swipe-carousel.tsx` (+ `swipe-carousel-math.ts`) — prefer over hand-rolled swipe |
| Swipe-to-reveal row actions | `swipe-actions.tsx` (+ `swipe-actions-math.ts`) — `useDrag`, x-locked, `touch-action: pan-y`; tray is `aria-hidden` when closed, so every action it holds must also be reachable without the gesture |
| Carousel dot indicators | `carousel-dots.tsx` — the dots under a swipe carousel. Owns the touch area (`tap-target-dot`) and the 24 px pitch that keeps neighbouring hit areas disjoint; never hand-roll the row (2026-08-09, Q-160) |
| Small-control touch areas | `.tap-target-dot` (24×44, dot rows) and `.tap-target-44` (44×44, isolated controls) in `app/globals.css`, next to the `.tap-dense` floor opt-out. **Which one — or neither — depends on the clearance to the nearest interactive neighbour**: a hit area wider than that gap steals the neighbour's taps (Q-160, Q-176) |
| Multi-select chips | `chip-group.tsx` (`ChipGroup`) — the pill row. An option is a plain string (its own value and label) or `{ value, label }` when the value is an **id the user must never see**; `selected` is always values. Used by the meal-plan wizard's six chip rows and the meal builder's tag picker (`components/nutrition/meal-type-tags.tsx`) |
| Weight dial | `weight-dial.tsx`, `weight-dial-modal.tsx` |
| Empty state / skeleton / switch / select / input / textarea / label / avatar / scroll-area | `components/ui/*.tsx` |
| Bottom action bar | `bottom-action-bar.tsx` |
| **Status-bar scrim (DV-6)** | `components/shell/status-bar-scrim.tsx` (`StatusBarScrim`, rendered ONCE by `TabShell`) + `lib/shell/status-bar-scrim-controller.ts`. A gradient fades in behind the status bar when the panel on show is scrolled, so content no longer runs through the clock. **Do not add a per-screen scrim** — the shell already covers every tab, which is the whole point: a per-screen rule is one every future screen can forget. It listens in the **capture** phase because this app scrolls inner containers, never the document, and `scroll` does not bubble; it scopes events with `[data-tab-active="true"]`, which `TabShell` sets. Fades from `var(--background)`, never `--page-bg` (`DynamicBackground` sets that to `transparent`). Height is the `pt-safe` utility — referencing `--pt-safe-value` by hand fails the Custom Rules safe-area check. The decision logic is a plain `.ts` module because the vitest projects cannot transform `.tsx` |
| Tab route `loading.tsx` boundary | `components/shell/tab-loading.tsx` (`TabLoading`) — shared skeleton for the five tab routes' `loading.tsx` files (covers cold route entries only; warm tab switches never hit these). A loading.tsx also covers every pushed route under its segment, so the bottom nav is drawn only when the route is a tab (#2441). A code-split tab is `createPreloadedTab` in `components/shell/preloaded-tab.tsx`: renders the screen directly once the idle warm-up has fetched its chunk (#2442) |
| Persistent tab shell | `components/shell/tab-shell.tsx` (`TabShell` — keeps all five tab trees mounted, switching = visibility flip, no router nav) + `components/shell/tab-page.tsx` (`TabPage` — the shared server wrapper every tab route renders) + `components/shell/tab-visibility.tsx` (`useTabVisibility` `{visible, epoch}` — re-show refresh signal; `useRefreshOnTabShow(fn)` — runs `fn` on every re-show, never first mount, for leaf cards with their own load function. **Any `useEffect(…, [])` fetch inside a tab runs once per app launch** — use one of these two or it never refreshes) + `lib/shell-nav.ts` (`navigateToTab` — shell-aware cross-tab navigation, router fallback outside the shell). Tab identity/keys: `components/shell/tabs.ts` (`TabKey`, `hrefForTab`, `tabKeyForHref`). `bottom-nav.tsx` drives the shell via `onTabChange` when mounted inside it, else falls back to `<Link>`/router (admin, `/workout-select`, error pages) |
| Color / theme pickers | `color-swatch-picker.tsx`, `components/theme-color-picker.tsx`, `packages/shared/src/brand-themes.ts`, `packages/shared/src/session-palette.ts` |

| Hook | File |
|---|---|
| Count-up animation | `lib/hooks/use-count-up.ts` |
| Back / fallback nav | `lib/hooks/use-back-or-fallback.ts` |
| Sheet back-dismiss / **the overlay registry** | `lib/hooks/use-sheet-back-dismiss.ts` (React wiring) → `lib/hooks/sheet-back-stack.ts` (**the decision logic — when to close, whether a pop is one of ours, how deep a surface sits**; module-level stack and self-pop counter, unit-tested against the sibling/nested/StrictMode sequences). **This IS the app's open-overlay registry — do not build a second one.** It is entered from `components/ui/back-dismiss.tsx` (`BackDismiss`), which **both** `SheetContent` and `DialogContent` already render, so all 52 overlay call sites are covered with no per-site wiring. `hasOpenSurface()` answers *is anything open* (BF-166). **⚠ Searching for `overlayStack`/`topOverlay`/`openOverlay` returns nothing and is a FALSE NEGATIVE** — the names are `openSurface`/`closeSurface`; one entry was filed on that grep proposing to build what already exists. |
| Roving tabindex + arrow keys for a `role="radiogroup"` | `lib/hooks/use-roving-radio-group.ts` — a hook, not a component: the 8 call sites render 5 different shapes, so what they share is behaviour. Selection is delegated by clicking the target, so each site keeps its own semantics (Q-350). |
| Pin a scroll panel to its newest content | `lib/hooks/use-scroll-to-bottom.ts` — ref goes on the `overflow-y-auto` element. **Never `scrollIntoView` a sentinel child**: it scrolls every scrollable ancestor including the document, so an appending panel drags the whole page (Q-532). |
| Edit/delete a day's logged exercise, session or activity | `lib/hooks/use-day-entry-mutations.ts` — the four write handlers plus their dialog state, shared by `/health/day` and `health-content.tsx` so there is one write path per domain (LB-1). Each mirrors into the local store and clears the caches **before** the caller refetches; `onChanged(date)` is that refetch. |
| Render a whole day's read-through (training · activity · energy · sleep · HR · body) | `components/health/day-detail/day-read-through.tsx` — the section stack itself, props-in and fetch-free, rendered by BOTH `/health/day` and the evening wrap-up's step 1 off the same `day-log:<date>` cache key (Q-112b). Edit/delete controls are optional; absent renders read-only, which is what lets the wrap-up host it without a second write path. **Do not add a fetch to it** — the two hosts need different strategies (`/health/day` guards responses against a swipe that has moved on; the wrap-up is one day and uses `useCachedValue`). |
| An exercise's clip and start frame | `lib/hooks/use-exercise-media.ts` → `useExerciseMedia(names)` for a set, `useExerciseMediaFor(name)` for one. **The only place `/api/exercise-gif` is fetched** — it replaced four hand-rolled copies rather than becoming a fifth (BF-65), and a guard test fails on a new one. The shared `exercise-media:<name>` key is the point: the warm-up screen fetches every exercise in the session and then unmounts, and the per-exercise ready screen paints its entry from cache instead of a spinner. `prefetchBinaries` pulls the files themselves so the service worker holds them offline. **Anything rendering one through `next/image` must pass `unoptimized` for a `.gif`** — the optimizer returns a still, so it appears, looks correct, and never moves. |
| Weather | `lib/weather/use-weather.ts` |
| PiP mode / actions | `hooks/use-pip-mode.ts`, `hooks/use-pip-actions.ts` |
| Health calcs | `app/health/hooks/use-health-calcs.ts` |
| Re-derive a stored scale reading at a different profile | **`recomputeStoredBodyFatPctAtHeight()`** / **`heightUsedForStoredBmr()`** (`lib/scale-ble/composition.ts`) — composition is computed once at ingest, so a later height/DOB/sex correction never reaches stored rows. `bmr_kcal` has no impedance term and is linear in height, so it gives the height back; impedance then follows from the stored body fat. Only the INVERSE lives there — the forward half calls `computeBodyComposition` (RV-165) |
| Online/offline status | **`useOnlineStatus()`** (`lib/use-online-status.ts`) — DOM events + Capacitor Network, **ANDed with whether requests are actually completing**. It answers *"can the app reach the server"*, not *"is the radio attached"*: both native sources report true in low reception, which is the state a gym is in (BF-195) |
| "Are requests getting through?" | **`requestsCompleting()` / `subscribeToReachability()`** (`lib/sqlite/cache.ts`) — one flag, flipped false by a fetch **timeout** and true by any settled response. A rejected response still counts (a 500 proves throughput); an ordinary network throw does NOT (that is server-down, not no-reception). Do not add a second connectivity source — read this one |

| Cross-cutting UI | File |
|---|---|
| Haptics | `lib/haptics.ts` (`hapticTick`, `hapticLight`, `hapticSuccess`) |
| Class merge | `packages/shared/src/utils.ts` → `cn(...)` |
| Fetch + toast / retry | `packages/shared/src/ui/fetch-with-toast.ts`, `packages/shared/src/fetch-with-retry.ts` → `fetchWithRetry(key, url, ttl, onData, isCancelled, attempt?, fetchFn?, opts?)`. Three bounded retries (2.5s/5s/7.5s) when neither cache nor a 200 produced a response. **`opts.onExhausted` is how a caller learns it gave up (RV-85)** — before it fires, an absent value means in-flight; after, it means failed. Without it the helper fixed the transient case and silently accepted the persistent one, landing on the blank widget its own header says it exists to prevent. Any surface that gates a whole row on the fetched value needs it, or a failure renders nothing at all. |
| Navigation transitions | `lib/navigate-with-transition.ts` |
| Dynamic background system | `components/dynamic-background/*` + `lib/background/` (`day-phase.ts`, `palettes.ts`, `screen-palettes.ts`, `weather-filters.ts`) — screens go through `bg-page`, never opaque per-screen paint |
| Toasts | `components/ui/sonner.tsx` |
| Offline shell | `public/sw-template.js` (precache/fallback logic) + `lib/sw/manifest.ts` (pure precache-manifest builder, unit-tested) + `app/sw.js/route.ts` (injects the per-build manifest + cache name); `app/offline/page.tsx` is the precached unauthenticated fallback (must stay logic-free — no auth/data fetches); `components/shell/offline-indicator.tsx` is the "Offline — showing saved data" pill; `components/more/sw-status-row.tsx` is the diagnostics row (More → About) |

| **Whether an `<Image>` may use Next's optimizer** | `packages/shared/src/media/private-media.ts` → `mustBypassImageOptimizer(src)` / `PRIVATE_MEDIA_PREFIX`. Everything under `/exercise-media/` is behind `middleware.ts`'s session gate, and the optimizer fetches its source **server-side with no cookie** — so it is redirected to `/sign-in`, gets HTML, and answers 400, which draws as a broken image. Measured 2026-09-24 (DV-18): the direct URL is a 307, `/_next/image?url=%2Fexercise-media%2F…` a 400. It fails before storage is consulted, so the stored bytes are irrelevant. **Folds in the GIF rule** (the optimizer serves a still frame of an animation) so one predicate answers both: the previous shape, `unoptimized={src.endsWith('.gif')}` copied to six call sites, is exactly what let every non-GIF private URL through — and `mediaKey` writes start/end frames as `.png`, which `exercise-media-panel.tsx` falls back to, so it was user-facing in the workout screen. Use it at **every** `<Image>` whose src can be app-served media. |
| **The real type of an uploaded image** | `packages/shared/src/http/request-guards.ts` → `sniffImageMime(bytes)`, returning an `AllowedImageMime` or null from the magic bytes (PNG's 8-byte header, JPEG's `FF D8 FF`, WebP's RIFF + `WEBP` form type). Distinct from `isAllowedImageMime`, which validates a **declared** type — useless where nothing declares honestly: `/api/admin/reference-figure` stored every upload as `image/png` whatever arrived, and the phone feeding it shoots HEIC. Tied to `ALLOWED_IMAGE_MIME` rather than to string literals, so the two cannot drift. Serve side: `downloadMediaWithType` (`lib/exercise-storage.ts`) returns the stored `ContentType` so the proxy serves what was written instead of guessing from the extension. |

| **Which model generation a stored score came from** | `packages/shared/src/health/model-generation.ts` → `modelGeneration(version)` / `generationCensus(versions)` (LA-135). `body_battery_daily.model_version` is written as `v6:rest0.05:chg0.12:…` — a generation prefix plus the constants that generation ran with — and until LA-135 **nothing read it**. Compares the PREFIX ONLY: the constants change on every tuning pass (TN-55 moved three inside v6's string), so whole-string comparison would call each tuning a model change. Measured 2026-09-24 the table held four generations, and v4 → v5 moves the mean end-of-day value 62.9 → 15.2 — a correlation across that is a correlation with a code change. Used by `/api/admin/battery-recovery-calibration`, which reports the census beside its figure and adds a per-generation calibration when the window spans a boundary. **It does NOT narrow the window** — a filter that quietly drops days answers a question nobody asked; an absent version buckets as `unknown` rather than disappearing. |

## 12. Activity / GPS / weather

| Concern | Where |
|---|---|
| **Gait & movement domain map + mission** (steps ↔ walk/run detection share one ring gait signal) | [`docs/gait-movement-domain.md`](gait-movement-domain.md) — read before touching steps OR walk/run detection; the shared cadence/periodicity discriminator (`gaitBandAutocorr` + decoded `stride_frequency`) is One-Formula-One-Place across both |
| **Which planned meals a one-tap day-fill may log** | `components/nutrition/plan-day-fill.ts` → `fillableMeals()` / `planMealHour()` / `hourFromTzDatetime()` (Q-187 step 4). The one place that decides what a *"log the meals so far"* press writes, and it is **bounded by the clock on today** — the property Q-187 exists to protect is that the day's totals never count food nobody ate, and a button that logs the whole day at 9am hands that straight back. Past days offer everything (retrospective logging); future days offer nothing; already-logged, declined and ingredient-less meals are skipped, and an empty result means the control is hidden rather than disabled. `planMealHour` resolves `suggestedTime` before the meal type's start hour, and a meal it cannot place is not offered on today. Consumed by `components/nutrition/meal-plan-section.tsx`, which derives the list itself from the same `variant` it renders — deriving it in the caller would let the button offer a meal the list is not showing. |
| **Guided walk pacer (which signal paces, and how it is doing)** | `lib/walk/walk-pacer.ts` — the ONE place the interval walk decides what to pace you by and what band you are in (Q-410). `readPacer()` walks a precedence ladder **cadence → speed → heart rate** and returns the band, a mark, a sentence, a bar fill and a `fallbackNote` naming the rung, so the screen never silently changes which signal it means. `bandFor()` adds an amber ring around `classifyZone`'s green/not-green threshold rather than restating it, and bands by **signed** distance — on a fast block, faster than the floor stays green however far above. `speedTargetsFromHistory()` derives the speed rung's pair from the walker's own past fast/slow segments (`/api/guided-walk/segment-stats`, already fetched by `walk-config.tsx`) instead of asking for a third target block; it returns null rather than a half-pair on a thin, pace-less or non-separating history. `STOPPED_SPM`/`STOPPED_KMH` exist because "under the ceiling" would otherwise make standing still a perfect slow block. Rendered by the leaf `components/guided-walk/walk-pacer-bar.tsx`, which owns its own cadence subscription so a 1 Hz strap reading does not re-render the route map. ⚠ the cadence and HR rungs are device-gated — no BLE in the sandbox; `e2e/walk-pacer-speed-rung.spec.ts` covers the speed rung. |
| **Guided walk Exit (how the back gesture reaches the walk's one prompt)** | `lib/walk/walk-exit.ts` — a two-function registry (`registerWalkExit` / `requestWalkExit`), not store state (#2134). The walk is immersive: no tab bar, one **Exit walk** button, and the prompt (`components/guided-walk/leave-walk-dialog.tsx`, mounted once in `walk-active.tsx` because that screen holds the HR samples and cadence a save needs). `MobileAuthHandler`'s `backButton` listener lives outside that tree, so it calls `requestWalkExit()` and the walk screen opens the same prompt the button does. It returns `false` when nothing is mounted (config, summary, the route's error boundary) and the listener then falls through to its ordinary back — which is why this is a registry and not a flag, which would be set and never read. Do not mount a second `LeaveWalkDialog`: two copies are how the button and the gesture offered different choices |
| **Guided walk save (one row per walk, DV-19 ③ #2118)** | `lib/stores/guided-walk-store.ts` → `walkId` (minted by `start`, persisted) + `claimWalkSave()` / `releaseWalkSave()`. `components/guided-walk/walk-summary.tsx` claims before it writes and writes the local row under `walkId`; a remount on a walk still `'done'` (Back off the summary, then the walk route again) finds the claim taken and writes nothing. The claim is handed back only when BOTH the local write and the web POST failed | **Never guard the save with a ref or component state** — those are per mount, and the remount has lost the samples and elapsed time, so its write was a 0-minute row that the outbox replay (last-write-wins on `(user, date, start_time)`) laid over the real walk. Pinned by `components/guided-walk/__tests__/dv19-walk-summary-saves-once.test.tsx`, which mounts the real component |
| GPS route math and the saved route fields | `packages/shared/src/activity/` — `route-metrics.ts` (haversine, distance, splits, best efforts, pace series, elevation, average pace), `route-encoding.ts` (`RoutePoint`, Douglas-Peucker `simplifyRoute`, the polyline codec, written out to match `@mapbox/polyline` byte for byte) and `route-summary.ts` (`summariseRoute(points, paceElapsedSec)`: the nine route fields an activity log saves, null under two points; splits and an average slower than `MAX_PACE_SEC_PER_KM` are left out and pace-series buckets are clamped to it, so a pause never fails the save schema and the scrub marker stays aligned). `route-summary.ts` also holds `averagePaceSecPerKm`, the one capped average-pace rule: `summariseRoute`, a guided walk's per-segment pace (`lib/walk/segment-stats.ts`, #3128) and the server's derivation of a pace a client left out (#3120) all use it. Moved from `lib/activity/` for TrainingAi Preview (#2845); `lib/activity/activity-metrics.ts` and `route-encoding.ts` re-export them. The current app's `activity-store.ts` finish, its guided walk's `walk-summary.tsx` and Preview's `ActivityStore.sessionPayload` all call `summariseRoute`, each passing the activity's active (unpaused) seconds as the required pace denominator, the same time its duration counts (#3092); splits and the pace series stay on GPS fix time. |
| Preview's GPS recording | `mobile/src/features/activities/data/route-recording.ts` (`keepsFix`, the current app's 5 m filter; `gpsStatus` → paused, searching, tracking, lost, denied, disabled, unavailable, failed; `liveRouteStats`, null distance before the first fix; `watchRoute`, the watcher and the readout clock, set at start and on every fix), `useRouteRecording.ts` (a thin React wrapper over `watchRoute`; foreground only, stops on pause), `ActivityStore` (`appendFix`, `checkpoint` every 5 s and on pause, finish and backgrounding, under `activities.route.v1`, tied to the recording's `startMs`), and `mobile/src/integrations/location/route-location.native.ts` (expo-location). Saved through the existing `/api/sync/push` outbox. No background recording until #2846. |
| Activity tracking, metrics, blending | `lib/activity/` — `activity-metrics.ts`, `blend-activity.ts`, `treadmill-utils.ts`, `gps-tracking.ts`, `route-encoding.ts`, `motion-detection.ts`, `motion-gate.ts`, `auto-detection-service.ts`, `detection-thresholds.ts`, `location-permission.ts`, `gps-watchdog.ts` (pure `evaluateWatchdog` — timer-independent GPS force-stop: absolute watcher cap, hard probe cap, data-driven stall, and a no-fix end for a confirmed session that never got a GPS point (#2477); evaluated on GPS points/gate ticks/app resume so it fires with the screen off). **Since #2471 the phone's motion sensor is the only live trigger**: the service no longer subscribes to the ring-gate feed, because the ring delivers those windows in hourly drains (median 33 min old) and every one was read as "now"; AD-2's ring-cadence confirm and `gait-confirm.ts` went with it. A session is confirmed by GPS speed and notified by the AD-1 distance/elapsed gate. **Walks the ring saw are offered after the fact**: `packages/shared/src/health/walk-detection.ts` → `detectWalksFromMet(grid, exclusions)` (5 of any 6 minutes above 2.0 MET, gaps ≤ 3 min merged, ≥ 7 min, never inside sleep, lifting or a logged activity) over the ring's own MET grid, served by `GET /api/activity-detection/walks` (computed on read, today only) and fetched by `lib/activity/ring-walks.ts` → `offerRingWalks()` whenever new Oura data reaches the app (`OURA_DATA_CHANGED_EVENT` in `lib/oura-data-changed.ts`, the `ta:oura-ble-synced` event: fired when a Bluetooth drain settles and, since #3077, by `invalidatePulledDomains` after a sync pull carrying Oura-fed rows) and when detection starts. The store's `offerRingWalks` adds each as a `source: 'ring'` pending session (no route, no distance) unless already answered (`answeredRingWalks`, 3 days) or overlapping one on the card; the card shows the owner's wording, "Looks like you walked 9:10–9:32. Save it?". `gate-feed.ts` still feeds the Guided Walk cadence tracker, which now fetches the decoder table itself. See `docs/gait-movement-domain.md`. |
| **Walk-detection funnel telemetry (#2478)** — candidate, confirmed, notified, offered, saved, dismissed | `lib/activity/detection-events.ts` → `recordDetectionEvent(detectionId, kind, gate, detail)` (queues in a capped localStorage outbox, debounced flush, never throws) and `flushDetectionEvents()` (on detection start and resume; 2xx and 400 clear a batch, anything else keeps it) → `POST /api/activity-detection/events` (session auth, 30/min, ≤50 events, `.strict()` Zod in `packages/shared/src/validation/detection-event.ts`) → `detection_events` (`claude_ro.detection_events`), insert-or-ignore on `(user_id, detection_id, kind)` so retries are no-ops. Callers: `auto-detection-service.ts` (candidate at probe start, confirmed at session start, notified, probe-ended dismissals via `probeEndGate`), `auto-detection-store.ts` (`endSession`'s quality gates → dismissed/offered, `discardSession`, the card's `dismissSession`) and `exercise-review-sheet.tsx` (saved/dismissed `user_review`). **Read-only telemetry: nothing reads it back, and no gate or threshold changed.** One detection = one GPS probe; the gate names per kind are listed in `DETECTION_GATES`. Server-only table by design: the device never reads these rows, so there is no local-store domain, only the outbox |
| Location | `lib/location.ts` |
| Weather (Open-Meteo) | `lib/weather/` — `open-meteo.ts`, `geocode.ts`, `use-weather.ts`, `types.ts` The WMO code → condition table is `packages/shared/src/weather/weather-condition.ts` (`weatherCondition`, `WEATHER_CONDITIONS`), shared with Preview's own Open-Meteo client (#3041). |
| Route map HR-zone coloring | `packages/shared/src/activity/route-hr-zones.ts` (`buildRouteZoneSegments`; moved from `lib/activity/` for TrainingAi Preview, #2845, and re-exported there) — splits a route into runs of consecutive points sharing one HR zone color, since the encoded polyline format drops per-point timestamps: correlates along-route distance → elapsed time, then elapsed time → a wall-clock HR reading. Prefers the real `paceSeries` via `packages/shared/src/activity/scrub.ts`'s `estimateTimeAtDistanceKm` (also moved for #2845; `lib/activity/scrub.ts` re-exports it) (the exact inverse of `estimateDistanceKmAtTime`, which the map's scrub marker already uses); falls back to assuming constant pace across the whole route (using just start/end time) when no pace series was captured — covers older logs and passively-detected sessions, which never have one. Zone colors come from `packages/shared/src/health/hr-zones.ts` (`HR_ZONE_META`/`computeHrZones`/`zoneForBpm`) — no second palette. Returns `null` (flat single-color line) only when HR readings are missing, or neither a pace series nor an end time exists. **Any caller building the `startTime`/`endTime` strings must construct them via the multi-argument `Date` constructor (or an already-absolute `Z`-suffixed ISO string) — a bare no-offset string handed to `new Date()` is ambiguous across JS engines and can silently collapse the whole route to one color (see the 2026-07-29 fix entry).** Wired into `activity-detail-sheet.tsx`, `done-activity-screen.tsx`, and `exercise-review-sheet.tsx`, and into Preview's `RouteMap` (`mobile/src/features/activities/components/RouteMap.tsx`) on its activity detail, which fits the route into its box with `route-shape.ts` and follows the HR chart's selected moment with the same scrub helpers. |
| Route map tiles (Leaflet + Thunderforest/OSM) | `packages/shared/src/map-tiles.ts` (`getTileProvider()`), `components/activity/activity-route-map.tsx`. **The Leaflet attribution control is currently hidden** (`attributionControl={false}`) at the owner's explicit request — this is a deferred license-compliance gap, not an oversight: OpenStreetMap's ODbL and Thunderforest's ToS (even on the free Hobby tier this app uses) both require visible on-map attribution, acceptable to skip only because this app is personal-use-only right now. **Tracked in [`docs/public-launch-checklist.md`](public-launch-checklist.md) — must be restored (or replaced with a compliant collapsed/info-icon treatment) before any public release.** Both `img-src` and `connect-src` CSP directives must list any tile domain used (see the 2026-07-28 blank-tile-map entries in `docs/overview/entries/` for why both are required — a service worker's own re-fetch of a tile request is governed by `connect-src`, separately from the `<img>` element's `img-src`).

## 13. Types & validation

| Concern | Where |
|---|---|
| Shared domain types | `packages/shared/src/types/` (program, progression, nutrition, body, mood, injury, supplement, user, log, friends, ai-periodization, goal-recommendation, builder, day-checkin) |
| Zod validators | `packages/shared/src/validation/` (`body-metrics.ts`, `day-checkin.ts`, `session-rpe.ts`, `generated-program.ts`) and `packages/shared/src/validators/` |
| "Does this payload say anything?" | `packages/shared/src/validation/day-checkin.ts` → `dayCheckinHasAnswers(body)` (Q-465). A shape-valid body can still carry no information: `{}` used to write a check-in row with every metric null, indistinguishable from one where the user answered nothing. Counts the ten scales, an illness context, a non-blank journal, a non-empty sore-muscle list — **not** `phase`/`date` (addressing) or the `*Touched` flags (metadata about a prefill). Used by the route **and** the `pushMutations` branch, because the outbox reaches the same table. If another domain grows the same problem, put its predicate beside its schema the same way. |
| Cross-field plausibility (ingest) | `packages/shared/src/validation/plausibility.ts` — the one place a payload's fields are checked *against each other*, which per-field Zod bounds cannot express: `activityImplausibleReason` (speed/kcal/steps per minute, HR ordering; also used by `fitness-tests` after unit conversion), `sleepImplausibleReason` (stage total vs the night's span), `oneRmImplausible`, `rrContradictsBpm` (a chest-strap packet's RR set vs its own bpm). Every threshold is deliberately generous — a rejection means a decode fault or unit mix-up, never a hard session. **Reuse these; do not restate a bound.** |
| Ingest clock reconciliation | `packages/shared/src/validation/ingest-clock.ts` — `resolveMeasuredAt` clamps a client-supplied timestamp to a ±window around server time, falling back to server time rather than rejecting the reading. Sibling: `resolveCompletedAt` (`packages/shared/src/workout/complete-workout.ts`), which lives there because it also reconciles against the session's own `startedAt`. |
| Scale weigh-in → `body_metrics` | `lib/scale-ble/apply-reading.ts` → `applyScaleReadingToBodyMetrics(repo, userId, {measuredAt, tz, weightKg, composition})`. **The only** path from a confirmed scale reading to `body_metrics`, shared by `/api/scale-ble/samples` and `/api/scale-ble/pending/[id]/confirm` (they held duplicate copies and both drifted — Q-25). Keys the row on the reading's **own local day**, never `todayInTz`; enforces first-reading-of-day-wins for the trend value. Composition math stays in `lib/scale-ble/composition.ts`, and **whether to compute it at all** is `resolveCompositionInputs(user, ageYears)` there — height, age and sex are inputs to a measurement, so a missing one returns null and the caller stores weight only (PS-33: the routes filled them with 170 cm / 35 y / female and stored the result under source `scale_ble` as a real reading). |

## 14. Version, changelog & ops scripts

| Concern | Where |
|---|---|
| App version | `package.json` (bump on user-visible change) |
| Changelog | `packages/shared/src/changelog.ts` (add an entry on user-visible change) |
| Update-available check | `packages/shared/src/version-check.ts` → `isUpdateAvailable` + `resolveUpdateState` (3 states — **`unknown` is not `current`**); `GET /api/version` returns `version` **and** `nativeVersion`; card on Profile. Compare the installed APK against **`nativeVersion`** (the newest published APK), never `version` — the APK is a WebView on Railway, so nearly every release lands without a reinstall |
| Which commit this deploy is | `lib/build-sha.ts` → `readBuildSha()`. `RAILWAY_GIT_COMMIT_SHA` for a GitHub-sourced deploy, else the `BUILD_SHA` file the release workflow writes into a `railway up` upload. Read by `/api/version`, `/sw.js` and `next.config.ts`; never read the env var directly |
| Newest published APK | `lib/github-release.ts` → `fetchLatestApkRelease()` (I/O, 5 s timeout, fails to null) + `parseNativeReleaseVersion`/`mapApkRelease` (pure). One lookup of the rolling `apk-latest` release, shared by `/api/version` and `/api/download-apk`. Needs `GITHUB_RELEASES_TOKEN` while the repo is private |
| **TrainingAi Dev** (second APK, #2367) | `android/app/build.gradle` — `prod`/`dev` product flavours. `dev` is `com.trainingai.app.dev` on `http://localhost:3000` (over `adb reverse`), with no ring/strap/scale component, Bluetooth or Health Connect (`src/dev/AndroidManifest.xml`, `BuildConfig.DEVICE_BLE_ENABLED` in `MainActivity`). `verify<Variant>Flavour` fails the build on drift. Published to the rolling `apk-dev-latest` release, never `apk-latest`. Runbook: [`canonical-runtime-android.md`](canonical-runtime-android.md#trainingai-dev--unreleased-code-on-the-phone-2367) |
| Liveness / status | `GET /api/status` (no-auth), `GET /api/version` |
| **Did the deploy actually land?** | `scripts/check-deploy-landed.js`, run by `.github/workflows/deploy-check.yml` on `push: [main]` (OR-168). Polls `/api/version` for **`webBuildSha`** — `process.env.RAILWAY_GIT_COMMIT_SHA`, the only field there that identifies a WEB deploy: `version` is `CHANGELOG[0].version` and moves only when a PR bumps the changelog (most merges do not), and `nativeBuildSha` is the APK's. **Notify only, never act** (owner, 2026-09-25): an automatic revert across a migration can leave production worse than the bad deploy. **Bust the cache on every read** — that route is `public, max-age=300`, the one written exemption in `check-api-no-store.js`, so an unbusted poll answers from five minutes ago; do not remove the header to fix this. Compares by **prefix in either direction** with a 7-character floor, because `app/sw.js/route.ts` truncates the same value to 12 for its cache name — which also means **the deployed sha is readable without this route at all**, from `/sw.js`'s `ta-<12>`, the trick used to measure the lag. **Measured 2026-09-25: 205s from merge to production**; deadline 1200s, ~6x, because a false alarm teaches everyone to ignore the alarm. Deliberately NOT a required check — it runs after the merge, so gating on it is circular, and it is a separate workflow because `ci.yml` has no `push: [main]` trigger by design (~11 billed minutes per merge) |
| **Driving the real APK over USB** | `scripts/device/` — `pw.js` (Playwright `connectOverCDP` driver the probes use: hit-tested `tap`, `recordNetwork`, `watchAfter`, `offline`, `metrics`, read-only `localQuery`), `sweep.js` (P4), `census.js` (P2/P7/P10), `perf.js` (P11–P16: cold start, time-to-content, waterfall chain depth, long tasks), `selftest.js` (no-phone check), `cdp.js` (`connect()`, `systemBack()`, and a `session` with `evaluate`/`screenshot`/`tap`/`record`), `probe.js` (the read-only first run), `record.js` (compositor frames over time, each named by its ms offset — the `motion-polish` checks are timing questions a still frame cannot answer; **read the timestamps, never the frame count**), `tour.js` (walks a set of screens, capturing a screenshot **and** an in-page DOM digest each — route, active tab, error text, horizontal scroll, and the lowest action row's padding against the measured inset; committed to a throwaway `device-captures/` branch so a session that is not on that machine can review the running app), `README.md` (the runbook). **⛔ Never read the route from an inspector's address bar** — it updates on `Page.frameNavigated`, which `history.replaceState` does not fire, so it goes stale on this app and stays stale; that artifact nearly got a shipped fix (LA-109) recorded as failing on device. The ONE way to exercise what `playwright.config.ts` says its own harness cannot: *"`getLocalStore` returns null… evidence about the web path only"*. Reaches native SQLite, the real `env(safe-area-inset-*)`, the Samsung compositor's own pixels, and — via `adb shell input keyevent 4` — the Android system back that arrives over a Capacitor channel and so is invisible to Playwright. Raw CDP over `adb forward`, no dependency (Node 22's global `WebSocket`); **try `chromium.connectOverCDP` against the same port before writing a second bespoke check**, since that would run the existing `e2e/**` specs against the device. Needs the debug APK (`MainActivity` gates the socket on the manifest's debuggable flag) and gesture navigation on, or every inset reads 0. `TRAININGAI_APP_ID=com.trainingai.app.dev` drives TrainingAi Dev instead, with no fallback to the real app. **⚠ Never run against a device — OR-127's `Keep:` is that first run.** |
| Ops scripts | `scripts/` — `reset-password.js`, `db-backup.sh`, `check-reconcile.js`, `check-oura-models-dormancy.js` (fails CI on a vendored model file nothing reaches — **file**-level reachability only, see its header), `upload-model-assets.js` (pushes the 8 `.onnx` files to the bucket; `--check` verifies without writing), `local-db/` (setup/migrate/seed) |
| Colmi R09 connector (**learning mode**) | `lib/colmi-ble/ble.ts` — pair (`namePrefix: 'R09_'`, never a stored device id), connect, run the sync commands, decode, POST. In-WebView `@capacitor-community/bluetooth-le`, **no native plugin and no APK**. `paired-ring.ts` holds the pairing. `framesToPayload` is exported and pure so the decode→ingest mapping is testable without BLE. Returns `reason: 'silent'` when zero frames arrive — a sleeping ring must never be recorded as "no data". `lib/colmi-ble/resolve-time.ts` turns the decoders' RELATIVE time into instants in the **user's** zone, built from wall-clock strings rather than `midnight + minutes` (that is wrong across a DST transition, and Brisbane would never show it). Storage: `lib/data/postgres/slices/colmi.ts`, migrations 231/232. Routes: `POST /api/colmi/samples`, `GET /api/colmi/status`. UI: `components/settings/colmi-pairing.tsx` on More → Devices |
| Colmi R09 ring wire protocol (**learning mode**) | `lib/colmi-ble/protocol.ts` — 16-byte V1 framing, **mod-256** checksum (NOT 255; two working implementations disagree with the Python clients' prose), CRC16-Modbus + 6-byte header for the V2 "big data" channel, BCD date parts, and every command builder. `lib/colmi-ble/decode.ts` — battery / real-time HR / activity / HRV / stress / HR-log / sleep / skin-temperature / SpO2 decoders. **Pure: no I/O, no clock.** Decoders return RELATIVE time (`daysAgo`, `minuteOfDay`, BCD parts) and never construct a Date — the reference client resolves these in the *device's* zone, which is the banned pattern; the caller anchors them in the user's. Decoders are infallible (unknown frame → `{kind:'unknown'}`, never a throw), held by a 300-case fuzz test. **This module may not reach any scoring input** — `scripts/check-learning-mode-isolation.js` fails CI if it names a scoring table, calls a shared writer, or is imported outside the comparison adapters. Plan: [`2026-08-26-alternative-ring-colmi-testing.md`](superpowers/plans/2026-08-26-alternative-ring-colmi-testing.md) (PS-8) |
| CI rules that encode a CLAUDE.md bug class | The `Custom Rules` job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — simple classes are inline greps, list-bearing ones are scripts: `check-push-mutations.js`, `check-reconcile.js`, `check-doc-links.js`, `check-oura-models-dormancy.js`, plus (2026-08-08) `check-migration-numbers.js` (duplicate migration numbers — also prints the next free number), `check-timezone-rendering.js` (`toLocale*String` with no `timeZone`), `check-date-param-regex.js` (dash-only date schemas) `check-component-size.js` (`.tsx` over 800 lines) and `check-color-mix-hue.js` (`color-mix(in oklch, …, <achromatic>)`, which interpolates the HUE ANGLE toward 0 — a green tint renders salmon; use `in oklab`. Mixing with `transparent` is fine and not flagged), plus (2026-08-10) `check-api-no-store.js` (`max-age`/`stale-while-revalidate` on an `app/api` route — Q-166) and `check-test-user-ids.js` (two DB-touching test files sharing a `TEST_USER_ID` — they delete each other's fixtures in parallel workers; mocked files exempt — Q-177). **The last three carry shrink-only grandfather lists** — each fails if a listed file is fixed but left in the list, so the inventory cannot rot into a stale allowlist. Adding a rule here is how a recurring bug class stops recurring; prefer it over another CLAUDE.md paragraph. Plus (2026-08-15) `check-cache-ttl-divergence.js` — one canonical TTL per cache key (Q-242): it compares TTL *expressions* rather than resolved values across `cachedFetch`/`cachedFetchToday`/`setCached` **and the sync-provider warm list**, and prints how many keys it could not resolve statically, so a clean run is not read as full coverage; and `check-hex-literals.js` — a shrink-only per-file baseline for hex colour literals under `app/`+`components/` `.tsx` (Q-244), the theme-token rule's ratchet: any file not listed must have zero, a listed file may only shrink, and a row for a file that reaches zero must be deleted. Plus (2026-08-19) `check-fetch-once-effects.js` — a shrink-only per-file baseline for `useEffect(() => { … cachedFetch … }, [])` (Q-359), which never re-runs and so holds its first payload forever in the persistent tab shell; its baseline is grouped by whether the site can actually bite, and its own mutation check found that the scan behind the original count missed single-line effects. Plus (2026-08-18) `check-tz-aware-cache-guards.js` — `isBodyMetadataFresh`/`isWorkoutDataToday` called with no timezone argument (Q-478), and `check-memo-prop-stability.js` — an inline object/array/arrow in the props of a `memo(...)` component, shrink-only per-file baseline (Q-490); the second is the render-discipline rule's ratchet, and it found four defeated call sites that the audit which filed Q-490 had reported did not exist. Plus (2026-08-25) `check-dead-repo-methods.js` — a `WorkoutRepository` method whose only references are its own declaration and implementation (LA-26), the shape behind Q-301/Q-270/Q-231 that `tsc` cannot see because an unused export is not an error; deliberately narrower than "no caller outside `lib/data/`", which returns 21 mostly-legitimate internal helpers against this rule's 6. Plus (2026-08-26) `check-learning-mode-isolation.js` — a device in **learning mode** (ingested, stored, compared, read by nothing that produces a score) may not reach the five scoring tables `oura_heartrate`/`body_metrics`/`sleep_sessions`/`oura_daily`/`oura_daily_derived`, may not be imported outside its own directory except by the comparison adapters, and may not appear in `HEALTH_SOURCES` — its **absence** from that tuple is what makes a shared-table write a compile error, since every such writer takes `source: HealthSource`. Empty baseline. It exists because the ranked per-field merge protects *writes* and every scoring *read* is source-blind: `getHrForWindow` has no source predicate and `preferStrapBuckets` is an allowlist of one (`chest_strap`), so a row in a shared table is a scored row however it is stamped. Landed with the Colmi R09 plan (PS-8), before the integration, so promotion out of learning mode has to delete a line from the script and show up in a diff. Plus (2026-08-31) `check-save-preference-in-effect.js` — `savePreference`/`savePreferences` called from inside a `useEffect` (LB-28), which is a network PATCH on every mount wearing the syntax of a free `localStorage` write; the failure it produces names unrelated specs, because it strands requests in a launch burst. Its scanner is a separate module (`scripts/lib/save-preference-in-effect.js`) with fixtures, and it blanks comments and string literals before counting parens so an unbalanced paren in either cannot extend an effect's span across the file. Two exempt sites, each with its reason in the script: `usePersistedPreference` itself, and Home's section-order reconciliation. Plus (2026-09-07) **`scripts/check-route-test-coverage.js`** — an API route with no test importing its handler (PS-39), shrink-only at **131 of 222**. The number matters: the entry said 93, counting a route covered when any test merely mentioned its URL, so a cache-invalidation test 'covered' a route it never called. It RESOLVES the specifier (relative against the importing file, alias against the root) and ignores type-only imports — the substring rule was wrong in both directions by almost the same amount, 13 false negatives against 12 false positives, so its 140 was accidentally near-right for two wrong reasons, and a NEW route arrives uncovered and fails — which is the half that earns its keep, since the 131 are debt. Plus (2026-09-07) **`scripts/lib/strip-comments.js`** — the ONE comment stripper (LA-64). A rule that matches its own explanatory comment gives a **false negative**: it reports clean over code it never parsed, which is how `check-icon-button-names.js`'s companion scan passed against a fully reverted fix. Eight checks had grown their own copy; the six identical ones shared a regex pair that fired inside string literals, so `const s = "a // b"; banned()` hid the call after the string — the same defect class the strippers exist to prevent. The shared one is a scanner: strings are copied through (a banned identifier in a raw `sql` template is a real hit), newlines and byte lengths are preserved so reported line numbers stay right, and a regex literal containing a quote is the one known limit. `check-aest-midnight-timezone.js` keeps its own `stripCommentsAndStrings`, which blanks strings too — a different contract. The inline greps filter `^[^:]+:[0-9]+:\s*(//|\*|\{?/\*)` instead, JSX comments included. **Do not fix this class by banning the pattern in comments** — the explanation is what stops the next person reintroducing the thing. Plus (2026-09-07) **`scripts/check-node-version-agreement.js`** (LA-60) — every workflow `node-version` major must match `package.json` `engines.node`. CI pinned Node 20 while the sandbox and production both ran 22, so a local suite could pass on an API the job lacked (`fs.globSync` did exactly that). Which way to converge was settled by MEASUREMENT: three Node-internal frames in production `error_events` stacks match Node 22.22.2 sources line-for-line, so CI was the outlier. A version agreement rather than a banned-API list, because a list of Node-22 APIs goes stale when Node 23 ships. Plus (2026-09-07) **`scripts/__tests__/check-comment-blindness.test.ts`** (LA-72) — which checks need the stripper is now MEASURED, not judged: each case runs the check clean, with its banned construct as real code (the positive control), then with the same construct in a comment. Without the control a fixture in a file the check does not scan reads exactly like a check that strips, which produced two false passes while it was written. It converted 11 checks and found the payoff: **`check-hex-literals` held 8 baseline rows for files whose only "hex literal" was a PR number in a comment** (`(#919)`, `#185`), so each carried an allowance for a colour it does not contain and a real one could have been added under it. Plus (2026-09-12) **`scripts/check-workflow-job-timeouts.js`** (LA-91) — a workflow job with no `timeout-minutes`, which inherits GitHub's 360-minute default, so one hung step holds a runner for six hours while the PR reads `mergeable_state: unstable` and looks merely slow. Presence only, never a value: the right limit is sized from measured runs of that job (the eight current ones are in the LA-91 journal entry) and a script asserting a number would go stale as the suite grows — E2E moved 24:36 to 27:43 in three days. Empty baseline: LA-91 gave all eight jobs a limit, so a job without one is a regression, and the guard exists because a NEW job arriving without one is silent. Plus (2026-09-27) **`scripts/check-keyboard-viewport.js`** (RV-210) — the two source conditions a soft keyboard needs, held at zero TOGETHER because either alone is a half-fix: `interactiveWidget: "resizes-content"` on the viewport export, and no `[NNvh]` height under `components/`+`app/` (`dvh`/`svh`/`lvh` are fine). Android defaults to `resizes-visual`, which draws the keyboard OVER a full-height page so neither `dvh` nor `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` moves — which is why a `dvh` sweep without the viewport line buys nothing. Empty baseline: the sweep took all 23 `vh` sites, and it exists because **22 other sheets were already on `dvh`** and nothing recorded which unit was right, so each new sheet was a coin toss. **It cannot check behaviour** — a headless Chromium has no soft keyboard, so `interactive-widget` is inert and `dvh` resolves exactly as `vh` there; the device pass is RV-205's P26. Plus (2026-09-26) **`scripts/check-test-comment-strippers.js`** (LB-160) — the TESTS' half of the stripper rule above, which they never got: **88 `.test.ts` files had grown their own regex pair**, and it deleted 25–56% of the four source files any of them actually read, because the `/` `*` inside `accept="image/*"` opens a comment for it. The damage direction is the silent one — a `.not.toMatch` over source that was never there. Converting all 88 exposed no wrong assertion, so the value here is the ratchet rather than a bug haul; empty baseline. It matches the five comment REGEXES rather than the surrounding helper, because those 88 spelled the helper a dozen ways. Two traps its error message names: `require` is an eslint error under `components/**`, and a `@ts-expect-error` on the ESM import is unused and fails `check-test-typecheck`. One real interaction: the shared stripper BLANKS comments to spaces to preserve line numbers, where the hand-rolled pair deleted them — so any assertion measuring a byte WINDOW between two source features was calibrated against the broken one and needs its whitespace collapsed (`sleep-provisional-surfaces.test.ts`) |
| Object storage (S3-compatible, Railway) | `lib/exercise-storage.ts` — one client, one credential set, used by exercise gifs **and** the ONNX model delivery. `downloadMedia(key)` for reads (returns null on any failure — right for a read with a fallback); `statMedia(key)`/`listMediaKeys(prefix)` for **diagnostics**, which surface non-404 errors instead of swallowing them, because "absent" and "auth rejected" must not collapse into one answer |
| Third-party error alerting (Q-404) | `@sentry/nextjs`, initialised in `sentry.server.config.ts` / `sentry.edge.config.ts` / `instrumentation-client.ts`, all three importing `scrubEvent` from **`lib/observability/sentry-scrub.ts`** — the one place deciding what does not leave for a third party. **Additive to `error_events`, never a replacement:** that table is the queryable record in our own database; Sentry exists for the thing it structurally cannot do, which is *notify*. Bodies, cookies and `Authorization` are dropped outright; paths are scrubbed by shape (uuid → `:id`, `YYYY-MM-DD` **and** `YYYY/MM/DD` → `:date`) rather than by a route allowlist, which would go stale on the next route. No replay, no profiling, `tracesSampleRate: 0`. A no-op until `SENTRY_DSN` / `NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN` are set. |
| Are the ONNX models in the bucket? | `lib/oura-models/bucket-report.ts` → `reportModelBucketAssets()` (I/O) + `buildBucketReport(...)` (pure, 3 verdicts: `complete`/`incomplete`/`unreachable`), served by admin-only `GET /api/admin/model-assets` alongside the on-disk `verifyModelAssets`. **The Q-49 Phase A1 gate** — a `complete` verdict is what permits deleting the repo-tree `.onnx` copies and making the boot check fatal |
| How fast is navigation on the device? | `lib/perf/nav-timing.ts` (pure: normalise/summarise) + `lib/perf/nav-timing-recorder.ts` (rAF watcher on `location.href`, always-on via `components/perf/nav-timing-probe.tsx` in the root layout). Read it in the **Device data capture** console (`/admin/data-capture`). Records `urlMs` / `paintMs` / `settleMs` per tap plus `rscCount` — **`rscCount === 0` means the route was prefetched**, which is the direct read-out on whether a `router.prefetch` is working. Do not hook `usePathname` for this: `/workout` → `/workout?session=…` changes only the query and would be missed |
| Server errors that escape a route | `onRequestError` in `instrumentation.ts` → `lib/observability/request-error.ts` → `error_events`. Covers the 80 route files with **no** `catch`. **Does NOT see a route that catches its own error and returns a 500** — those need `reportServerError` (`lib/observability.ts`) in the catch block. Deduped 60 s per route+message — on the **base** message, before the pg-code prefix is added, so a cause that varies between identical failures can't defeat the window; the DB volume is the binding constraint. Talks to the `pg` pool directly, never the repository — the adapter pulls onnxruntime-node, which webpack can't bundle from instrumentation |
| Lifting the real Postgres error out of a Drizzle wrapper | `lib/observability/pg-cause.ts` → `summariseCause()`. **Imports nothing**, deliberately: `request-error.ts` runs from an instrumentation entry point and so can never import `lib/observability.ts` (adapter → onnxruntime-node). Both error paths call it, so there is one implementation rather than a copy on each side. Puts the pg `code` in a message **prefix**, because the standing session-start query groups by `left(message,120)` and a `Failed query:` message runs past that — a suffix is invisible in exactly the read that matters |
| Touch-target size on a rendered page | `e2e/touch-target-size.spec.ts` (Q-282) — the accessibility check no linter can make. Measures DOM geometry against **this repo's 48 dp bar**, not WCAG's 24 px, and covers the roles `app/globals.css`'s `button, [role="button"]` floor does not (`<a>` is excluded there on purpose, plus `role="tab"`/`"radio"`/`"switch"`). Honours the two documented opt-outs (`.tap-target-44`, `.tap-target-dot`) and allowlists by label, shrink-only. **Do not replace it with `@axe-core/playwright`** — that was installed, measured and removed: WCAG 2.5.8 exempts a *spaced* small control, so axe passed a deliberately-shrunk 12×12 button, and `color-contrast` cannot parse the `oklch` tokens (it evaluated no nodes on Home) |
| Contrast of text under 12 px that is dimmed (issue 2429) | `scripts/check-small-text-contrast.js` — blends each `text-[Npx]` (N under 12) element's colour and `opacity-NN` over card/background/muted at the worst brand hue; sites under 4.5:1 sit in a shrink-only per-file `BASELINE`, a new one fails (`--report` prints the table). Colour maths is `scripts/lib/contrast-math.js`, shared with `check-contrast.js`. Findings and the proposed `--muted-foreground-subtle` token: `docs/small-text-contrast-audit.md` |
| Running the actual app in a test (E2E) | `playwright.config.ts` + `e2e/` (Q-249) — `pnpm e2e`, its own `E2E` CI job, **not** a required check yet. `e2e/auth.setup.ts` signs in through the real form once and saves `storageState`; `e2e/fixtures.ts` holds `visitTwice` / `settleRouteBoundary` / `expectNoSkeleton`. **Read `e2e/README.md` before trusting a green run** — it drives the **web** build (`getLocalStore` is null, so the device branch never runs), uses `pnpm dev` because the pg pool forces SSL under `NODE_ENV=production`, and counts skeletons **in the viewport only**, so a tabbed screen like Health is one panel covered. `vitest.config.ts` excludes `e2e/**`; without that, vitest collects the specs and reports "1 failed file, 0 failed tests". **A `page.route` stub must not hand the app a literal date** — `check-e2e-stub-dates.js` (LA-107) fails Custom Rules on one, because the app compares it against `todayInTz` and a literal goes red on the next rollover and never recovers; exemptions are per (file, date) with a stated reason |
| Runbooks | `docs/runbooks/` |

## 15. API route index (grouped)

Full list under `app/api/`. Groups:

- **Workout / logging:** `workout-data`, `log-exercise`, `complete-workout`, `workout-entry`, `workout-sessions` (+`/day`, `/rpe`), `exercise-history`, `next-session`, `log-rest-day`, `confirm-early-deload`, `program-week`, `phase-sets` (+`/clone`), `progression-styles`, `workout-templates`
- **AI:** `ai-chat` (+`/tts`), `builder-chat`, `generate-program`, `ai-periodization/*` (`prescribe`, `respond`, `transition`, `program-overview`, `weekly-volume`, `baseline/complete`)
- **Oura BLE rollup readiness (Q-91-followup):** `lib/oura-ble/rollup-wait.ts` → `waitForRollup({read, sleep, baseline})` — the ONE place that decides "has the server-side rollup actually derived anything yet". Everything external is injected, so it is testable off-device. Backed by `GET /api/oura-ble/rollup-state` (`oura_rollup_state`'s `last_rolled_ds`/`epoch`). **Both the background listener (`sync-provider.tsx`) and manual pull-to-sync (`lib/oura-ble/sync.ts`) go through it** — do not add a third path that guesses at a delay instead: `ingestStored` advances 3 s BEFORE the rollup's debounce even fires, so any fixed wait shorter than the rollup's duration caches a pre-rollup read.
- **Oura BLE server watermark in wall-clock (#2579):** `GET /api/oura-ble/rollup-watermark` → `{ rolledThroughMs }` — `repo.getSleepCoverageEnd(userId)`, i.e. `oura_rollup_state` resolved through the clock offsets; null with no completed rollup or a watermark from an earlier epoch. Session-scoped, takes **no** parameters (any query param is a 400, so no `userId` can be passed), 30/min, `private, no-store`. Its one caller is `maintainOuraRawStore` (`lib/oura-ble/raw-store-maintenance.ts`), which marks device raw rows folded on the server from it.
- **Zero-argument mock guard (LB-62):** `scripts/check-zero-arg-mock-indexed.js` — a `vi.fn` declared with no parameters whose `.mock.calls[n][m]` is then read is TS2493, and the spec PASSES because vitest records arguments regardless of the signature. Shrink-only baseline (39 sites, 13 files). It finds nothing `check-test-typecheck` misses — it finds it in Custom Rules (~25 s, no install) instead of the Build job. Do not "fix" a hit by widening the baseline; type the parameters the assertion reads.
- **Health / metrics:** `readiness-score`, `readiness-verdict`, `training-load`, `muscle-recovery`, `strength-trend`, `health-trends`, `health/trends`, `muscle-tonnage-trend`, `weekly-muscle-sets`, `weekly-stats`, `weights-summary`, `body-battery`, `body-metadata`, `progress-summary`, `sleep-sessions`, `sleep/manual-bedtime` (Q-519 — writes `manual_sleep_start`, which ONLY `user/bedtime-estimate` reads; never `sleep_start`), `sleep-performance-correlation`, `streak-data`, `day-timeline`, `day-review/week-window` (Q-112c — the prior-7-day series for the four day-review trend stats: resting HR, steps, session volume, weight; eight ascending points plus a seven-day mean that EXCLUDES the anchor day), `weekly-review/month-window` (LB-64 — the same four metrics at WEEKLY buckets: five ascending weeks ending at the judged week, plus `priorAverages` over the four completed weeks before it. Daily metrics are **meaned** per week, session volume is **summed**; `weekStart` snaps any day to its Monday and defaults to the last COMPLETED week), `calendar-data`, `year-review`
- **Nutrition:** `nutrition/*` (`food-items`, `food-logs`, `saved-meals`, `meal-types`, `scan`, `barcode`, `recent-for-meal`, `weekly-summary`, `adherence`, `targets`), `nutrition-goals/*`, `day-checkin`, `day-log`, `mood`, `water-log`, `supplements/*`
- **Oura / Health Connect:** `oura/*`, `health-connect/ingest`
- **Activity:** `activity-logs` (+`/[id]/metrics`), `activity-types`
- **Sync:** `sync/pull`, `sync/push`, `sync/sleep-ids`, `sync-health`
- **User / auth:** `auth/*`, `user/*` (`profile`, `avatar`, `password`, `goals`, `bedtime-estimate`, `equipped-title`), `profile/[userId]`, `friends/*`, `seasons`, `achievements`, `push/*`
- **Admin:** `admin/*` (`users`, `errors`, `feedback`, `exercises`, `invites`, `time-audit`, `program-export` [read-only active-program dump — role + sets×reps@% + rest + est-vs-budget time; `lib/admin/program-export.ts` formatter, `components/admin/program-export-card.tsx` copy UI], `generate-exercise-media`, …)
- **Platform:** `status`, `version`, `client-error`, `download-apk`, `feedback`
